  retry records `retry_not_scheduled` with reason `successor_cap` or `meta_session_parked`; the quota ladder records
  `outcome: returned` with the same reason. A new run into a parked meta-session is refused at admission with
  `INVALID_REQUEST` and writes nothing.
- Stall evaluation (`findStalledRuns`) is read-only; it never cancels, signals or transitions the run. The envelope and
  the digest read the park events by default; `get_run`/`list_runs` still pass only `now`, so *T* and the cap on that
  path are the Assumption defaults until `src/mcp/server.ts` passes
  `loopBreakerAttention(…, config.controlPlane.loopBreaker)`.

#### 3.4.7 Pause and budget persistence (Proposed)

```sql
CREATE TABLE control_plane_state (      -- migration T14.6; one row per project_id or '*' for global
  project_id    TEXT PRIMARY KEY,       -- configured or dynamic project id, or '*' for global pause
  paused        INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (paused IN (0,1)),
  paused_by     TEXT,                   -- mcp:<client_id> or os user for CLI
  paused_at     TEXT,
  budget_flags_json TEXT CHECK (budget_flags_json IS NULL OR json_valid(budget_flags_json))
) STRICT;
```

`meta_session` gains `parked_loop INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0` (T14.5). `approval.decided_by` accepts
`mcp_proxy:<client_id>` (T14.8, ADR-0047); no separate `decided_via` column.

**As built (T14.6, Delegated, not owner-approved).** Migration **0018** (`src/ledger/migrations/0018_control_plane_state.ts`)
creates `control_plane_state` exactly as above and the append-only operator signal table §3.4.4 lets T14.6 choose:

```sql
CREATE TABLE control_plane_event (     -- migration 0018 (T14.6); append-only (BEFORE UPDATE/DELETE guards as run_event)
  id            TEXT PRIMARY KEY CHECK (id LIKE 'cpe\_%' ESCAPE '\'),   -- cpe_ + ULID
  ts            TEXT NOT NULL,
  type          TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (type IN (
                  'operator_pause_requested','operator_resume_requested','operator_pause_applied')),
  scope         TEXT NOT NULL,         -- '*' today; a project id is allowed for a later operator per-project command
  payload_json  TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (json_valid(payload_json))  -- { requested_by, reason } or { request_event_id, paused, applied_by }
) STRICT;
```

T14.5's `parked_loop` stays an event-derived flag (§3.4.6); no column was added. T14.4 reads budgets on read without
`budget_flags_json`; the column is created, nullable, and unused. The M15 schedule and CI-trigger tables (§18.5, §18.6)
take the next free migration number after 0018.

#### 3.4.8 Approval proxy tools (FR-CTL-08, ADR-0047)

Offered in `tools/list` only when `mcp_clients[].approval_proxy` is true (default false). Set only in operator
configuration; never over MCP. Optional `mcp_clients[].approval_proxy_targets`: string array of client ids (default: the
client itself).

**`list_pending_approvals`**

```jsonc
{ "input": { "type": "object", "properties": {
    "project_id": { "type": "string" } } },
  "output": { "type": "object", "required": ["approvals"], "properties": {
    "approvals": { "type": "array", "items": { "$ref": "#/$defs/approval_proxy_item" } } } } }
```

Lists `PENDING` approvals for listed target clients only. `approval_digest` = SHA-256 over a canonical JSON object:
`{ "v": 1, "approval_id", "kind", "scope_hash" | "revision_hash", "target_client_id", "requested_at" }` (privileged
uses §3.1 `scope_hash`; dynamic project uses ADR-0030 revision hash). Descriptions are controller-generated fields only
(ADR-0047 item 2d).

**`decide_approval`**

```jsonc
{ "input": { "type": "object", "required": ["approval_id","approval_digest","decision"], "properties": {
    "approval_id": { "type": "string" },
    "approval_digest": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^[0-9a-f]{64}$" },
    "decision": { "enum": ["approve","deny"] } } },
  "output": { "type": "object", "required": ["approval_id","status"], "properties": {
    "approval_id": { "type": "string" },
    "status": { "enum": ["APPROVED","DENIED"] } } } }
```

Fail closed: wrong digest, stale id, target client not in `approval_proxy_targets`, or row not `PENDING` →
`INVALID_REQUEST`, nothing written. On success, same transitions as CLI `approve`/`deny` (§3.1), with `decided_by =
"mcp_proxy:<proxy_client_id>"` and a `approval_decided` `run_event`. Each success triggers sink delivery
(§3.4.9). Clients without `approval_proxy` do not see these tools (absent from `tools/list`, not error at list time).

*Amends §3.1 **Approve**:* except for `approval_proxy` clients using §3.4.8, no MCP tool approves.

**Implementation (T14.8, `src/control/approval-proxy.ts`, wired in `src/mcp/server.ts` and `src/cli/serve.ts`;
Delegated, not owner-approved):**

- Configuration: `approval_proxy` is a boolean (default false); `approval_proxy_targets` is a non-empty list of unique
  configured client ids and is refused at load without `approval_proxy: true` or when it names an unknown client.
  Without it a proxy targets only itself. `tool_profile` is not consulted.
- Kinds: `privileged_scope` lists a `PENDING` row whose run is AWAITING_APPROVAL and passes every §3.1 hash check
  (`approval_id` is the `apv_` row id). A dynamic project revision lists while it is the latest, `PENDING_APPROVAL` and
  undecided (`approval_id` is the `prv_` revision row id); it is `push_revision` when its approval reasons include
  `push_policy`, `commit_identity` or `commit_signing`, else `dynamic_project`. `requested_at` is the row's
  `requested_at` or the revision's `created_at`; `target_client_id` is the run's client or the revision's creator.
- `revision_hash` (ADR-0030 stores none): hex SHA-256 over `canonicalJson({ v: 1, project_id, revision, client_id,
  definition_json, approval_reasons_json, created_at })` of the revision row.
- `scope_summary` uses closed vocabularies and counts only (for example "shell allowlist of 1 command(s)"); it never
  repeats an argv, host, path, objective, project name or other request or agent text.
- Every failed check (unknown or non-pending id, not a listed target, digest missing, malformed or different) returns
  the same `INVALID_REQUEST` message and writes nothing; the digest is compared in constant time inside the decision's
  `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction. A client without the flag gets the unknown-tool answer.
- Recording: a privileged decision appends `operator_decision_requested` (`requested_by = "mcp_proxy:<client_id>"`) and
  applies it through the lifecycle's `applyApprovalDecision` in the same transaction, so `decided_by`,
  `approval_decided` and the T3/T19 transition are those of the CLI path. A revision decision goes through the project
  store's `decide` with `decided_by` (and the `project_event` `operator`) `mcp_proxy:<client_id>`. A proxied approve of
  a scope with `bypass: true` is an `APPROVED` row like any other for the §3.1 **Bypass** check, which still requires
  every hash to match (ADR-0047 Decision 1 and 2g; ADR-0017 item 5's "operator CLI" wording is not edited here).
- Sinks (§3.4.9, read-only reuse): a privileged approve is announced by the existing transition hook (T3,
  `approval_decided` / `privileged_scope`); a privileged deny and every revision decision are announced with
  `approval_decided` and the `approval_kind` as reason code (`run_id` null for a revision) through the same dispatcher.

#### 3.4.9 Notification sinks (FR-CTL-10, ADR-0048)

**Configuration** (`notification_sinks[]`, off when absent):

| Field | Type | Meaning |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `id` | string | Operator-chosen label |
| `kind` | `webhook` \| `ntfy` | Delivery mechanism |
| `url` | string (https) | Webhook URL (operator may point this at the Grok bot's deployment-generated webhook; no Grok-specific code) |
| `authorization` | string (optional) | Webhook authorization header value (FR-CTL-12, T14.14); sent as `Authorization: <authorization>`, never logged or echoed |
| `ntfy` | object | For `kind: ntfy`: `{ "server", "topic", "token"?: string }`; publish uses ntfy's documented HTTP publish API ([ntfy publish](https://docs.ntfy.sh/publish/), cited here per owner decision 53) |
| `events` | array of `sink_event_kind` | Filter; default all kinds |
| `max_per_minute` | integer ≥ 1 | Rate limit; excess dropped and counted |

**Payload** is exactly `notification_payload` (§3.4.1). **Link** is the configured MCP HTTP resource URL itself (a
route this server serves; there is no per-run or digest page, and the consent-page token never reaches a sink payload),
else a `switchboard://run/<run_id>` or `switchboard://digest` placeholder for stdio-only deployments. Delivery is
asynchronous, bounded timeout (proposed target: 5 s), HTTPS with verification; failures append `sink_delivery_failed`
counts to the ledger without payload or URL (NFR-SEC-01). Never blocks admission or run execution.

**Implementation (T14.9, T14.14, `src/control/sinks.ts`, `src/config/notification-sinks.ts`; Delegated, not owner-approved):**

- `url` and `ntfy.server` must be `https://` with no userinfo, query or fragment; plain HTTP is refused at load, also
  for loopback. Optional `authorization` on `kind: webhook` sinks is sent as the `Authorization` header and stored like
  the `ntfy` `token` (FR-CTL-12, T14.14); `doctor` shows `set` or `unset` and the value is never logged, written to the
  ledger or returned by MCP tools. `ntfy.topic` matches `^[-_A-Za-z0-9]{1,64}$`. At most 8 sinks with unique `id`s
  (**Assumption**). `max_per_minute` defaults to 10 and is at most 600 (**Assumption**; a sliding 60 s window per sink).
  Config errors name the key and never echo a configured value.
- Reason codes per `event_kind`: `needs_attention` takes a `needs_attention_reason`; `approval_pending` and
  `approval_decided` an `approval_kind`; `run_terminal` the terminal state (`SUCCEEDED`, `FAILED`, `CANCELLED`);
  `budget_exhausted` the literal `budget_exhausted`; `project_paused` `project_paused` or `global_paused`;
  `queue_low` the literal `queue_low` (T15.4, FR-QUE-04; `run_id` null); `digest` the literal `digest` (T14.16,
  FR-CTL-14; `project_id` `*`, `run_id` null, and the integer-only `counts` object of §20.5, which no other kind may
  carry). `project_id`
  is a project id, `-` (a run admitted without a project) or `*` (all projects). An event with any other value, or a
  `run_id` that is not `run_` + ULID, is dropped, not sent.
- Link: the configured `public_url` resource itself when the HTTP transport is set (`sinkLink` returns it unchanged
  after validating it is a URL); else `switchboard://run/<run_id>` or `switchboard://digest`. No per-run or digest
  route exists, so the link never encodes one, and the consent-page token never reaches a sink payload.
- Webhook: `POST` to `url`, `Content-Type: application/json`, body exactly `notification_payload`, with
  `Authorization: <authorization>` when `authorization` is configured (FR-CTL-12, T14.14). ntfy: JSON publish
  ([ntfy publish](https://docs.ntfy.sh/publish/), "Publish as JSON") to the server root with body `{ topic, title:
  "Switchboard: <event_kind>", message: "project_id=<id> reason_code=<code> run_id=<id|none>", click: <link>, tags:
  ["switchboard"] }` and `Authorization: Bearer <token>` when `token` is set. For a `digest` event the message
  carries the counts tail of §20.5 after the base fields.
- Delivery starts on a later event-loop turn than the transition, uses no connection reuse, follows no redirect and
  times out after 5 s. Outcome codes: `timeout`, `transport_error`, `http_status` (with the status) and
  `rate_limited`. A failure or drop for an event with a run appends a `sink_delivery_failed` run event `{ sink_id,
  event_kind, reason_code[, status] }` and logs the same fields; an error's own text is never used.
- Producers (all T14.9, through the same dispatcher, filters and rate limits; every emission is one closed event off
  the run path, never polled and with no new run state):
  - `approval_pending` / `privileged_scope` with `needs_attention` / `awaiting_approval`: the lifecycle admits a
    privileged run (FRD T2), via `RunLifecycleOptions.onApprovalPending` (never on an idempotent replay or a
    client-policy approval, which never waits).
  - `approval_pending` / `dynamic_project` or `push_revision` (the approval proxy's kind rule, §3.4.8): a
    `create_project` / `update_project` write that leaves a revision waiting for the operator, via
    `announcePendingRevision` (one revision row announces once).
  - `approval_decided` / `privileged_scope`: the existing transition hook (FRD T3); a privileged deny and every
    revision decision via `announceApproval` (T14.8, unchanged).
  - `needs_attention` / `parked_loop` and `failed_after_retry`: the lifecycle's terminal path (the loop-breaker park,
    a failure at the successor cap or a parked failure, the T14.2 derivation's rule), via `onNeedsAttention`, once
    per (run, reason) in this process.
  - `needs_attention` / `skill_cleanup_failed`: a failed native-skill removal pass on the run path
    (`before_verifying`, terminal entry), via `onNeedsAttention`, once per run in this process. The startup-reconcile
    and retention-gc passes record the same ledger events (so the envelope and digest still flag the run) but do not
    emit (the dispatcher does not exist yet at startup reconcile).
  - `needs_attention` / `ci_fix_stuck`: the CI poller setting the stuck flag (the 0→1 transition only), via `onStuck`.
  - `budget_exhausted` (T14.4, unchanged): the budget refusal hook already announces it, so it is not announced again
    as `needs_attention`.
  - `stalled` and `secret_scan_blocked` have no event-time hook (both derived on read only: `findStalledRuns`
    never acts, and a push refusal is a tool result read back from the meta-session's `git_push` events, §3.4.5;
    T9.14) and are never emitted; `run_terminal`, `project_paused` / `global_paused` and `queue_low` are unchanged.

#### 3.4.10 `tool_profile` (FR-CTL-11)

`mcp_clients[].tool_profile`: `orchestrator` (default) or `console`. `tools/list` returns:

| Profile | Tools |
| --- | --- |
| `orchestrator` | Today's full set (unchanged) |
| `console` | `get_digest`, `list_runs`, `get_run`, `delegate_task`, `steer_run`, `pause_project`, `resume_project` |

Approval-proxy tools are added to the list only when `approval_proxy` is true, regardless of profile. `console` is the
first target for the Grok bot (owner decision 55); orchestrator profile stays for primary coding agents.

As built (T14.11, Delegated): the filter lives in `src/mcp/tool-profile.ts` and applies to `tools/call` as well as
`tools/list`; a console client's call to any other tool fails `INVALID_REQUEST` exactly like an unknown tool (a
project-admin call is still recorded, FR-PRJ-10). A console name with no registered handler yet is absent rather than
invented. Config load refuses an unknown `tool_profile` value and refuses `console` together with `manage_projects:
true`, which would otherwise be a silent no-op.

As built (unknown-tool `details.tools`, 2026-10-20, Delegated, not owner-approved): both `tools/call`
unknown-tool refusals report `details.tools` as exactly the names `tools/list` returns for that client, derived from
the same descriptors (`offeredDescriptors` in `src/mcp/server.ts`), so the list can never lag the registered set
(work-queue, skill and approval-proxy tools included when offered). Pinned by `src/mcp/unknown-tool.test.ts`
(`details.tools` equals the sorted `tools/list` names for an orchestrator and a console client).

#### 3.4.11 Consent-page approval link (FR-CTL-09, ADR-0047)

A secondary way to decide one pending approval (ADR-0047 Decision 3), after the CLI (§3.1) and the approval proxy
(§3.4.8): a single-use, expiring link to the M13 consent page (§3.3). Available only when the HTTP transport is
configured. Research input: [consent-page approval link spike](../research/consent-page-approval-link-spike-2026-10-15.md).

**Implementation (T14.10, `src/control/consent-link.ts`, `src/control/consent-link-page.ts`, routed in
`src/mcp/http.ts`, wired in `src/cli/serve.ts`; Delegated, not owner-approved):**

- Link: `<issuer origin>/approval/<handle>`, where the handle is `sbal_` plus 32 random bytes in base64url. Nothing
  else: no query, no OAuth token, no authorization code, no `approval_digest`, no passphrase, no pairing code, no
  approval or run id and no scope text. The handle is not a credential; it only selects which approval an
  authenticated operator is shown.
- Handles are held in memory by the one `serve` process that owns the listener, as SHA-256 hashes (as the T13.8
  authorization codes); no ledger table is added and a restart drops every link (fail closed). Lifetime 30 minutes
  (**Assumption**), at most 256 live links (**Assumption**).
- Issuance: on each operator poll `serve` issues a link for every decidable approval that has no live one and logs
  `approval_id`, the URL and its expiry. Decidable means listed by §3.4.8's rules for a proxy that targets every
  configured client (read-only reuse), so the kinds, `scope_summary` and `approval_digest` are those of §3.4.8. A
  pending approval whose link expired gets a new handle; the old one stays dead.
- A link dies at its first decision (whatever the outcome), at expiry, and as soon as its approval is decided another
  way, stops being decidable or changes: the digest stored at issue is compared with a fresh one on every use.
- Page flow (FR-MCP-17; no login session, Q-76): `GET` shows one fixed sign-in form, identical for a live, spent,
  expired, unknown or malformed handle, with no approval field. A `POST` with the operator passphrase or a one-time
  pairing code is required before the link is looked up; a dead link then gets a fixed 410 page, a live one the closed
  §3.4.8 item fields (without the digest) and Approve / Deny. The decision `POST` spends the link.
- CSRF and headers as §3.3's consent page: a single-use server-side visit id and CSRF token per step, the token also in
  a `__Host-sb_approval` cookie (`Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite=Strict`), no foreign `Origin` and no cross-site
  `Sec-Fetch-Site`; each visit is bound to its handle's hash, so a form for one link cannot decide another. The
  transport admits a same-origin `Origin` under `/approval/` only when the page is mounted.
- Attempt limits: wrong passphrases count against the same lockout as the OAuth consent page (one `AttemptLimiter`
  shared by `serve`); wrong pairing codes are throttled as there.
- Recording: as §3.4.8, with `requested_by` / `decided_by` `consent_page`; the digest is re-checked inside the
  decision's `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction and any mismatch writes nothing. Sinks (§3.4.9) are told as for the proxy: a
  privileged approve by the transition hook, a privileged deny and every revision decision as `approval_decided`.
- Audit: page events carry event names, reason codes, methods and (after sign-in only) approval ids; never a handle,
  URL, passphrase or pairing code.

## 4. Failure taxonomy

`failure_code` on a `FAILED` run holds exactly one of these codes. `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` and `INVALID_REQUEST` were **added
during planning** (not in [R2]'s list). Retryability follows FR-CAP-05: only `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT`,
`PROVIDER_OVERLOADED` and `PROCESS_CRASH` before any workspace mutation retry automatically; rollback-or-resume instead
of replay follows FR-WS-04.

| Code | Meaning | Retryable? | Workspace action | Typical sources |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `AUTH_REQUIRED` | Provider not logged in; operator must log in natively | No | None (no mutation expected); report login command | `authPreflight`; headless auth error (FR-AUTH-02) |
| `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` | Operation denied by profile/permission rules, or caller above its max profile | No | Roll back if mutated | Provider permission-denied event; MCP caller check (FR-MCP-06, FR-POL-05) |
| `SANDBOX_VIOLATION` | Sandbox blocked an action | No | Roll back if mutated; flag for operator review | Provider sandbox event; outer sandbox denial (FR-POL-05) |
| `MODEL_UNAVAILABLE` | Requested model not present or not entitled | No (re-route may succeed) | None | Provider error category; model registry miss |
| `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` | Subscription limit hit | No automatic retry; fail over, queue until known reset, or return (FR-CAP-06) | Roll back if mutated | Provider-reported limit; billing error category |
| `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT` | Short-term throttle | Yes, if workspace unchanged | None if clean; else rollback or resume | Rate-limit error event; 429-like category |
| `PROVIDER_OVERLOADED` | Provider-side overload/server error | Yes, if workspace unchanged | As above | `overloaded`/server error categories |
| `TOOL_FAILURE` | Agent tool or command failed in a way the run cannot continue | No | Keep result if checkpointed, else roll back | Provider tool error; non-zero exit with error event |
| `TEST_FAILURE` | Verification commands failed after the agent finished | No | Keep post-run checkpoint (work product), mark failed verification | Verification stage (FR-EXE-08, M3) |
| `PROCESS_TIMEOUT` | Wall-clock or idle timeout; process group killed | No | Assess mutation; roll back or keep as unverified | Supervisor timers (FR-EXE-01) |
| `PROCESS_CRASH` | Provider process died abnormally, or controller restart orphaned it (cause `controller_restart`, FR-EXE-06) | Yes only if no mutation since pre-run checkpoint | Mutation check before any action | Signal/exit; reconciliation |
| `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR` | Unparseable, oversize-line or unexpected-protocol output | No | Assess mutation; roll back if changed | Parser; line limit (§8) |
| `ADAPTER_VERSION_UNVERIFIED` | Installed version outside the validated range, or unknown or unparseable (a missing manifest is `ELIGIBILITY_NONE`, [FRD BR-3](../requirements/FRD.md#3-business-rules)) | No | None | Probe; manifest check (FR-ADP-03, FR-POL-07) |
| `CANCELLED` | Operator or caller cancelled | No | Roll back to pre-run checkpoint unless operator keeps partial work (Proposed) | `cancel_run`, WF-4 |
| `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` *(added during planning)* | No provider passes hard eligibility for this request | No (state may change) | None | Router (FR-RTE-01, FR-MCP-05) |
| `INVALID_REQUEST` *(added during planning)* | Schema, path, size or idempotency validation failed | No | None | MCP validation (FR-MCP-09, FR-POL-10) |

`CANCELLED` is also a run *state* (FRD); the code is used in error envelopes and `run_event` payloads, while a cancelled
run has `state = 'CANCELLED'` and `failure_code` NULL per the DDL check.

A handoff bundle that cannot be built (M5) is reported as `TOOL_FAILURE` with `details.reason =
"HANDOFF_BUNDLE_UNAVAILABLE"` before any run exists ([§15.7](#157-continue_task-with-handoff),
[ADR-0038](../adr/0038-handoff-bundle-and-continuity.md)); no code is added for it.

Inner-agent failures (unreachable, timeout, invalid output, open circuit, suspension) are **not** run failure codes: they
never fail a run, never appear in `run.failure_code` and never feed retry (FR-INN-06, FR-INN-08); they are recorded as
`inner_call` outcomes and `inner_*` run events ([§13.8](#138-telemetry-and-events)).

## 5. Workspace and lease algorithm

Implements FR-WS-01..06 and [ADR-0006](../adr/0006-worktree-checkpoints-no-blind-replay.md). All Git commands use
`execFile('git', args)` with a scrubbed environment, `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null` and `--no-verify` on commits
(Proposed, see [TDD §3.4](TDD.md#34-security-engineering)); porcelain/`-z` output only.

**Naming.** Worktree path `<data_dir>/worktrees/<sess>/<run>`; branch `switchboard/<sess>/<run>`. One controller branch
per run, created from the previous head, so history per meta-session is a linear chain of run branches (Proposed).
`inspect-untrusted` on a non-Git directory uses no worktree and read-only access (FR-WS-06).
*M5 (Proposed, Delegated, [ADR-0038](../adr/0038-handoff-bundle-and-continuity.md)):* a native resume whose adapter
does not declare `resumeCwd: 'independent'` runs in the worktree path of the run that created the native session, with
its own new branch at the head checkpoint ([§15.6](#156-working-directory-of-a-native-resume)); paths stay under
`<data_dir>/worktrees/<sess>/` and are never shared across meta-sessions (FR-WS-08).

**Lease (one writer per meta-session chain, Proposed).** Because each run branches from the session head, two concurrent
writers would fork; the lease lives on `meta_session` (`lease_run_id`, `lease_expires_at`). Parallel tasks use separate
meta-sessions and therefore separate worktrees (FR-WS-02). Concurrent runs of one project each get their own worktree
and lease whichever meta-session they belong to (FR-WS-08, owner decision 42, Delegated). Parallel subtasks inside one
session: to be detailed in M5.

*As built (T9.11, 2026-10-13, Delegated).* FR-WS-08 needed no change to the lease or worktree model: a run's worktree
and branch are keyed by meta-session and run (`<data_dir>/worktrees/<sess>/<run>`, `switchboard/<sess>/<run>`), the
lease is one column pair per `meta_session` row, and a meta-session has at most one non-terminal run (FR-SES-06), so
two concurrent runs of one project are always in different meta-sessions with their own worktree, branch and lease;
nothing is keyed by project or context. The one rule that holds per worktree (FR-WS-02, at most one write lease) is
unchanged. One defect was found and fixed: another run's ref check could see a checkpoint commit before its `post` row
(see [§12.5](#125-ref-snapshot-and-violation-handling), "Concurrent runs"); finalization is now serialised per
repository root. Tests: `src/lifecycle/concurrent-worktrees.test.ts`. Not built: the `container` clause of FR-WS-08
(M10, FR-PRJ-05) and FR-WS-09 (Proposed, not accepted; Q-55 answered 2026-10-18: no merge detection, abandon-only
removal; the amendment follow-up amends and accepts it; the abandon-removal trigger is pinned by two running tests
and only the merge test stays a skipped placeholder).

*As built (worktree creation, 2026-10-13, Delegated).* Git takes no lock on `.git/worktrees/` during `git worktree add`,
so concurrent preparations on one repository failed intermittently: an add that enumerated worktrees read a sibling's
half-created admin directory (`fatal: failed to read .git/worktrees/<id>/commondir: Success`), or a sibling's
`git worktree prune` removed it (`fatal: could not open '.git/worktrees/<id>/locked' for writing`). The partial-worktree
repair, the directory creation and `git worktree add` now run one at a time per repository root in the process
(`src/workspace/manager.ts`, `serialisedWorktreeCreate`); no retry. Test: "creates worktrees for concurrent runs on one
repository one at a time (T9.11)" in `src/workspace/workspace.test.ts`.

```mermaid
sequenceDiagram
  participant S as Scheduler
  participant L as Ledger txn
  participant W as Workspace Manager
  participant X as Supervisor
  S->>L: BEGIN IMMEDIATE; check lease free or expired; set lease_run_id, expires_at; run -> LEASED; event
  L-->>S: COMMIT
  S->>W: resolve base (session head); write 'pre' checkpoint row (intent)
  W->>W: git worktree add -b branch path base; record pre checkpoint
  S->>X: spawn (run -> RUNNING); heartbeat extends lease_expires_at
  X-->>S: exit + events
  S->>W: detect mutation, commit post checkpoint, write diff artifact
  S->>L: run -> FINALIZING/terminal; release lease; head_commit = post; event
```

**Acquire.** In one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction: if `lease_run_id IS NULL` or `lease_expires_at < now`, set both and
transition the run to `LEASED` with an event; otherwise the run stays queued. Lease TTL default 2 x wall-clock timeout,
extended by a heartbeat while the supervisor holds the process (proposed target; values in §8). An expired lease whose
holder run is non-terminal is resolved by reconciliation, not by simply stealing it: the stale run is first marked
`PROCESS_CRASH` (cause `lease_expired`) and its worktree assessed.

**Base and pre-run checkpoint.** Base = `meta_session.head_commit`, or for a new session the repository `HEAD` of the
allowed workspace (read via `git rev-parse`; the operator's checked-out branch and working tree are never modified,
FR-WS-05). A `base` row is written to `workspace_checkpoint`, then `git worktree add -b <branch> <path> <commit>`. The
`pre` checkpoint equals the base commit of the worktree; no commit is made at this point because the worktree starts
clean. Dependency seeds (FR-WS-10, Proposed) are populated after this point and before spawn, into Git-ignored
directories only, so they never change a checkpoint ([§5.1](#51-dependency-seeds-fr-ws-10-proposed-t912)).

**Post-run checkpoint (FR-WS-03).** After the process exits and verification (if any) ends: `git add -A` in the worktree
(hooks disabled), `git commit` with a controller identity and the run ID in the message, record `post` checkpoint row,
write `diff` (`git diff --binary <pre>..<post>`) and `changed_files` artifacts. Commit is skipped if the tree is clean.

**Mutation detection.** Before any retry, rollback or resume decision, compare worktree to the `pre` commit: `git status
--porcelain=v2 -z --untracked-files=all` plus `git diff --quiet <pre>` and `HEAD == pre`. Any difference, a changed
`HEAD` or an unreadable worktree counts as *mutated* (fail closed, NFR-SEC-06). Known limits: ignored files are not
tracked by default (Proposed: also check with `--ignored` for the profile's high-risk patterns) and writes outside the
worktree are out of Git's sight; the sandbox, not detection, bounds those.

**Failure handling (FR-WS-04).** Unchanged worktree: eligible for retry per §9. Mutated: either `git reset --hard <pre>
&& git clean -fd` in the worktree (rollback; the failed attempt's diff is saved as an artifact first), or native resume
of the provider session on the same worktree, which counts as a continuation and never replays the original request.
Choice is policy-driven; default rollback for `TOOL_FAILURE`/`OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR`, resume only when the adapter
reports a resumable session (Proposed).

*As built in M1 (FRD §4.4 owns the outcome, so it wins over the destructive reset above):* the run lifecycle commits the
failed attempt's state to the run's own branch as an evidence checkpoint, stores the diff artifact, and moves the
meta-session head back to the pre-run checkpoint; it does not run `git reset --hard` / `git clean` on the evidence.
`WorkspaceManager.rollback` exists in `src/workspace/` but the lifecycle follows FRD §4.4. The reset-based wording above
is superseded for the lifecycle path.

**Push and operator tree.** The workspace manager never runs `git push`, touches no remote and writes nothing to the
operator's working tree or checked-out branch; the result is the run branch plus diff artifact (FR-WS-05). Publication
is a separate, policy-gated path owned by COMP-GIT ([§12](#12-injected-git-tools-commits-and-publication)). Checkpoint
commits here carry the controller identity and the run ID in their message, which is acceptable only because they are
internal: they are never pushed and never part of the publish ref (FR-GIT-08). *As built in M1* the checkpoints are
made on the run branch `switchboard/<sess>/<run>` with the identity in `src/workspace/git.ts`; M9 must not change
that, only add the publish ref beside it.

**GC (FR-WS-07, NFR-DATA-01, M3; the as-built paragraph after this one wins where they differ).**
Retention job deletes, per expired terminal run, in one
logical unit: worktree directory (`git worktree remove --force`, then `git worktree prune`), controller branch,
artifact files (set `deleted_at`). Never touches branches outside `switchboard/`. *Proposed (FR-WS-09, owner decision
42):* a concurrent run's worktree may also be removed on merge or abandon before retention; the branch and
artifacts stay until GC. *Split 2026-10-18 (Delegated, ruling 1 under decision 62):* the abandon trigger is
`ARCHIVED` by retention GC only (no earlier removal step exists and none is added), so the worktree, branch and
artifacts are removed together by that GC unit; no merge detection. From M9 the publish refs (§12.3) are
collected with the rest of their meta-session. Orphan sweep: worktree directories or `switchboard/*` branches with no
matching `run` row are reported by `doctor` and removed only by GC after the retention period.

*As built (T3.12, Delegated decision 28 under owner decision 25):* the unit is a whole idle meta-session
([Q-18](../ROADMAP.md#73-open-questions-for-zemdregon-not-blocking-m2-unless-noted), `src/workspace/gc.ts`; `run_event`
rows are kept, Q-19). It is wired three ways, none with a new config key. `doctor` reports what `retention.days`
(default 30) would collect and the orphan sweep, read-only (`findOrphans`, `planRetentionGc`). `switchboard gc` lists
the same by default (dry run); `--apply` collects, needs `retention.days` set in the config and the data-directory
lock (so it is refused while `serve` runs). `serve` runs one pass after reconciliation, before the lifecycle starts or
MCP accepts calls, and then every `retention.interval`, only when `retention.days` is set: unset means nothing is
collected. Orphans are removed by the same collecting passes (`gc --apply`, `serve`), never by `doctor` or the dry run
(`collectOrphans`, [ADR-0054](../adr/0054-orphan-gc-mark-first-ledger-table.md)): the first pass that sees one records
it in `gc_orphan` (ledger migration 19) and removes nothing; a later pass removes it once that mark is older than
`retention.days`, in the session GC order (intent transaction that re-checks the ledger, removal, outcome), worktree
directories first, then `git worktree prune`, then `switchboard/*` branches. Removal is refused through a symlinked
`worktrees` or session directory; a symlinked orphan is unlinked, not followed. The sweep does not count a publish ref
(`refs/heads/<prefix><sess>`, §12.3) of a meta-session that has a ledger row as an orphan; one whose session is
unknown is still reported. GC marks first (R-42, decision 28): one transaction re-checks idleness, sets the
meta-session `ARCHIVED` and appends the intent event, and admission refuses an `ARCHIVED` meta-session with
`INVALID_REQUEST`; a marked, unfinished session is a candidate again and rolls forward.
*As built (T3.12 interval-timer test, #707, Delegated):* the interval pass runs through `scheduleRetentionGc` in
`src/cli/serve.ts` (`RetentionGcTimers` seam, delay capped at `MAX_TIMER_MS`, never runs at scheduling time, a
throwing or rejecting pass is logged first-line-only and never stops later passes, `stop` on shutdown; wired into
`runDaemon` with no behaviour change), covered by `src/cli/serve-retention-timer.test.ts` ("serve retention GC
interval timer (T3.12)"). *Not built (no claim):* a process-level test waiting for a real interval pass (the
shortest configurable `retention.interval` is `1m`); a process-level `SIGKILL` mid-GC test (in-process crash
injection after every step, for sessions and orphans, covers the order).

*Free-disk floor (T3.23, FR-WS-11, BR-51; Delegated under owner decision 25, not owner-approved).* `limits.min_free_disk_mb`
is off when absent: no probe, no new event, `limits` resolved as before. When set, the disk-probe port
(`src/workspace/disk.ts`: `DiskProbe.freeBytes(path)`, synchronous; production `statfsDiskProbe` reads `bavail × bsize`)
is asked by `pump()` once per dispatch pass while runs are queued, for the data directory and its `worktrees` root
(`assessDiskFloor`: the lowest readable value counts; a path the probe cannot read is skipped, and when none can be read
the floor is not assessable and nothing is held). Under the floor every `QUEUED` run stays `QUEUED` and gets one
`disk_hold` run event per hold episode (`reason: FREE_DISK_BELOW_FLOOR`, `free_bytes`, `min_free_bytes`, `path`);
admission (`submit`, `delegate_task`) is unchanged and no run is ever failed or refused by the floor. The wake timer
re-probes every `DEFAULT_DISK_RECHECK_MS` (30 s, Assumption), and the early sweep (`runEarlySweep`, `src/workspace/gc.ts`)
starts at most once per period and once at a time; its end re-probes at once. The sweep is an **ordered reclaimer
list** (`earlySweepReclaimers`): the dependency-seed reclaimer for idle worktrees (FR-WS-10) comes first and is
registered by T9.13 (`seeds`; the list has none until then), then `meta_sessions`, which is `runRetentionGc` with the
`stopWhen` seam bound to the floor (oldest idle meta-session first, stop once free space is back above the floor) and
runs only when `retention.days` is set, the one switch for every collection. Every reclaimer receives
`ReclaimContext.leasedWorktrees` (worktree paths of the runs holding `meta_session.lease_run_id`) and must never touch
one; the retention reclaimer cannot, because its candidate query and `beginGc` both require no lease. `serve` runs the
sweep behind the same in-flight slot as the retention pass (never two collecting passes at once). `doctor` adds a
`Free disk` check only when the key is set: `[ok]` with the free space and the floor, `[warn]` under it (naming the
hold and whether the sweep collects), `[warn]` when no path can be probed; never `[FAIL]`.

**Crash safety.** Order is intent row (with the `ARCHIVED` mark, one transaction), then Git action, then outcome
row. On startup reconciliation (WF-8) a run in
`LEASED`/`RUNNING` with a worktree but no outcome is assessed by the mutation check; a lease row is cleared only after
its run reaches a terminal state. `git worktree add` interrupted mid-way is detected by `git worktree list` disagreement
and repaired by remove-and-recreate before the pre-state is used.

### 5.1 Dependency seeds (FR-WS-10, Proposed, T9.12)

*Designed by T9.12 and built by T9.13 (2026-10-17, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved; every choice below stays
Proposed).* Decision and trade-offs: [ADR-0057](../adr/0057-dependency-seed-store.md). **As built (T9.13):** the store is
`src/workspace/seeds.ts` (`createSeedStore`, `seedWorktree`, `seedReclaimer`); the workspace manager seeds a fresh
worktree in `prepare` right after the `pre` checkpoint (`WorkspaceManagerOptions.seeding`), so the lifecycle is
unchanged; `serve` composes the store only when a configured project declares a seed (`serveSeeds`), reconciles it at
startup and registers `dependency_seeds` first in the early disk sweep; the `container` environment reads a run's mounts
from the store (`ContainerEnvironmentOptions.seeds`, `seedMountPlan`) and `containerSeedCommand`
(`src/environment/seed-build.ts`) builds a container project's seed in its image. Ledger migration 0022 adds the two
tables below and the `seed_log` artifact kind. Differences from the design, all Delegated: (1) a dynamic project
definition (FR-PRJ-10) cannot declare `dependency_seeds` (the key exists only in the configuration file's project
schema), so no `projects approve` path is needed yet; (2) `dependency_seed_use` also records `directory`, so the early
sweep removes a private copy without the configuration; (3) the use row is written when the entry is resolved, before
populate, so a post-build eviction can never take the entry a run is about to copy; (4) a directory tracked at the base
checkpoint counts as `not_ignored`; (5) stale `.build/`, `.tmp/` and unknown entry directories are removed by the
store's startup `reconcile`, not by the T3.12 orphan pass; (6) the manager passes no cancel signal, so a cancel during
seeding takes effect when `prepare` returns (the seed's own time bound still applies); (7) `projects validate` does not
yet check the lockfile and the ignore rule (not built). The copy-on-write attempt was checked on ext4 on the
operator host: `COPYFILE_FICLONE_FORCE` fails with `ENOTSUP` and the seed falls back to a private copy, as
designed. The empty mount point a container run leaves in the host worktree is treated as absent on a later seed; that
Docker creates it is an Assumption not run on rootless Docker by T9.13 (tests use a scripted runtime). It sits
between the pre-run checkpoint and spawn above: the worktree is created from the base checkpoint (FR-WS-01,
[ADR-0006](../adr/0006-worktree-checkpoints-no-blind-replay.md)), each declared seed is populated, and only then is the
provider process prepared and started. The seeded directory is Git-ignored, so the `pre` checkpoint, mutation detection,
the post-run checkpoint (`git add -A`) and the diff artifact are the same as for an unseeded worktree.

**Configuration (project level, [§11.1](#111-configuration-shape)).** Absent means off. Nothing in a request, a
continuation or an agent's output can add, change or select a seed.

```jsonc
{ "projects": [ { "id": "web-app", /* ... */
    "dependency_seeds": [ {                             // at most 8 per project (Assumption)
        "id": "npm",                                    // ^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,40}$, unique in the project
        "lockfile": "package-lock.json",                // relative to the primary context
        "directory": "node_modules",                    // relative; the ignored directory the install produces
        "install": ["npm", "ci", "--ignore-scripts"],   // argv, no shell; same shape rules as verification_commands
        "timeout_seconds": 600                          // optional; 1 to 3600, default 600 (Assumption)
    } ] } ],
  "limits": { "seed_store_mb": 10240,                   // optional; total store bound, default 10240 (Assumption)
              "seed_entries_per_seed": 3 } }            // optional; entries kept per (project, seed), default 3 (Assumption)
```

`loadConfig` refuses (strict schema plus structural rules, JSON path in the error, never the value): an absolute path,
a `..` component, an empty path, a path under `.git`, a `directory` equal to or containing the `lockfile`, two seeds of
one project whose directories are equal or nested. `projects validate` additionally checks, at `base_ref`, that the
lockfile exists and that `git check-ignore` reports the directory as ignored, and warns (never fails) otherwise. The
`limits` keys apply only when a project declares a seed. A dynamic project (FR-PRJ-10) may declare seeds; a revision
that adds or changes `dependency_seeds` needs `projects approve` like a `container` or push-enabled revision, because
it runs a command on the host.

**Key.** `seed_key = sha256(canonical JSON of {v: 1, project_id, seed_id, lockfile_sha256, install_argv, profile,
environment})`, where `lockfile_sha256` is the SHA-256 of the lockfile blob at the run's base checkpoint (read with
`git cat-file blob <base>:<lockfile>`, never from the worktree), `profile` is the build profile below, and
`environment` is `{kind: "host", platform, arch}` or `{kind: "container", image_digest}`. Keys are hex; the entry
directory is `<data_dir>/seeds/<seed_key>/` and holds the seeded directory's contents only.

**When a run is seeded.** For each declared seed of the run's primary context, in declaration order, after the `pre`
checkpoint row and before the environment's `prepare`:

1. *Eligibility.* `inspect-untrusted` runs and runs without a worktree are not seeded (no event: verification is
   refused to them too, FRD V7). Build profile = the run's profile, lowered to `trusted-development` when it is
   `privileged` (a seed build is a controller command; it never runs under a scope or bypass).
2. *Preconditions at the base checkpoint.* Lockfile blob missing → `seed_failed` `lockfile_missing`. Directory not
   ignored (`git check-ignore -q` in the run worktree) → `not_ignored`. Directory already present in the worktree
   (a native resume in an existing worktree, §15.6) → leave it, record `seed_applied` with `method: "existing"`.
3. *Resolve.* In one `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction look up `dependency_seed` by key. `ready` → use it. `building` with
   a live builder → wait (poll every 500 ms, Assumption) up to the seed's timeout (`wait_timeout`); the run is still
   `LEASED`, so the wait is outside the provider's wall clock, and a cancel ends it (`cancelled`). None, or `building` with
   a dead builder (pid and start ticks, as §8) → insert or take over the row as `building` with this run as builder,
   and build (below). `evicting` → treat as none after the evictor's outcome row; never use it.
4. *Populate* (below), insert a `dependency_seed_use` row, update `last_used_at`, append `seed_applied`.

Any step that cannot complete appends one `seed_failed` event and moves on to the next seed; the run proceeds. No
failure in this section changes the run's state, failure code or admission result.

**Build.**

- *Scratch worktree.* `git worktree add --detach <data_dir>/seeds/.build/<uuid> <base commit>` with the hardened Git
  of §5 (hooks off, scrubbed environment), serialised with the other worktree creations of that repository root
  (`serialisedWorktreeCreate`). It is never a run's worktree, so nothing an agent wrote can reach an entry.
- *Command.* Spawned exactly as a verification command ([§8.1](#81-verification-stage-m3-t310)): argv, `shell: false`,
  own process group, stdin closed, cwd the scratch worktree; the same environment (allowlist minus `GIT_*`, the signing
  and agent variables, plus `limits.verification_env`); one verification slot and `verification_nice` when slots are
  configured (FR-EXE-09); wrapped in the outer sandbox when `confinedVerification` is configured, failing closed
  without it. Pid, pgid and start ticks are written to the `dependency_seed` row before output is read, so
  reconciliation can kill an orphan. Bound: `timeout_seconds`, with the §8 termination sequence.
- *Container projects.* The command runs in a container of the project's image (`image_digest`, ADR-0052 checks
  included) with the project's network policy and limits, mounting only the scratch worktree read-write at its own
  path; label `switchboard.seed=<seed_key>`; removed afterwards and by the orphan sweep.
- *Output.* stdout and stderr captured as in §8.1 (1 MiB per stream, redacted) into a `seed_log` artifact of the
  triggering run (`application/json`, version 1: `{v, seed_id, seed_key, outcome, exit_code, signal, duration_ms,
  stdout, stderr, ...truncated}`). No argv or output in events.
- *Publish.* On exit 0, the produced directory is checked (exists, is a real directory; every symlink resolves inside
  it, else `unsafe_entry`; no device, FIFO or socket; set-user-ID and set-group-ID bits cleared; total size within
  `seed_store_mb`, else `store_full`), moved with `rename` into `<data_dir>/seeds/.tmp/<uuid>`, made read-only
  (`a-w`), then renamed to `<data_dir>/seeds/<seed_key>/`; the row becomes `ready` with `size_bytes`. Any failure, a
  non-zero exit or a timeout deletes the row (or marks it for the next builder) and appends `seed_failed`
  (`build_failed`, `build_timeout`, `unsafe_entry`, `store_full`, `build_error`). The scratch worktree is removed
  (`git worktree remove --force`, `git worktree prune`) on every path.

**Populate (Q-103 Delegated default: no hardlinks under any profile).**

- *`host`.* Walk the entry without following symlinks and create the seeded directory in the run worktree: regular
  files by copy-on-write clone (`fs.copyFile` with `COPYFILE_FICLONE_FORCE`; Assumption, T9.13 confirms on the CI
  filesystems), falling back for the whole seed to a private copy (`fs.copyFile` without the clone flag) on the first
  `ENOTSUP`, `EXDEV` or `EINVAL`; symlinks recreated with the same relative target; directories created. Copied files
  get write permission back (`u+w`), so the run owns a writable private tree. Never `link` and never a symlink to the
  store. `method` is `clone` or `copy`. A write, `chmod` or
  delete in one worktree therefore changes neither the store nor another worktree.
- *`container`.* No copy. The environment adds one read-only bind mount, source `<data_dir>/seeds/<seed_key>/`, target
  `<worktree>/<directory>` inside the container, after the worktree mount (Docker applies nested mounts in order;
  Assumption, T9.13 confirms on rootless Docker). `method: "mount"`. This is the only path under the data directory a
  container ever sees, and only that entry (FR-PRJ-05; T9.13 adds the dated SRS note). The host-side directory in the
  worktree stays absent, so the host's checkpoints see nothing.

**Records.**

```sql
-- ledger migration: next free number when T9.13 lands; additive (ADR-0005)
CREATE TABLE dependency_seed (
  seed_key        TEXT PRIMARY KEY,              -- hex sha256, see Key
  project_id      TEXT NOT NULL,
  seed_id         TEXT NOT NULL,
  lockfile_sha256 TEXT NOT NULL,
  profile         TEXT NOT NULL,
  environment     TEXT NOT NULL,                 -- canonical JSON
  state           TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (state IN ('building','ready','evicting')),
  builder_run_id  TEXT, builder_pid INTEGER, builder_pgid INTEGER, builder_start_ticks TEXT,
  size_bytes      INTEGER,
  created_at      TEXT NOT NULL, ready_at TEXT, last_used_at TEXT,
  evict_intent_at TEXT
);
CREATE TABLE dependency_seed_use (
  run_id        TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES run(id),
  seed_key      TEXT NOT NULL,                   -- no FK: the entry row is deleted on eviction, the use is history
  worktree_path TEXT NOT NULL,
  method        TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (method IN ('clone','copy','mount','existing')),
  populated_at  TEXT NOT NULL,
  removed_at    TEXT,                            -- private copy removed by the early sweep
  PRIMARY KEY (run_id, seed_key)
);
```

Run events (`run_event`, payloads closed; no paths outside the worktree, no argv, no output): `seed_applied` `{seed_id,
seed_key, method, built: boolean, bytes}` and `seed_failed` `{seed_id, reason}` with `reason` one of `lockfile_missing`,
`not_ignored`, `build_failed`, `build_timeout`, `build_error`, `unsafe_entry`, `store_full`, `populate_failed`,
`wait_timeout`, `cancelled`.

**Eviction (LRU).** An entry is *in use* while any `dependency_seed_use` row names it for a run that is not terminal,
and while it is `building`. After each successful build, and when called by the early sweep, the store evicts `ready`
entries not in use, least recently used first (`last_used_at`, then `created_at`), until each `(project_id, seed_id)`
has at most `seed_entries_per_seed` entries and the store total is within `seed_store_mb`. Order as ADR-0054: one
transaction re-checks "not in use" and sets `state = 'evicting'`, `evict_intent_at`; then the directory is removed
(refused through a symlink, unlinked rather than followed); then the row is deleted. A row left `evicting` rolls
forward at the next pass; a resolve never uses it. Unknown directories under `<data_dir>/seeds/` and stale `.build/`
and `.tmp/` entries are orphans, collected by the T3.12 orphan pass in the same mark-first way. Retention GC of a
meta-session (§5, GC) removes its worktrees, and with them their private copies; it does not touch the store.

**Crash safety and reconciliation (WF-8).** At startup: a `building` row whose builder is dead has its process group
killed if still recorded, its scratch worktree removed and the row deleted (the next run rebuilds); `evicting` rows roll
forward; a run that crashed during populate leaves a partial ignored directory that the next populate of the same
worktree replaces (remove, then populate) rather than trusts.

**Interfaces (for T9.13 and the T3.23 reclaimer).** In `src/workspace/seeds.ts` (Proposed path):

```ts
export interface SeedSpec {
  readonly id: string; readonly lockfile: string; readonly directory: string;
  readonly install: readonly string[]; readonly timeoutMs: number;
}
export type SeedEnvironment =
  | { readonly kind: 'host'; readonly platform: string; readonly arch: string }
  | { readonly kind: 'container'; readonly imageDigest: string };
export type SeedFailureReason =
  | 'lockfile_missing' | 'not_ignored' | 'build_failed' | 'build_timeout' | 'build_error'
  | 'unsafe_entry' | 'store_full' | 'populate_failed' | 'wait_timeout' | 'cancelled';
export type SeedOutcome =
  | { readonly ok: true; readonly seedKey: string; readonly method: 'clone' | 'copy' | 'mount' | 'existing';
      readonly built: boolean; readonly bytes: number;
      /** container only: the read-only bind mount the environment adds after the worktree mount. */
      readonly mount?: { readonly source: string; readonly target: string; readonly readOnly: true } }
  | { readonly ok: false; readonly reason: SeedFailureReason };

export interface SeedStore {
  /** Resolve or build (single flight), then populate the run's worktree. Never throws; never fails the run. */
  seed(run: { readonly runId: string; readonly projectId: string; readonly profile: TrustProfile;
              readonly baseCommit: string; readonly repoRoot: string; readonly worktreePath: string;
              readonly environment: SeedEnvironment; readonly signal: AbortSignal },
       spec: SeedSpec): Promise<SeedOutcome>;
  /** LRU eviction of unused ready entries down to the configured bounds; stops when `ctx.enough()` is true. */
  evict(ctx: ReclaimContext): Promise<ReclaimOutcome>;
  /** Remove the private copies (method clone or copy) in worktrees of idle runs; never a leased worktree. */
  removeIdleCopies(ctx: ReclaimContext): Promise<ReclaimOutcome>;
  /** Startup reconciliation, see above. */
  reconcile(): Promise<void>;
}

/** The early disk-floor sweep's first reclaimer (T3.23, FR-WS-11): `earlySweepReclaimers({ ..., seeds })`. */
export function seedReclaimer(store: SeedStore): DiskReclaimer; // name 'dependency_seeds'
```

`ReclaimContext`, `ReclaimOutcome` and `DiskReclaimer` are the existing types in `src/workspace/gc.ts`. The reclaimer
runs `removeIdleCopies` first (an idle worktree is one whose run is terminal and whose path is not in
`ctx.leasedWorktrees`; removal sets `dependency_seed_use.removed_at` and counts one unit per directory), then `evict`,
checking `ctx.enough()` between units. A container use has no private copy and is skipped. A later native resume in
that worktree is seeded again by step 2. The lifecycle calls `SeedStore.seed` for each spec after the `pre`
checkpoint and passes the container mounts to `Environment.prepare` (§11.3) as an added read-only mount list; a `host`
environment ignores it. With no `dependency_seeds` configured, `serve` builds no store, the lifecycle makes no call,
and worktrees, events and mounts are byte-identical to `main` (the first T9.13 test).

**Tests T9.13 must add** are the T9.13 row's Done-when ([ROADMAP §6.3](../ROADMAP.md#63-m9-projects-and-controlled-commits-tasks));
in addition: a symlink leaving the directory fails the build with `unsafe_entry`; a `privileged` run's build records
profile `trusted-development`; an entry `evicting` is never used; a `container` run's argv carries exactly one extra
`:ro` mount and no other path under the data directory.

## 6. Compliance manifest

One manifest per provider, stored in the repository (versioned data, not code), loaded and Ajv-validated at startup;
hash recorded in `adapter_version.manifest_hash`. Based on the manifest in
[R2](../research/headless-ai-coding-controller-verified-2026-10-12.md#compliance-and-authentication-boundary), extended
with `validated_version_range` (FR-ADP-03). It records provider-policy review status (FR-POL-07); it is not a statement
that any use is contractually compliant (NFR-COMP-01).

```jsonc
{ "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema", "type": "object", "additionalProperties": false,
  "required": ["schema_version","provider","validated_cli_version","validated_version_range","validated_on",
               "auth_modes_allowed","automation_surface","credential_export_allowed",
               "dashboard_scraping_allowed","network_protocol_reverse_engineering",
               "commercial_multi_user_status","source_revision"],
  "properties": {
    "schema_version": { "const": 1 },
    "provider": { "type": "string" },
    "validated_cli_version": { "type": "string" },                    // exact version last validated
    "validated_version_range": { "type": "string" },                  // semver range; Proposed (non-semver CLIs: explicit list, to be verified in M2)
    "validated_on": { "type": "string", "format": "date" },
    "auth_modes_allowed": { "type": "array", "items": { "enum": ["native_cached_subscription"] } },  // other modes: to be added only by manifest review
    "automation_surface": { "type": "array", "items": { "enum": ["documented_headless","documented_acp","documented_service"] } }, // last two Proposed
    "credential_export_allowed": { "const": false },
    "dashboard_scraping_allowed": { "const": false },
    "network_protocol_reverse_engineering": { "const": false },
    "commercial_multi_user_status": { "enum": ["requires-review","reviewed-permitted","reviewed-prohibited"] }, // [R2] shows requires-review; others Proposed
    "source_revision": { "type": "string" },                          // provider terms date or id reviewed
    "notes": { "type": "string" },
    "otlp_metrics": { "type": "object", "properties": {              // T8.15, FR-TEL-06, ADR-0063: optional; absent unless the cited provider docs were checked
      "endpoint_env": { "type": "string" },                         // OTLP/HTTP metrics endpoint variable (set to the run's loopback receiver)
      "protocol_env": { "type": "string" },                         // optional, pinned to http/json when the provider documents it
      "resource_attributes_env": { "type": "string" },               // optional run/task resource-attributes variable (provider-documented)
      "docs": { "type": "string" } },                               // provider-documentation citation; required when declared
      "required": ["endpoint_env","docs"], "additionalProperties": false } } }
```

**Eligibility effect (FR-POL-07, FR-ADP-03).** The adapter is ineligible if: no manifest exists (failure code
`ELIGIBILITY_NONE`, per [FRD BR-3](../requirements/FRD.md#3-business-rules), which owns the code mapping); the installed
binary version is outside `validated_version_range` or cannot be parsed (failure code `ADAPTER_VERSION_UNVERIFIED`);
the manifest fails validation (fail closed); the run's auth mode is not in `auth_modes_allowed`; the provider's
`automation_surface` lacks the transport the invocation needs; or `commercial_multi_user_status` is
`reviewed-prohibited`. In the single-operator deployment
`requires-review` is recorded but does not block (Assumption,
[ADR-0011](../adr/0011-single-operator-local-deployment.md)); multi-user or resale use is out of scope. The three
`const: false` fields mean a manifest that claims otherwise cannot load. A provider whose manifest omits
`otlp_metrics` gets no per-run OTLP listener and no extra environment (T8.15, FR-TEL-06,
[ADR-0063](../adr/0063-per-run-provider-otlp-metrics-receiver.md)); no shipped manifest declares it yet.
`switchboard adapters validate <provider>` (T4.4, FR-OPS-02) updates
`validated_cli_version`, `validated_on`, `validated_version_range` and appends a dated evidence sentence to `notes`, and
nothing else, only after the probe (FR-ADP-02 steps 1 to 5), an `OK` auth preflight and the conformance subset including
the structured-output smoke call (step 7, one provider call) pass; on any failure it writes nothing.
The smoke task is compiled under `inspect-untrusted` first. Only when that is `Ineligible` and the adapter is Grok
Build, Cursor or Kimi Code (owner decision 59), OpenCode or Antigravity (owner decision 60; Delegated readings) does
it run in the adapter's only compiled cell instead: `privileged` with the narrowest bypass scope that adapter compiles,
approved by an in-memory approval local to that call (no ledger row, approval record or config change), with the
throwaway repository passed writable where the cell refuses a read-only workspace (Cursor, Kimi Code, OpenCode,
Antigravity). Before the spawn the argv's bypass-class flags must be exactly the one flag the cell declares
(`--always-approve`, `--force`, `--prompt`, `--dangerously-skip-permissions`; OpenCode declares none, so none may
appear). The step detail, the `--json` `smoke_profile` field and the evidence line name the cell and its ruling; every
other adapter, Claude Code and Codex included, is unchanged. A failed smoke step reports the run's classified failure
code and its classifier detail (redacted with the ledger's secret patterns, e-mails and URL queries stripped, clipped
to one line; never raw stderr or stdout), and the `--json` smoke step adds `failure_code` and `failure_detail`.
The range is the installed `X.Y.Z` to the next minor, `>=X.Y.Z <X.(Y+1).0` (Q-12; an Assumption, not a provider
statement); a pre-release version is refused.

**Manifest file location (Delegated, Assumption).** The command edits the manifest in the source tree, the file a
contributor changes in a pull request, and the operator commits that change. It finds it from its own module location:
the package root must hold `package.json`, a `.git` entry and `src/adapters/manifests/`. The copy that the built package
loads from `dist/` is a build artefact (rebuild after committing), and an installed package has no source tree, so the
command then fails closed and writes nothing. There is no option to point it elsewhere (only tests inject a directory).
The write is a line-level text replacement of those four properties (`notes` is added as the last property if
absent), checked by re-validating the result and comparing every other field, then a rename over the original.

## 7. Telemetry spans

Span structure from [R2] (FR-TEL-02, M8). Run events in `run_event` are the source of truth from M1 (FR-TEL-01); OTLP
export is derived. Redaction is by construction: spans are built from an allowlist of attributes (ADR-0012).

| Span | Parent | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `controller.delegate` | root | one per `delegate_task`/`continue_task` |
| `route.classify` | `controller.delegate` | M7 |
| `rag.retrieve` | `controller.delegate` | M12; one per admission-time retrieval, attributes in [§14.10](#1410-events-and-records); never on a run's critical path beyond `admission_timeout_ms` |
| `provider.acquire_capacity` | `controller.delegate` | queue wait, concurrency semaphore, breaker |
| `workspace.create_checkpoint` | `controller.delegate` | lease + worktree + pre checkpoint |
| `provider.spawn` | `controller.delegate` | process lifetime |
| `agent.tool.read` / `agent.tool.edit` / `agent.tool.shell` | `provider.spawn` | derived from `tool_call` events |
| `provider.model_turn` | `provider.spawn` | derived from usage/message events |
| `workspace.diff` | `controller.delegate` | mutation detection + post checkpoint |
| `verification.tests` | `controller.delegate` | M3 |
| `run.finalize` | `controller.delegate` | terminal transition |
| `inner.call` | `controller.delegate` when the call belongs to a run, else root | M11; one per inner-agent call, attributes in [§13.8](#138-telemetry-and-events); never on a run's critical path beyond the per-call bound. **Not emitted as of 2026-10-16**: the OTLP transport (T8.1) and the export-time filter (T8.4, which already admits the `inner.*` keys) exist, but the inner pipeline builds no span; it writes a body-free log line of this name instead (§13.8 as-built note) |

**Allowed attributes:** `run.id`, `session.id`, `provider`, `adapter.version`, `model`, `trust_profile`, `run.state`,
`failure.code`, `retry.attempt`, `transport`, durations (`duration_ms`), `exit.code`, `exit.signal`, tool kind
(`tool.kind`, one of `read|edit|shell|other`), counts (`changed_files.count`, `events.count`), token/usage numbers as
reported (`usage.input_tokens`, `usage.output_tokens`, `usage.cache_read_tokens`, `usage.cache_write_tokens`),
`route.eligible_count`, `artifact.id`, `artifact.sha256`, `artifact.size_bytes`, and (M11) the `inner.*` attributes
listed in §13.8. The concrete key names for the prose categories (durations, tool kind, counts, usage) were pinned by
T8.4 (2026-10-16, Delegated); this list is the exact exported key set.
**Forbidden (FR-TEL-03, NFR-SEC-01):** prompt or objective text, constraints text, shell command lines and tool
arguments, file contents and diffs, stdout/stderr text, environment variable names or values beyond a count,
credential-like values (including OAuth access or refresh tokens, API keys and webhook or sink URLs), absolute home
paths (use run-relative paths), provider reasoning text (NFR-PRIV-02), MCP request or response bodies, and inner-agent
prompt or response text. Large payloads are artifacts referenced by ID and hash only, never inlined on spans.

**Configuration (FR-TEL-02, FR-TEL-06, NFR-PRIV-01).** Two optional keys, documented in [TDD §3.1](TDD.md#31-configuration):

| Key | Default | Behaviour |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `telemetry.otlp_endpoint` | absent (no export) | When omitted or empty, Switchboard does not load `@opentelemetry/api` or an OTLP exporter at startup, opens no trace-export listener (the T8.15 per-run provider-OTLP receiver is separate: loopback-only, off by default, next row), and makes no outbound OTLP connection (NFR-PRIV-01). When set, it must be an `http` or `https` URL validated at config load (T8.1); traces export to that endpoint only. |
| `telemetry.provider_otlp` | `false` (no receiver) | When anything but `true`, no per-run receiver opens for any run. When `true`, a run whose provider manifest cites `otlp_metrics` gets one loopback listener (FR-TEL-06, T8.15, [ADR-0063](../adr/0063-per-run-provider-otlp-metrics-receiver.md); [§20.13](#2013-tamper-evident-ledger-provider-telemetry-evaluation-brief-and-critic-t813-t815-t814-t514-t1417)). |

**Lazy load and export pipeline (T8.1, ADR-0012).** OTel packages are imported only after a non-empty
`telemetry.otlp_endpoint` is loaded, on first span export, not at module top level, so configs without telemetry match
pre-M8 startup and test runs that omit the key. Span attributes are built only from controller-observed facts (ledger
events, spawn metadata, routing decisions, artifact IDs and hashes), never from provider global config, transcript files
or hook payloads. Immediately before OTLP export, the pipeline drops any attribute whose key is not on the allowlist
(defence in depth; T8.4). Forbidden categories must not be set even on internal builders; CI asserts both drop behaviour
and absence of forbidden keys on exported spans (T8.3, T8.4).

**T8.1 transport (2026-10-16, Delegated).** `src/config/load.ts` validates the optional URL without echoing its value
in errors. `src/telemetry/index.ts` creates an inert, instance-local transport in `serve`; the first completed span
loads the API, trace SDK and OTLP/HTTP exporter. No global provider is registered and no resource detectors run.
The explicit endpoint is passed to the exporter. A bounded batch processor queues at most 512 spans, exports batches
of at most 64 after 100 ms, and bounds each export at 1 second (Delegated best-effort defaults, not measured targets).
Pending initialization accepts at most 512 spans; failures are dropped without logging exporter error bodies or URLs.
Shutdown closes only an initialized transport. Wrapper emission and attribute builders remain T8.2; the export-time
attribute filter is T8.4 (below). Tests: `src/config/config.test.ts`, `src/telemetry/index.test.ts` and
`src/cli/serve.test.ts` (startup with OTel imports rejected).

**T8.2 wrapper emission (2026-10-16, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved).** `src/telemetry/spans.ts` holds the
builders and the per-process `SpanTracker`: `controller.delegate` starts at each non-replayed admission (`submit`)
and completes on the terminal transition (`tryMove`, one funnel for every terminal path) with `run.id`,
`session.id`, `provider`, `trust_profile`, the T7.12 pinned `model` when set, `retry.attempt` for a successor
(attempt > 1), `run.state` and `failure.code` on failure; `provider.spawn` starts in the spawn callback (T9,
`onSpawn`) and completes when the supervised process exits with `adapter.version` (preflight), `model`,
`transport`, `exit.code` and `exit.signal`. Both carry `duration_ms` beside the intrinsic span start/end times.
Builders accept only typed controller-observed facts, so prompt, constraints, command lines, env values and
stdout/stderr text have no path onto a span by construction (FR-TEL-03, ADR-0012); no provider transcript or global
hook config is read. `RunLifecycle` takes an optional `Telemetry` (unset = no emission, fire-and-forget when set);
`serve` passes the T8.1 instance. A run whose process never spawned (spawn error) exports no `provider.spawn` span;
a run admitted before telemetry existed exports nothing. At T8.2 merge the spans exported as roots; parent links and
the forbidden-key collector assertions came with T8.3, and the export-time allowlist drop filter with T8.4 (both
below). These two spans are the only ones emitted today; the other rows of the table above (and `inner.call`) have no
builder yet. Tests:
`src/telemetry/spans.test.ts` (fake-adapter run through the lifecycle into an in-process OTLP collector, a planted
objective canary asserted absent from every attribute and raw OTLP body, and builder/tracker unit tests).

**T8.4 export-time drop filter (2026-10-16, Delegated).** `src/telemetry/allowlist.ts` holds the exact key set above
(`ALLOWED_SPAN_ATTRIBUTE_KEYS`, including the §13.8 `inner.*` keys). `createTelemetry().recordSpan` filters every span's
attributes before it is queued for the transport, so a disallowed key never reaches the OTLP exporter. The match is
exact and case-sensitive (no prefix or wildcard matching). Values must be strings, booleans or finite numbers; arrays,
objects, `null` and non-finite numbers are dropped even on an allowlisted key. Drops are silent (no log line, because a
dropped value may itself be forbidden text), and an attribute object whose read throws is dropped whole (fail closed).
The span itself, its name and its timestamps still export. The filter does not inspect values for forbidden content;
builders remain responsible for never setting forbidden categories (T8.2), and T8.3 scans exported spans. Tests:
`src/telemetry/allowlist.test.ts`, including a check that the key set equals the backticked keys in this section and
§13.8.

**T8.3 parent links (2026-10-16, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved).** Spans reach the transport as completed,
fire-and-forget records, and a child (`provider.spawn`) usually completes before its parent (`controller.delegate`),
so the parent link cannot be read from a live parent span. `src/telemetry/links.ts` therefore encodes the table above
as a static parent-name map (`SPAN_PARENTS`) and derives ids from the run id: the trace id of every span of a run is the
first 16 bytes of a SHA-256 over a fixed label and the `run.id` value, and the span id of a once-per-run span that can
be a parent (`controller.delegate`, `provider.spawn`) is the first 8 bytes of a SHA-256 over another label, the run id
and the span name. A child names its parent's derived id without the parent having been exported; a span that can occur
more than once per run gets a random span id; an all-zero id is replaced with a valid one. The derivation is one-way
and takes nothing beyond the run id (no prompt, path or environment value). Resolution order in
`createTelemetry().recordSpan`: explicit valid hex `traceId`/`spanId` (and optional `parentSpanId`) on the
`CompletedSpan` win; otherwise the (already filtered) `run.id` attribute selects the run's trace; otherwise the span is
an unrelated root with random ids (the shape `inner.call` outside a run will take). A name not in the map is a root of
its run's trace. The transport stages the derived ids into the SDK id generator for the duration of one synchronous
`startSpan` and starts every span from `ROOT_CONTEXT`, so an ambient caller context can never become a parent and no
global OTel provider or propagator is registered. Tests: `src/telemetry/collector.test.ts` (describe `span links`).

**Collector test harness (T8.3, M8 acceptance).** Shape for the integration test, without requiring a provider CLI:

1. Start an in-process OTLP/HTTP receiver on loopback with an ephemeral port (or equivalent test fixture).
2. Load config with `telemetry.otlp_endpoint` set to that URL; run one `delegate_task` through finalize on the fake
   adapter.
3. Read recorded spans from the fixture and assert: expected span names and parent links from the table above; every
   attribute key is on the allowlist; no forbidden category appears (including a planted objective string in the delegate
   request); secret-pattern scans over exported attributes match NFR-SEC-01 expectations for fixtures.

*As built (T8.3, 2026-10-16, Delegated).* The fixture is `src/telemetry/fixtures/collector.ts`: an in-process OTLP/HTTP
receiver on `127.0.0.1` with an ephemeral port that keeps raw request bodies, a span decoder, a forbidden-category scan
over keys and values (`findForbiddenAttributes`) and a tree check against `SPAN_PARENTS` (`parentLinkViolations`).
`src/telemetry/collector-lifecycle.test.ts` runs one delegate/finalize path on the fake adapter through `RunLifecycle`
for a successful and a failed run and asserts exactly two spans, `provider.spawn` under `controller.delegate` in one
trace per run, every key allowlisted, `failure.code` on the delegation span only, and planted objective, constraint,
environment and workspace-path canaries absent from every attribute and every raw OTLP body; a positive control plants
a forbidden category and expects the scan to fail. `src/telemetry/collector.test.ts` adds unit positive controls for
each forbidden key and value pattern. The scan is a test oracle, not an export-time control: at export only the T8.4
key filter applies, and no value is inspected.

**Traceability (T8.0, 2026-10-16, Delegated).** The forbidden list above implements FR-TEL-03 and NFR-SEC-01 for
telemetry. [HLD TB-6](HLD.md#3-trust-boundaries) and [SDD §5](SDD.md#5-requirements-to-design-traceability) rows for
FR-TEL-02, FR-TEL-03, NFR-SEC-01 and NFR-PRIV-01 point here; no separate requirement definitions are added in those
documents.

## 8. Run supervisor details

`COMP-EXE` implements FR-EXE-01..07 for the `subprocess-jsonl` transport (M1); `acp-stdio` is Proposed in
[§8.2](#82-acp-stdio-transport-m4-proposed-t47) (M4, ADR-0036); `local-service` deferred (FR-EXE-04).

- **Spawn.** `spawn(argv[0], argv.slice(1), { cwd, env, detached: true, stdio: ['pipe' or 'ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
  shell: false })`. `detached` puts the child in a new session and process group (`pgid = pid`). Persist `pid`, `pgid`
  and the process start time (`/proc/<pid>/stat` ticks on Linux, `ps` `lstart` on macOS: §8.2) in `run` *before*
  reading any output, so reconciliation can tell a live orphan from a reused PID.
- **Stdin.** Closed (`ignore`) unless the Invocation supplies stdin text; prompts passed on stdin rather than argv where
  an adapter supports it, to keep them out of process listings (to be verified per adapter).
- **Cancel and timeout.** On cancel, wall-clock or idle timeout: send `SIGTERM` to `-pgid`, wait `killGraceMs` (10 s,
  proposed target), then `SIGKILL` to `-pgid`; confirm group empty (`kill(-pgid, 0)` fails with ESRCH) before reporting
  terminal. Defaults: wall-clock 30 min, idle (no stdout bytes) 5 min, preflight probe 10 s
  (`DEFAULT_PROBE_TIMEOUT_MS` in `src/adapters/run.ts`); the wall-clock and idle values are configurable. Timeout yields
  `PROCESS_TIMEOUT`, cancel yields `CANCELLED`.
- **Line framing.** Stdout is decoded as UTF-8 and split on `\n` (tolerating `\r\n`); one JSON document per line for
  JSONL transports; for `json` output, the entire stdout is one document capped at the artifact limit. Max line size 1
  MiB (proposed target): an oversize line aborts the run as `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR` (kill group, keep captured data),
  never truncated silently. Invalid UTF-8 or JSON on a line is a protocol error unless the adapter declares the line
  type ignorable.
- **Capture.** Raw stdout and stderr are streamed to files in a temp area as received, then moved into the artifact
  store and hashed on completion (`provider_stdout`, `provider_stderr`, FR-EXE-05); stderr is never parsed as protocol.
  Per-stream size cap 64 MiB (proposed target); beyond it capture stops, a `run_event` records truncation, and the run
  continues.
- **Backpressure.** Events are parsed and enqueued to an in-memory bounded buffer (1,000 events, proposed target)
  drained by a single ledger writer that batches inserts per transaction. When the buffer is full the supervisor
  `pause()`s the child's stdout stream so the OS pipe fills and the provider blocks; it resumes at half capacity.
  High-rate partial-delta events are coalesced and not stored individually (Proposed); the raw stream remains in the
  artifact.
- **Exit.** On `exit` + stream `close`, collect `{code, signal}`, flush the writer, then call `extractResult` and
  `classifyFailure`. A controller-side exception in one run's task is contained and fails only that run (NFR-REL-02).
- **Environment.** Built per Invocation from the allowlist (FR-EXE-07); the supervisor asserts no key outside
  `envAllowlist` is present.
- **Reconciliation (WF-8).** For each non-terminal run on startup: if `pgid` is set and a process matching
  `proc_start_ticks` exists, send the cancel sequence to its group (the controller cannot re-attach to its pipes); then
  run the mutation check (§5) and mark `PROCESS_CRASH` with cause `controller_restart` (FR-EXE-06). Killing a still-live
  orphan is a **Proposed** extension of FR-EXE-06, which speaks only of dead processes.
  *As built in M1 (Proposed, Linux `/proc` based, `src/reconcile/process.ts`):* a live orphan group is terminated when
  its leader's start ticks equal the recorded ticks, or when the leader is gone but the group still has live members
  (Linux does not reuse a PID still in use as a group ID). A leader with different ticks (PID reuse) or with no recorded
  ticks is never signalled. Known limit: a crash between spawn and the `pid`/`pgid` commit leaves an orphan that cannot
  be found (ROADMAP R-12). *Since T4.9* the start time and the group scan come from the platform port of §8.3, and a
  leader whose presence cannot be determined while its group still answers `kill(-pgid, 0)` is never signalled either
  (`unverifiable`).

### 8.1 Verification stage (M3, T3.10)

*Implemented 2026-10-13 (Delegated agent work, not owner-approved; every choice below is Proposed).* `delegate_task`
`verification_commands` (FR-EXE-08) run in VERIFYING ([FRD §4](../requirements/FRD.md#4-canonical-run-state-machine),
T11, T14) in `src/lifecycle/verification.ts`, driven by `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`:

- **Admission (FRD V7, owner decision 11).** A non-empty list is refused `INVALID_REQUEST` (`details.field =
  "verification_commands"`) under `inspect-untrusted` and accepted under every other profile; host execution is the only
  kind, because `container` environments are not built (M10). Shape: at most 16 argv arrays of 1 to 64 strings of at
  most 4096 characters, the first non-empty, none containing NUL (the schema plus a re-check in the lifecycle for
  internal callers). The list is stored in `run.request_json` only when non-empty and is part of the idempotency hash.
  `continue_task` takes none ([FRD §7.2](../requirements/FRD.md#72-continue_task)).
- **When.** Only after the agent's process exited successfully (T11; otherwise T13 as before, nothing is run). The
  commands run one after another; the first that does not pass ends the stage and the rest are recorded `not_run` (fail
  fast).
- **How.** Each command is spawned by the run supervisor (§8): argv only, `shell: false`, its own process group, stdin
  closed, cwd the run's worktree. Its `pid`, `pgid` and start ticks are written to the `run` row (with a
