  `verification_command_started` event) before any output is read, so reconciliation can kill an orphan.
- **Environment.** The names in the run's `Invocation.envAllowlist` (the adapter's allowlist, the same source as the
  provider process), read from the controller's environment, minus every `GIT_*` name and the signing and agent
  variables (`GNUPGHOME`, `GPG_AGENT_INFO`, `SSH_AUTH_SOCK`) even if an allowlist names them. Adapter-declared variables
  (`Invocation.env`, which can carry the tool-channel token) are never passed. Today that leaves `PATH` and, for Claude
  Code and Codex, `HOME`.
- **Time.** The run's wall-clock bound (`Invocation.timeouts.wallClockMs`, 30 minutes by default) bounds the whole
  stage; each command gets what is left of it, and there is no idle timeout. Timeout or cancel sends the §8 sequence
  (SIGTERM to the group, the run's kill grace, SIGKILL). The lease is extended at the start of the stage. No new
  configuration key: the bound is the existing per-run one.
- **Outcome (BR-16).** All passed: pending success. Any non-zero exit, signal, timeout or start failure: pending
  `TEST_FAILURE` with the failing command's index in `failure_detail`. Cancel (`cancel_run`, operator cancel, shutdown):
  pending cancelled (FRD §4.3). A controller-side error: `TOOL_FAILURE`. T14 carries the pending outcome and the
  per-command summary. The agent's own result (summary) is kept either way.
- **Records.** Each command that ran appends a `verification_command` event; the terminal transition and the envelope's
  `result.verification` carry one object per command: `{index, outcome: passed|failed|timeout|cancelled|error|not_run,
  exit_code, signal?, duration_ms, output_truncated?}`. No argv and no output appear in events or the envelope
  (FR-TEL-03). The `test_report` artifact (`application/json`, version 1) holds `{v, outcome, commands: [{index, argv,
  outcome, exit_code, signal, duration_ms, stdout, stderr, stdout_truncated, stderr_truncated}]}`; it is stored even
  when the stage was cancelled or failed, and `get_artifacts` returns it. Since T8.10 the body also carries `head_sha`
  (null when the worktree's tree differs from that commit's) and `tree_sha`, and each stored report gets one
  `verification_result` row keyed by `(publish_ref, head_sha)` (T8.10, FR-EXE-10,
  [§20.6](#206-head-pinned-verification-evidence-and-the-docs-alignment-skill-fr-exe-10-fr-skl-11-t810)).
- **Bounds and redaction.** Each stream is captured up to 1 MiB per command (`VERIFICATION_OUTPUT_CAP_BYTES`) and then
  truncated with the marker `[switchboard: output truncated after <n> bytes]`. Argv and output pass through the artifact
  store's redactor (FR-TEL-03, NFR-SEC-01) before they are put into JSON, and the store redacts the whole body again
  when it is written. Redaction is pattern based and best effort (TDD §3.3).
- **Git refs.** The T9.5 ref check (§12.5) runs at FINALIZING, after VERIFYING, so a ref a verification command moved (a
  `git commit` on the run branch, a new tag) is recorded as `git_policy_violation` and controller-owned refs are put
  back before the checkpoint, exactly as for the agent. Prevention is not possible: the commands are arbitrary code on
  the host. A push to a remote cannot be seen (ROADMAP R-61).
- **Confinement (T3.11, FR-EXE-08, FR-POL-06, decision 11).** Optional hardening: when `confinedVerification` is
  configured (or opted in via `SWITCHBOARD_CONFINED_VERIFICATION`), verification commands run wrapped in the outer sandbox
  (`bubblewrap`, §8.4) with the worktree writable and the rest of the host filesystem read-only, all namespaces unshared
  (including network) and `--die-with-parent`. Writes outside the worktree are refused by the kernel with `EROFS`
  (read-only file system). If confined verification is requested but bubblewrap is unavailable, verification commands fail
  closed (`TEST_FAILURE`) without running unconfined.
- **Restart (WF-8).** A VERIFYING run found at startup has its recorded command group killed and ends FAILED
  `PROCESS_CRASH` (cause `controller_restart`) through T14 and T16; the commands are never run again. A recorded
  operator cancel ends it CANCELLED instead.
- **Workspace.** Files the commands write are part of the post-run checkpoint, like the agent's edits (§5).
- **Verification slots (T3.22, FR-EXE-09).** *As built 2026-10-17 (Delegated agent work, not owner-approved; every
  choice below is Proposed).* Off unless the configuration sets a `limits.verification_*` key, so an unconfigured
  controller verifies exactly as described above. Keys: `verification_slots` (1 to 64, Assumption default 2),
  `verification_nice` (0 to 19, Assumption default 10) and `verification_env` (at most 16 upper-case names with string
  values of at most 256 characters; `PATH`, `HOME`, `GIT_*`, `LD_*`, `DYLD_*`, `NODE_OPTIONS` and the signing and agent
  variables are refused). The defaults apply only once any of the three keys is set.
  - *Slot.* Before its first command the stage takes one slot and holds it until its last command ends
    (`src/lock/slots.ts`). A slot is a Unix-domain socket the controller listens on: on Linux an abstract-namespace
    name (`switchboard-<uid>-verification-default-<i>`), elsewhere a socket file in a `0700` directory
    `<tmpdir>/switchboard-<uid>/verification-slots` (refused if another user owns it or others can write it). Binding is
    atomic and the kernel closes the socket when the holder dies, `SIGKILL` included; no ledger row is involved. Slots
    are shared by every controller of the same operator account on the host; the uid in the name keeps another account
    from occupying them. A verification command that outlives a crashed controller no longer holds a slot;
    reconciliation kills it at the next start as above. On non-Linux hosts two waiters clearing the same stale socket
    file at once can, rarely, both bind; the cost is one extra concurrent command.
  - *Wait.* When every slot is held the run stays VERIFYING, appends one `verification_wait` event `{slots}` and retries
    every 100 ms (no FIFO order). The wait is inside the stage's wall clock; a wait that outlasts it ends the stage
    with `outcome: "timeout"` in the `test_report`, every command `not_run`, and the run FAILED `PROCESS_TIMEOUT` through
    T14 and T16. A cancel while waiting ends the run CANCELLED without running a command. A slot that cannot be set up
    (for example an unsafe socket directory) is a controller-side error: `TOOL_FAILURE`, nothing run.
  - *Priority and variables.* Each command's process is reniced by `verification_nice` relative to the controller's
    niceness (capped at 19) right after spawn, before any output is read; descendants inherit it. This is best effort
    (a command that exits first is not an error) and is not a security control. `verification_env` names are added to
    the command's allowlist (FR-EXE-07) with their configured values, which override the controller's environment;
    they are never given to the provider process. Agent-run test commands take no slot (Q-104, Delegated default).
  - *Evidence.* `src/lock/slots.test.ts`, `src/config/verification-limits.test.ts` and the "verification slots (T3.22)"
    tests in `src/lifecycle/verification.test.ts`.

### 8.2 ACP stdio transport (M4, Proposed, T4.7)

*Proposed (Delegated, 2026-10-13), [ADR-0036](../adr/0036-per-run-acp-stdio-client.md); not built (T4.8). Names are from
the ACP specification v1 (agentclientprotocol.com, retrieved 2026-10-13); numbers are proposed targets.* Everything in
§8 above applies to `acp-stdio` unless replaced here.

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T4.8; not owner-approved; tested only against the scripted fake ACP agent, so every
provider behaviour stays Unverified).* `runAcpAgent` (`src/exec/acp/client.ts`) drives one `supervise` call
(`src/exec/supervisor.ts`), which gained `keepStdinOpen` with `writeStdin` and `endStdin`, `fail` (protocol error or a
named timeout: `acp_initialize`, `acp_session`), `complete` (close stdin, wait `killGraceMs`, group kill, outcome
`exited`) and `beforeTerminate` (the graceful cancel step, raced against `killGraceMs`). Framing reuses the JSONL reader
with blank lines refused; `classifyEnvelope` (`src/exec/acp/framing.ts`) adds the JSON-RPC checks. `startAdapterRun`
(`src/adapters/run.ts`) runs the plan audit, then the client with `decidePermission` over the snapshot; the
allow-everything answer needs both the snapshot's and the run's `bypassApproved` and the audit's bypass check. An
asynchronous decision is raced against the 1 s bound and a synchronous one is measured, so either overrun is a
rejection. The permission answer is sent as `result: {outcome: {outcome, optionId?}}` (the [ACP-TC] outcome object in
the schema's response result; Unverified on a live provider); the mode field read is `currentModeId` ([ACP-MODE]).
Exit before the turn ends is reported as outcome `crashed` (`PROCESS_CRASH`). The lifecycle passes the snapshot (no
read-only contexts yet) and writes the three ACP run events. **Not built:** the `provider_stdin` artifact (the client
keeps what it sent on `AdapterRunOutcome.stdin`, but storing it needs a new `artifact.kind`, that is a ledger
migration); backpressure (as for JSONL, the supervisor does not pause stdout); `initialize` as a probe step; no adapter
other than the fake compiles `acp-stdio`.

- **Process.** One agent process per run, spawned like a JSONL provider (own process group, `pid`, `pgid` and start
  ticks persisted first, allowlisted environment, `cwd` = the run worktree) but with `stdio: ['pipe', 'pipe', 'pipe']`:
  the client (`src/exec/acp/`) keeps stdin open and writes JSON-RPC to it. No process outlives its run or serves two
  runs (not FR-EXE-04's persistent transport).
- **Framing.** Each stdout line is one UTF-8 JSON-RPC 2.0 object (newline-delimited, no embedded newline, ACP
  transports page). Max message 1 MiB (the JSONL `maxLineBytes`). `OUTPUT_PROTOCOL_ERROR` (kill the group, keep the
  data) for: an oversize line, invalid UTF-8 or JSON, a JSON array, `jsonrpc` other than `"2.0"`, a response to an id
  the client did not send, a message for another `sessionId`, more than 8 unanswered agent-to-client requests, or a
  `protocolVersion` other than 1 in the `initialize` result. No line is ignorable: ACP forbids non-protocol stdout.
  The client writes only its own messages, one per line; a write error is treated as process exit.
- **Sequence.** `initialize` (version 1; `clientCapabilities` `fs.readTextFile` and `fs.writeTextFile` `false`,
  `terminal` `false`, nothing else; `clientInfo`) within 30 s; then `session/new` (`cwd`, `mcpServers` from the plan)
  or, for a resume, `session/resume` if `sessionCapabilities.resume` was advertised, else `session/load` if
  `loadSession` was, else `TOOL_FAILURE` (`acp_resume_unsupported`), within 60 s, else `PROCESS_TIMEOUT`; an optional
  audited `session/set_mode` or `session/set_config_option`; then exactly one `session/prompt` with one `text` block.
  Never `authenticate`, `logout` or a terminal login; `-32000` on any request ends the run `AUTH_REQUIRED` naming
  `AcpPlan.loginCommand`.
- **Agent-to-client requests.** `session/request_permission`: answered at once from `decidePermission` (§2), with a
  1 s bound after which the answer is a rejection; never left pending. Every other method (`fs/*`, `terminal/*`,
  `elicitation/create`, `_`-prefixed, provider-specific such as `cursor/ask_question`): JSON-RPC error `-32601` at once
  and an `acp_request_refused` event. Notifications other than `session/update` are recorded as `unknown`.
- **End of turn.** The `session/prompt` result or error ends the turn (§2 mapping). The client then closes stdin, waits
  up to `killGraceMs` for the process to exit, and runs the group kill sequence. The exit status after a result is
  recorded and does not decide the outcome; an exit before a result is `PROCESS_CRASH`.
- **Cancel and timeout.** On cancel or a wall-clock or idle timeout: answer every pending permission request with
  `{"outcome":"cancelled"}`, send the `session/cancel` notification, wait up to `killGraceMs` for the `cancelled`
  result, then `SIGTERM` and `SIGKILL` the group as above. The group kill never waits on the agent. Idle time counts
  stdout bytes, as for JSONL; the client answers permission requests itself, so a pending request cannot hold the run
  idle on the controller's side.
- **Capture.** `provider_stdout` holds every received line; `provider_stderr` the agent's logs (never parsed); a
  `provider_stdin` artifact holds what the client sent, after the redactor. `agent_thought_chunk` text stays only in
  the raw artifact (NFR-PRIV-02).
- **Backpressure.** As above; while stdout is paused, permission answers already decided are still written.
- **Reconciliation.** Unchanged: an orphaned ACP agent group is terminated as a JSONL one (FR-EXE-06).

### 8.3 Process table per platform (M4, T4.9)

*Implemented 2026-10-13 (Delegated agent work, not owner-approved; every choice below is Proposed). The macOS path was
written and unit-tested on Linux against fixtures only; from 2026-10-14 to 2026-10-19 it ran on a GitHub-hosted
macOS runner (T4.10: `macos-26-arm64`, macOS 26.6.2, all tests that can run there passed; the job was then removed,
TDD §3.8). The operator's optional live check is
T4.12.* One macOS fact the CI leg found: `kill(-pgid, 0)` answers `EPERM`, not success, for a group whose members are
all zombies, so `groupAlive` (`src/exec/supervisor.ts`) asks the table on `EPERM` as well as on success; `ESRCH` alone
means the group is gone. The supervisor (§8) and reconciliation ask the
host's process table two questions through one port, `src/exec/proc-info.ts` (`ProcInfo`, selected by
`process.platform`):

- `startTime(pid)` → `present` with a start time, `absent`, or `unknown` (cannot verify). Recorded at spawn into
  `run.proc_start_ticks` and compared for equality at reconciliation (FR-EXE-06, NFR-REL-01).
- `liveGroupMember(pgid)` → whether the group has a member that is not a zombie, or `undefined` (cannot tell). Polled
  by the cancel sequence after `kill(-pgid, 0)` succeeds (FR-EXE-01); `undefined` counts as alive, so the controller
  keeps waiting and then escalates to `SIGKILL`, as before.

| Platform | Mechanism | Start time stored as | Cannot verify when |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `linux` | `/proc/<pid>/stat`: field 22 `starttime`; a scan of every `/proc/<n>/stat` for `pgrp` and state `Z`/`X` | clock ticks since boot | `/proc` cannot be listed (group scan only). Any failure to read one PID's `stat` means absent: the M1 behaviour, unchanged |
| `darwin` | one run of `/bin/ps -A -o pid= -o ppid= -o pgid= -o state= -o lstart=` per question, `execFileSync`, no shell, environment exactly `LC_ALL=C` and `TZ=UTC`, 5 s timeout, 16 MiB output cap | UTC epoch seconds parsed from `lstart` | `ps` is missing, exits non-zero, times out or prints any line outside the contract below; or `ps` does not list a PID that `kill(pid, 0)` says exists |
| anything else (`win32`, BSDs, ...) | none | nothing (`null`) | always |

**macOS sources** (retrieved 2026-10-13; fetched text is data). The macOS `ps(1)` manual page
([mirror of the Xcode manual pages](https://keith.github.io/xcode-man-pages/ps.1.html), footer "Mac OS X 12") says:
`-A` "Display information about other users' processes, including those without controlling terminals"; `-o` keywords
may be given "in the form of more than one `-o` option" and "If all keywords have empty header texts, no header line is
written"; the keywords `pid` ("process ID"), `ppid` ("parent process ID"), `pgid` ("process group number"), `state`
("symbolic process state", first character `Z` "Marks a dead process (a 'zombie')") and `lstart` ("The exact time the
command started, using the '%c' format described in strftime(3)"); and "When output is not to a terminal, an unlimited
number of columns are always used". In the POSIX locale `%c` is `d_t_fmt`, `"%a %b %e %H:%M:%S %Y"`, with the English
`abmon` names ([POSIX.1-2024 XBD 7.3.5](https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/9799919799/basedefs/V1_chap07.html)).
POSIX `fork()`: "The child process ID also shall not match any active process group ID"
([POSIX.1-2024 fork](https://pubs.opengroup.org/onlinepubs/9799919799/functions/fork.html)), which carries the
"leader gone, group alive" rule of §8 from Linux to macOS.

**Parsing contract (macOS).** Each non-blank line is `pid ppid pgid state lstart`, blank-separated, the first three
non-negative decimal integers, `state` one token, and `lstart` matching `Www Mmm d HH:MM:SS YYYY` (any run of blanks
between fields, since `%e` pads a one-digit day with a space) with a real calendar date. One line outside this
contract, a header line or empty output makes the whole table unusable (`unknown`/`undefined`); nothing is guessed.

**Assumptions and limits (the `ps` mechanism itself ran on the T4.10 macOS leg; the items below stay Assumptions).**

- `/bin/ps` is the base-system path on macOS (absolute, so `PATH` cannot substitute another program). The manual page
  above names no path.
- `LC_ALL=C` makes macOS `ps` print `%c` in the POSIX form, and `TZ=UTC` makes it print UTC. Both are standard C library
  behaviour; neither is stated in `ps(1)`.
- `lstart` has one-second resolution: a PID reused within the same second as the recorded process started would match.
  macOS is not documented here to allocate PIDs in any order, so this is a residual risk, not a proven impossibility.
- One `ps` run costs a process spawn (it blocks the event loop while it runs); the cancel sequence polls every 20 ms, so
  a cancel on macOS may run `ps` many times. Acceptable for a single operator (ADR-0011); not measured.
- `proc_start_ticks` keeps its name and `INTEGER` type; its unit is per host (Linux ticks, macOS seconds) and it is only
  ever compared for equality on the host that wrote it. A ledger moved between operating systems reads every live
  leader as `pid_reused`, which is never signalled (fail safe). No schema or trust-boundary change, so no ADR
  (Delegated).
- The supervisor's process-group handling itself (`detached` → `setsid`, `kill(-pgid, …)`) is POSIX and unchanged.
  Windows has no such groups and stays Deferred (NFR-PORT-03). Routing refuses every profile off Linux anyway
  (`VERIFIED_PROFILE_PLATFORMS` and `SANDBOX_DEPENDENT_PROFILES` in `src/routing/eligibility.ts`, ROADMAP Q-35), so
  no provider run reaches this port on macOS until a profile's macOS cells are verified (T4.12).

### 8.4 Outer sandbox (M3, T3.1)

Decision and limits: [ADR-0051](../adr/0051-outer-sandbox-detection-and-fail-closed-requirement.md) (Proposed,
Delegated). Code: `src/exec/outer-sandbox.ts`; the gate is passed to `createProfileResolver` (`src/policy/profiles.ts`)
by `serve`.

Configuration (`SwitchboardConfig.outer_sandbox`, `src/config/schema.ts`):

```json
"outer_sandbox": { "required_profiles": ["test-sandboxed"] }
```

`required_profiles` is a list of distinct non-privileged profile names; absent or empty is the default and changes
nothing. `privileged` is rejected at config load.

Detection (`detectOuterSandbox`, never throws; each probe runs with `PATH` only and a 5 s timeout, killed on expiry):

| Mechanism | Available when | Version |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `bubblewrap` | Linux, `bwrap` found on the daemon's `PATH`, and `bwrap --ro-bind / / --dev /dev --proc /proc --unshare-all --die-with-parent -- true` exits 0 | `bubblewrap <v>` from `--version` |
| `prlimit` | Linux, `prlimit` found on `PATH`, and `prlimit --core=0:0 -- true` exits 0 | `util-linux <v>` from `--version` |
| `landlock` | Linux and `/sys/kernel/security/lsm` names `landlock` | Always `null`: the ABI needs a system call Node.js does not expose |

Admission (`outerSandboxRefusal`): a run whose profile is listed is refused by the profile resolver with
`ProfileUnavailableError` (`ELIGIBILITY_NONE`, FRD BR-3) before any row is written. The message carries
`OUTER_SANDBOX_UNAVAILABLE` (bubblewrap or `prlimit` unavailable, each probe's detail included) or
`OUTER_SANDBOX_NOT_ENFORCED` (both available; this gate wraps no process, ADR-0051 item 4). Detection that did not run
counts as unavailable. `serve` detects once at startup, only when the list is not empty; `doctor` always reports the
`outer_sandbox` check (`ok` when nothing is required, `fail` for a missing mechanism, `warn` when only the wrapping is
missing). Wrapping verification commands is implemented by T3.11 (`wrapOuterSandboxVerification`, §8.1). The
provider process is wrapped for the Cursor bypass cell only
([ADR-0059](../adr/0059-cursor-bypass-cell-bubblewrap-wrapper.md), its own wrapper in
`src/adapters/cursor/confinement.ts`, [§2.0.1](#201-cursor-adapter-implementation-evidence-m4-t442-t443)); for every
other provider it remains follow-up work.

## 9. Retry/back-off algorithm

Applies only to `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT`, `PROVIDER_OVERLOADED` and pre-mutation `PROCESS_CRASH`, and only when the
mutation check shows the worktree equals the pre-run checkpoint (FR-CAP-05). Full jitter with a cap; parameters are
proposed targets until M6 measurement:

```text
delay(n) = random_uniform(0, min(cap, base * 2^(n-1)))     n = attempt number of the failed run, starting 1
base = 2 s   cap = 120 s   max_attempts = 3  (initial try + 2 retries)
if provider reported a reset or retry-after time: delay = max(delay, reported - now), bounded by the queue horizon
```

Procedure: failure classified; mutation check; if clean and attempts remain, insert a new `run` (`attempt+1`,
`parent_run_id` set, same `request_hash`, fresh worktree from the same base) in `WAITING_CAPACITY` or `QUEUED` with
`next_attempt_at`; the scheduler wakes on `next_attempt_at` (persisted, so it survives restart). A retry re-runs
preflight and routing, so a breaker or ineligibility ends the retry chain. Dirty workspace: no retry, FR-WS-04 applies.
`QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` follows FR-CAP-06 (failover, else queue if a reset is known within `capacity.queue_horizon`, else
return); no backoff makes a hard quota disappear. Breakers, successor fields, the wake timer and failover are detailed in
[§16](#16-capacity-and-resilience-m6) (T6.1); adaptive concurrency is not planned (per-provider limits are static, §16.1).

## 10. Idempotency and ordering

- **Write before side effect.** Every externally visible action is preceded by a committed ledger row recording intent:
  the `run` row (`QUEUED`) before returning from `delegate_task`; `LEASED` and the lease before `git worktree add`;
  `pid`/`pgid` before reading output; `FINALIZING` before the post-run commit. A crash between intent and effect is
  repaired by reconciliation, which re-derives truth from Git and the process table.
- **State transitions** (states and edges owned by [FRD](../requirements/FRD.md); not redefined here) are single SQL
  transactions:

  ```sql
  BEGIN IMMEDIATE;
  UPDATE run SET state=:to, version=version+1, updated_at=:now /* + failure_code, finished_at ... */
    WHERE id=:id AND version=:expected AND state=:from;     -- changes() must be 1, else ROLLBACK
  INSERT INTO run_event (id, run_id, seq, ts, type, payload_json)
    VALUES (:evt, :id, (SELECT COALESCE(MAX(seq),0)+1 FROM run_event WHERE run_id=:id), :now, 'state_transition', :p);
  COMMIT;
  ```

  Transition and event always commit together (ADR-0005). A failed `changes()` check means another actor moved the run
  (for example cancel vs. completion); the loser re-reads and re-decides. Terminal states are never left.
- **Event sequence numbers.** `seq` is per run, gapless from 1, assigned inside the writing transaction; `UNIQUE(run_id,
  seq)` is the backstop. Only the controller's single writer allocates sequences, so there is no cross-process race (TDD
  §3.5). Consumers (`get_run include_events`, `runs show`) page by `after_seq`.
- **Idempotent API calls.** `(client_id, idempotency_key)` is unique; the stored `request_hash` (SHA-256 over canonical
  JSON of the validated input) distinguishes replay from conflict (FR-MCP-08). The check and the insert happen in the
  same transaction. Calls without a key are not deduplicated.
- **Idempotent reconciliation.** Reconciliation (WF-8) can be re-run safely: each step is conditional on the current
  state and writes an event once.
- **Retention and migrations** interact with ordering only through `schema_migrations`; migrations run before any run
  processing starts (TDD §6).

## 11. Projects, contexts and environments

Implements FR-PRJ-01..10 and [ADR-0013](../adr/0013-projects-environments-and-contexts.md). **Proposed** shapes; M9
(`host`) and M10 (`container`).

### 11.1 Configuration shape

Part of the config schema ([TDD §3.1](TDD.md#31-configuration)); validated with Ajv at startup; an unknown key or
context kind aborts startup (NFR-SEC-06).

```jsonc
{ "projects": [ {
    "id": "web-app",                                   // ^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,40}$
    "name": "Web application",
    "environment": { "kind": "host" },                 // or { "kind": "container", "image_digest": "sha256:...", "limits": { "cpus": 2, "memory_mb": 4096, "disk_mb": 8192 } }
    "max_trust_profile": "edit-untrusted",             // optional; lowers the ceiling (FR-PRJ-06)
    "allowed_providers": ["claude", "codex"],          // optional
    "contexts": [ {
        "id": "main", "kind": "repository",            // only kind in this design; others fail validation
        "path": "/home/op/src/web-app",                // canonicalized, under allowed_roots (FR-POL-10)
        "base_ref": "main",
        "primary": true,                               // exactly one primary context; its worktree is the agent's cwd
        "writable": true,                              // non-primary contexts are read-only mounts (Proposed)
        "push": { "policy": "none" },                  // or { "policy": "branch", "remote": "origin", "branch_prefix": "switchboard/" }
        "identity": { "name": "Operator Name", "email": "operator@example.com" },
        "sign": false                                  // true: controller signs published commits (FR-GIT-10, §12.3.1)
    } ] } ],
  "mcp_clients": [ { "id": "openclaw", "max_trust_profile": "edit-untrusted", "projects": ["web-app"],
                     "manage_projects": false,           // default; true offers create/update/delete_project (FR-PRJ-10)
                     "max_push_policy": "none" } ] }     // widest push policy a dynamic project of this client may use
```

Only the primary context is writable in this design; additional writable contexts (multi-repository changes) are deferred
(ROADMAP §8). A context with `push.policy` other than `none` must have an `identity`.

*Note (2026-10-13, Delegated, [decision 29](../ROADMAP.md#42-owner-decisions), T3.21):* the top-level `timeouts` object
is part of the same Ajv-validated schema (strict; unknown keys abort startup) and today holds one key,
`"timeouts": { "preflight_ttl_seconds": 60 }` (integer 0 to 3600; default 60 is an **Assumption**; 0 disables the
preflight cache, NFR-PERF-02). The cache holds only successful `probe` and `OK` auth results, keyed by adapter id and
the resolved binary's path, `mtime`, size and inode, so an upgraded binary is re-probed immediately and a failure or
an unauthenticated state is re-checked on the next call (used by `delegate_task`, dispatch and `list_capabilities`; [§2](#2-adapter-contract)).

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.1):* `projects[]` is in the Ajv schema (`src/config/schema.ts`, strict: unknown keys
and context kinds abort startup with the JSON path) and `loadConfig` adds the structural rules: unique project and
context ids, exactly one primary context (a sole context is primary unless it says `"primary": false`), only the
primary context writable (default: writable iff primary), `push.policy: "branch"` needs `remote` and `branch_prefix`
(**Assumption**, from the example above), any push other than `none` needs `identity`, `image_digest` required for
`container` and refused for `host`. `base_ref` is required (FR-PRJ-02). Context ids match `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{0,40}$` and
the image digest `^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$` (**Assumptions**). `allowed_providers` takes the provider IDs of
`PROVIDER_IDS` (`claude-code`, `codex`, ...), not the short `claude` shown in the example. `max_trust_profile`,
`allowed_providers` and `sign` are checked for shape only; enforcement is T9.2 and T9.10. The filesystem rules run only
in `switchboard projects validate` (absolute path; `realpath` under an allowed root, so `..` and symlink escapes are
refused; a Git repository whose top level is also under an allowed root; `base_ref` resolves to a commit; a `container`
environment fails with "container environments are not implemented yet (M10)"), so `serve` starts on a config that
lists projects exactly as before and never starts a container. `mcp_clients[].projects`, `manage_projects` and
`max_push_policy` are not in the schema yet (T9.2, T9.9).

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.2):* `mcp_clients[].projects` is in the schema (array of unique project ids matching
the project id pattern); `loadConfig` refuses an id that names no `projects[]` entry (`$.mcp_clients[i].projects[j]:
unknown project id`). **An absent or empty list means the client may use no project** (fail closed; the SRS says "only
the projects listed"); requests that name a workspace path are unaffected. `manage_projects` and `max_push_policy` are
still not in the schema (T9.9).

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.9, [ADR-0030](../adr/0030-dynamic-project-revisions-and-approval.md)):*
`mcp_clients[].manage_projects` (boolean, default `false`) and `mcp_clients[].max_push_policy` (`none | branch`, default
`none`) are in the strict schema; `loadConfig` refuses `max_push_policy: "branch"` on a client without
`manage_projects: true` (`$.mcp_clients[i].max_push_policy: applies only to a client with manage_projects: true`).
`ResolvedMcpClient` gains `manageProjects` and `maxPushPolicy`; a hand-built entry without them reads as off and `none`.
The project shape is exported as `projectSchema` (`src/config/schema.ts`) and is also the `project` input of
`create_project` and `update_project` (§3). No client-level provider list exists, so a client's provider set is every
provider not disabled in `providers` (Delegated).

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.6, [ADR-0028](../adr/0028-controller-push-and-credential-reach.md)):* `loadConfig`
refuses a `push.remote` that is not a remote **name** (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, starting with a letter or digit,
at most 100 characters; so never a URL or anything with `@` or `:`) and a `push.branch_prefix` that is not a branch-name
prefix (letters, digits, `.`, `_`, `-`, `/`; no `..`, `//`, leading `/`, `-` or `.`, no `refs/`, no component starting
with `.` or ending in `.lock`), whatever the policy. The error names the JSON path and the rule, never the value.

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.7, [decision 38](../ROADMAP.md#42-owner-decisions)):* a repository context takes
`"finalize_commit": true` (boolean, default `false`; FR-GIT-09, FRD BR-30) to opt in to finalize-only mode. `loadConfig`
refuses it on a context without `identity` and on a context that is not the primary, writable one. It is frozen into
the run's policy snapshot as `project.finalize_commit: true` (absent when off) and shown by `projects show`. Example:
`{ "id": "main", "kind": "repository", "path": "/home/op/src/web-app", "base_ref": "main", "identity": { "name":
"Operator Name", "email": "operator@example.com" }, "finalize_commit": true }`.

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.4b, [ADR-0029](../adr/0029-claude-code-receives-tool-shim-via-mcp-config.md)):*
`providers.claude-code.git_channel` (boolean, default `false`; accepted under `claude-code` only, so the key on any
other provider aborts startup) is the operator opt-in for the Claude Code tool channel (§12.2). `ResolvedConfig` gains
`claudeCodeGitChannel`; `serve` passes it to the Claude adapter and logs a warning when it is on. It does not change
`providers.<id>.enabled`. Supported, bypass-only (owner decision 2026-10-16; ROADMAP T9.4b). Example:
`"providers": { "claude-code": { "git_channel": true } }`. *Note 2026-10-13 (owner decision 41,
[ADR-0033](../adr/0033-claude-channel-bypass-only-without-safe-mode.md)):* even with the key on, only an approved
`privileged` bypass run gets the channel (and runs without `--safe-mode`); every other run is as with the key off.

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T11.3, [§13.1](#131-configuration-shape)):* a project and each context take an
optional `"inner_agent": "off" | "local_only" | "inherit"` (strict schema; `projectSchema`, so a dynamic project's
`project` input may carry it too, and it needs no operator approval because it can only restrict).
`ResolvedProject.innerAgent` and `ResolvedContext.innerAgent` are present only when written;
`effectiveInnerSetting(project, contextId?)` (`src/config/projects.ts`) returns the most restrictive of the two,
`inherit` for no project. The value is not part of the policy snapshot; callers pass it to the pipeline as
`project_setting`.

### 11.2 Resolution and ceilings

At admission (FRD BR-23): (1) resolve `project` to its definition, else `INVALID_REQUEST`; (2) check the client's project
list, else `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`; (3) effective ceiling = the lower of the client's and the project's `max_trust_profile`
on the order `inspect-untrusted` < `edit-untrusted` < `test-sandboxed` < `trusted-development` < `privileged`, and a
request above it is `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`; (4) remove providers outside `allowed_providers` from the candidate set;
(5) canonicalize the primary context's path against the allowed roots again (config may have changed); (5a) for a
dynamic project use the highest `ACTIVE` revision with `approved_at` set where approval was required, else
`PERMISSION_BLOCKED` with `approval_required`; (6) freeze the
resolved project, environment (kind, image digest, limits), push policy and identity into `policy_snapshot_json`.

A project created by an orchestrator uses the same shape and is validated by the same code. Its `identity` must be copied
from a configured value the operator named in the client entry (`identities` list), so an orchestrator cannot invent an
author (Proposed); the repository `path` must already exist under an allowed root. Approval is `projects approve <id>
[--revision n]`, an operator CLI command that writes `approved_at`; it has no MCP counterpart (FRD BR-32).

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.9, [ADR-0030](../adr/0030-dynamic-project-revisions-and-approval.md)):* step 5a runs
in `resolveForRequest` (`src/mcp/project-tools.ts`) before the configured-project steps: a configured id always wins; a
dynamic project resolves only for the client that created it and only while that client has `manage_projects`
(otherwise `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`, `MANAGE_PROJECTS_OFF` for the creator); a deleted project is `INVALID_REQUEST`
(FR-PRJ-09); with no revision in force it is `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` with `details.reason` `approval_required` (or
`revision_rejected`). The in-force revision is re-checked against the client's current ceiling and `max_push_policy`
and against all the filesystem rules of §11.1 for every context (step 5, extended), then steps 3, 4 and 6 run exactly as
for a configured project; the client's `projects` list does not apply to its own dynamic projects. The snapshot shape is
unchanged; the `route_decision` reasons' `project` object gains `revision`. The orchestrator-identity rule above is not
built as an `identities` list: instead any revision with an `identity` (or `sign: true`) waits for the operator's
approval (Delegated, stricter). Approval is `switchboard projects approve <id> [--revision n] [--reject]`: it decides the
latest revision only when it is `PENDING_APPROVAL` and undecided, needs `--revision` once the project has more than one
revision, and writes a `project_revision_decision` row and a `project_event` row instead of `approved_at`
(`src/cli/projects-dynamic.ts`). `projects list|show` show dynamic projects (`source`, `creator`, `revision`, `state`,
`in_force_revision`, `pending_revision`, `approval_needed`; `show` lists the revisions). Push targets of approved
dynamic projects are refused by design (2026-10-18, owner decision 62; ROADMAP Q-46 answered): the `push` tool and
`switchboard git push` read push contexts from configuration only, so such a push is refused (`CONTEXT_UNKNOWN`);
no task will wire in-force-revision targets. Pinned by `src/git/push-dynamic-refused.test.ts`.

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.2; configured `host` projects only, no dynamic revisions, no step 5a, no push or
identity in step 6):* the steps run in `resolveProject` and `projectCandidates` (`src/mcp/server.ts`) in this order:
(1) unknown id `INVALID_REQUEST`; (2) not in the client's list `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`; a `container` environment fails here
(M10); (5) the primary context's path is canonicalised against the allowed roots again (`INVALID_REQUEST`) and its
`base_ref` must resolve to a commit; (3) the profile is checked against `effectiveMaxProfile` (`src/config/projects.ts`);
(4) candidates are filtered by `allowed_providers` (config ids mapped to adapter ids with `adapterIdForConfigId`;
**Delegated reading of FR-PRJ-06 against FRD BR-23**: `require_provider` naming a provider outside the set is
`PERMISSION_BLOCKED` before any write, a set that leaves no registered provider is `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` with
`details.reason = "PROJECT_PROVIDERS"`, and a non-required preference outside the set is ignored); the excluded
providers are recorded in the `route_decision` reasons as `project.excluded_providers`; (6) the project block is frozen
into the policy snapshot (ADR-0024). The project's `base_ref` starts the first worktree of the meta-session
(`PrepareInput.baseRef`, `src/workspace/manager.ts`); later runs start from the meta-session head as before. A
continuation re-runs steps 1 to 4 for the meta-session's own project: a project removed from configuration is
`INVALID_REQUEST`, one removed from the client's list `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` (history stays, FR-PRJ-09).

### 11.3 Environment port

COMP-EXE reaches the provider process through one port, so `host` and `container` share the supervisor logic:

```ts
export interface Environment {
  readonly kind: 'host' | 'container';
  /** host: returns the invocation unchanged. container: wraps it so the process runs inside the container. */
  prepare(run: RunContext, invocation: Invocation): Promise<PreparedInvocation>;   // creates the container (M10)
  /** Always called, on every terminal path; idempotent. */
  teardown(run: RunContext): Promise<void>;
  /** Startup reconciliation: containers carrying switchboard labels with no matching live run (FR-PRJ-07). */
  sweepOrphans(knownRunIds: ReadonlySet<string>): Promise<{ removed: string[] }>;
}
```

`host` is the identity implementation and keeps today's behaviour. A `container` implementation must not mount more than
FR-PRJ-05 allows, refuse an image with a Git client (`git` or `gh` on the container's `PATH`, FR-PRJ-05), label every
container `switchboard.run` and `switchboard.project`, and record `environment` run events. All Git operations for a
container run stay in the controller on the host.
Container details: [TDD §3.11](TDD.md#311-container-environments).

*As built (2026-10-16, T10.1, Delegated; [ADR-0052](../adr/0052-container-environment-docker-backend.md); text above
unchanged).* Code: `src/environment/types.ts` (the port: `prepare` returns `{ invocation, container? }`, `teardown`
takes the run id, `sweepOrphans` as above), `src/environment/host.ts` (identity), `src/environment/container.ts` (the
`container` implementation over the runtime port `src/environment/runtime.ts`), `src/environment/docker-runtime.ts`
(rootless Docker CLI driver), `src/environment/select.ts` (`environmentKindOf`, `snapshotEnvironment`). Where this
section was silent or the build differs:

- **`prepare` does not create the container.** It checks the runtime (rootless only), resolves `image_digest` among
  local images (never pulls), runs the ADR-0020 `PATH` scan in a labelled probe container, and returns the invocation
  wrapped as `docker run --rm --name switchboard-run-<run> ... <image id> <executable> <provider arguments>`. The
  container is created at spawn, by the supervisor, so the pre-spawn audits (T3.17, T9.4b, ADR-0033) see the
  provider's arguments unchanged. `teardown` force-removes the run and probe containers by name; `--rm` covers the
  normal exit. The executable is argv[0]'s basename, resolved by the image's `PATH`.
- **Lifecycle seam.** `RunLifecycleOptions.environments` (`EnvironmentResolver.resolve(projectSnapshot)` returning
  the environment plus the project's read-only context paths and limits, which the snapshot does not carry). The kind
  is decided from `project.environment_kind` before any lease or worktree; a `container` run with no container
  environment, or a resolver answering the wrong kind, fails `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` (`ENVIRONMENT_UNAVAILABLE`). `prepare`
  runs after the Git tool channel and immediately before the audits and spawn; its refusal fails the run
  `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` with the `EnvironmentRefusal` reason in the transition event; `teardown` runs in the same
  `finally` as the channel close.
- **Snapshot.** `ProjectSnapshot.image_digest` (FR-PRJ-04), frozen at admission for `container`; `snapshotEnvironment`
  is the helper for the admission path.
- **Events** go to the environment's `onEvent` callback (`image_checked`, `prepared`, `refused`, `removed`,
  `remove_failed`, `orphan_removed`); the ledger write and the startup sweep call are T10.2.
- **Admission and `serve` (2026-10-16, #554).** `serve` composes `runEnvironments` when a configured project is
  `container` (`src/cli/serve.ts`); MCP and work-queue admission refuse a `container` project only when placement is
  missing (`TOOL_FAILURE`, `ENVIRONMENT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`, `src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/queue/admission.ts`). A passing
  container run through MCP is therefore reachable on fakes when `serve` wires placement; live provider CLIs in the
  image were checked by the operator (T10.0 Done 2026-10-18, spike §6). A Switchboard container run that uses a login
  volume is still open under M10.
- **Still open after T10.1:** the injected Git tool channel is not reachable from inside a container (host-side socket
  and shim only). Tests: `src/environment/container.test.ts`, `src/environment/docker-runtime.test.ts`,
  `src/lifecycle/environment.test.ts`, opt-in `src/environment/container-docker.test.ts`.

*As built (2026-10-16, T10.2, Delegated; text above unchanged).* `environment` events and the startup sweep:

- **Ledger write.** `environmentEventSink(ledger)` (`src/environment/ledger-events.ts`) is the `onEvent` callback the
  controller composes: each event is appended as a `run_event` of type `environment` with the payload as emitted
  (`detail` truncated to 500 characters). An event of a run the ledger does not know, or a failed write, goes to a log
  line; the sink never throws. The port itself stays ledger-free.
- **Startup sweep.** `reconcile` takes `environments` and, before any run is examined, calls each one's
  `sweepOrphans(new Set(), { owns })`: nothing is live at startup (the data-directory lock is held and nothing has
  been started), so every labelled run or probe container of a run this ledger knows is an orphan. `owns` (a new
  optional argument, `SweepOptions`) leaves a container of a run the ledger does not know alone: it belongs to another
  controller on the same runtime. A listing failure fails reconciliation and so `serve` (fail closed: an orphan could
  still write the worktree). Each non-terminal `container` run gets a `reconciliation` event `environment_sweep`
  (`swept`, `containers_removed`) after its process group is handled and before its worktree is assessed (FR-PRJ-07,
  FR-EXE-06); with no container environment configured it records `swept: false`.
- **`serve`.** `orphanSweepEnvironments` composes the Docker-backed container environment with the ledger sink only
  when a configured project is `container`; a runtime whose `info` is unavailable is skipped with a warning
  (Assumption: a rootless daemon that does not answer runs no container). The lifecycle's run-time resolver is
  composed separately by `runEnvironments` (T10.1, #554) with the same ledger sink. Tests:
  `src/reconcile/reconcile.test.ts` ("T10.2: startup orphan sweep and environment run events"),
  `src/environment/ledger-events.test.ts`, `src/environment/container.test.ts`,
  `src/cli/serve-orphan-sweep.test.ts`.

*As built (2026-10-16, T10.3, Delegated; text above unchanged).* Validation, retention and the container leg of the
malicious-fixture suite:

- **`projects validate`** (FR-PRJ-08, FR-PRJ-05, ADR-0020): for a `container` project, `checkContainerEnvironment`
  (`src/cli/projects.ts`) runs the checks `prepare` runs, in the same order, without starting a run: the runtime
  answers and is rootless (`CONTAINER_RUNTIME_UNAVAILABLE`), the image digest is pinned, present locally and
  unambiguous (`CONTAINER_IMAGE_NOT_PINNED`, `_NOT_PRESENT`, `_AMBIGUOUS`; never pulled), and the no-Git-client scan
  completes with no `git` or `gh` on the image's `PATH` (`CONTAINER_IMAGE_CHECK_FAILED`,
  `CONTAINER_IMAGE_HAS_GIT_CLIENT`). The scan is the same throwaway probe container (`switchboard-probe-validate-<hex>`,
  no network, read-only root, `--rm`, and removed by name afterwards). A project that passes these checks validates
  clean (`projects validate` does not emit `ENVIRONMENT_NOT_IMPLEMENTED`; admission refusal when `serve` does not wire
  placement is separate, T10.1 #554).
- **Retention** (FR-PRJ-09): `runRetentionGc` takes an optional `containerRuntime`; its new `containers` step, after
  the intent and before the worktrees, force-removes every run and probe container labelled with one of the unit's run
  ids (`removeRunContainers`, `src/environment/container.ts`; `GcSessionTargets.runIds`). A listing or removal failure
  stops the unit before its worktrees are touched, and the next pass rolls forward. `gc --apply` composes the Docker
  runtime when a configured project is `container`; when no runtime answers, it reports `container_runtime` and
  collects the rest (`serve`'s startup sweep removes those containers later, T10.2). No volume is removed, because
  this backend creates none (no login volume is mounted yet, T10.1 gap 4).
- **Malicious-fixture container leg** (NFR-SEC-03, FR-GIT-01, FR-GIT-03): `test/malicious/container-leg.test.ts`
  (CI, an emulated rootless runtime) and `test/malicious/container-docker-leg.test.ts` (opt-in, rootless Docker) run
  an approved `privileged` scope with `bypass: true` under a `container` project; the worktree `.git` link check of
  §12.5 is the control the suite found missing.

## 12. Injected Git tools, commits and publication

Implements FR-GIT-01..09 and [ADR-0014](../adr/0014-tool-only-commits-and-publication.md). **Proposed** mechanisms; zemdregon
decided that agents have no commit or push ability and that tools injected by Switchboard do both. Layers and
per-provider unknowns: [TDD §3.10](TDD.md#310-git-commits-attribution-and-publication).

### 12.1 Tool contracts

Served by the shim to the agent as MCP tools; every object has `additionalProperties: false`.

```jsonc
// commit  (always offered under write-capable profiles when the adapter's commitChannel is "injected")
// M11: "message" is required only unless the commit_message task is enabled with mode != never (see below)
{ "input": { "type": "object", "required": ["message"], "properties": {
    "message": { "type": "string", "minLength": 1, "maxLength": 8192 },
    "paths":   { "type": "array", "items": { "type": "string" }, "maxItems": 256 } } },     // worktree-relative; default: all changes
  "output": { "sha": "string", "files_changed": "integer" } }

// push  (offered only when the context policy is "branch" and BR-31 holds)
{ "input": { "type": "object", "properties": {} },        // no arguments: the ref, remote and prefix come from configuration
  "output": { "pushed": "boolean", "ref": "string", "head_sha": "string" } }
```

`push` takes no arguments on purpose, so an injected agent cannot choose a ref. Tool errors use short codes
(`NOT_RUNNING`, `LEASE_LOST`, `PATH_OUTSIDE_WORKTREE`, `NOTHING_TO_COMMIT`, `MESSAGE_EMPTY_AFTER_SANITISING`,
`PUSH_NOT_ALLOWED`, `CREDENTIALS_UNAVAILABLE`, `REMOTE_REJECTED`, `SIGNING_FAILED`, from M11 `MESSAGE_REQUIRED`, and
from T9.14 `SECRET_SCAN_BLOCKED`, §20.9) and are `git_tool_denied` or `git_push` events. They are returned to the agent
and do not fail the run.

**`message` and the inner agent (M11, FR-INN-10, Proposed).** `message` stays required in the published tool schema
unless the `commit_message` task is enabled with `mode` other than `never`; the shim then serves the schema with no
required field, generated per run from the effective configuration. With `draft_if_missing` the agent's own message
always wins and no inner call is made when it is present and non-empty; with `always` the draft is requested even when
the agent supplied one. `MESSAGE_REQUIRED` is returned when no message was supplied and the draft is unavailable
(disabled, suspended, denied by a privacy gate, timed out, circuit open), empty, invalid, or empty after sanitising; its
text asks the agent to call `commit` again with a `message`. The controller never writes a stand-in message. Under
`always`, an unusable draft falls back to the agent's message when there is one, and returns `MESSAGE_REQUIRED` when not.
`MESSAGE_EMPTY_AFTER_SANITISING` keeps its meaning for a message the agent supplied. The tool call waits for the inner
call at most the per-call bound ([§13.4](#134-call-pipeline)).

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T11.4; owner text above unchanged).* Code: `src/inner/commit-message.ts` (task
contract and `createCommitMessageTask`), `src/git/commit.ts` (`CommitMessageTask`, `chooseMessage`),
`src/git/channel.ts`, `src/git/tool-protocol.ts`, `src/cli/tool-shim.ts`, `src/projects/inner-setting.ts`, wiring in
`src/cli/serve.ts`; tests `src/git/commit-message.test.ts`. Choices where this section was silent:

- **Per-run schema.** The channel decides once, at open: the mode is `draft_if_missing` or `always` only when the
  service is `ready`, the task is enabled, its mode is not `never`, and the effective project/context setting is not
  `off`. Then the shim command ends with `--message-optional`, the shim serves the §12.1 schema without `required`
  (`COMMIT_INPUT_SCHEMA_MESSAGE_OPTIONAL`), and `git_channel_opened` carries `commit_message: <mode>`. Otherwise the
  command, schema, argument check and library call are byte-for-byte as before; a missing `message` stays
  `INVALID_ARGUMENTS`. A present `message` is checked exactly as before in either case.
- **Call time.** Every `commit` call re-resolves the project setting from the current configuration (or a dynamic
  project's revision in force; an unknown project or a resolver error is `off`) and passes it as `project_setting`, so
  a project restricted after open gets `PROJECT_OFF` or `PROJECT_LOCAL_ONLY` from the gate and `MESSAGE_REQUIRED`.
- **Where the draft enters.** `src/git/commit.ts` never imports the service (it is now on the import-boundary test's
  protected list): the channel injects a `CommitMessageTask` whose `draft` receives `git diff-tree --numstat` and
  `-p` of the parent and new trees and up to five `git log --format=%s` subjects of the parent, after
  `NOTHING_TO_COMMIT`. Whatever text it returns goes through the publisher's own sanitiser (built-in and operator
  rules plus the run's native session ids), which is the only producer of the text `commit-tree` receives; its rule
  counts are the `sanitizer_json`. A draft is requested once per call and reused after a lost compare-and-swap race.
- **`MESSAGE_REQUIRED` text.** Fixed: "no commit message was given and none could be drafted; call commit again with a
  message". It never says why (gate, timeout, validator).

### 12.2 Channel

The controller creates `<data_dir>/runs/<run_id>/tools.sock` (directory mode `0700`, socket `0600`) and a random per-run
capability token before spawn, and writes the provider-form MCP definition from `channelArgs`: a command that starts
`switchboard tool-shim --socket <path>` with the token in the shim's environment. The shim speaks MCP on stdio to the agent
and newline-delimited JSON to the controller. The controller accepts only connections presenting the token for a run that
is RUNNING, and closes and removes the socket when the run leaves RUNNING. Nothing the agent sends is trusted beyond the
validation in §12.3. Whether provider sandboxes permit the connect is verified per adapter; if not, that adapter is
`finalize-only`.

*Note 2026-10-13 (decision 36).* For Claude Code the per-run definition is now decided: a `0600` file passed with
`--mcp-config`, one server, token in the environment, exact-name allow rules ([ADR-0029](../adr/0029-claude-code-receives-tool-shim-via-mcp-config.md),
T9.4b; supported for approved bypass runs, owner decision 2026-10-16). How other projects pass a per-run tool
definition to each provider (leads only, not
verified here) is listed in [TDD §3.10](TDD.md#310-git-commits-attribution-and-publication) under "External reference
(T3 Code, MIT, studied not copied)". Switchboard implements from provider documentation and `--help`, not from that
project's code.

*Note 2026-10-13 (T9.0 spike; Delegated; findings only, owner text above unchanged).* For Claude Code the per-run
definition is `--mcp-config <json file or string>` with `--strict-mcp-config` (`claude --help` 2.1.296); the JSON shape,
the MCP tool's allow-rule form and whether `--safe-mode` also disables it are unverified, and `--mcp-config` is barred
by ADR-0015 and the argv audit until a new ADR narrows that bar (ROADMAP Q-40). Codex: unverified (no installed binary,
not in [R2]). Whether either provider's sandbox lets the shim connect: `Unknown: live check`. A UNIX socket path is
limited to 108 bytes by the kernel (`/usr/include/linux/un.h`); `<data_dir>/runs/<run_id>/tools.sock` can exceed that
under a long data directory, so the controller should check the length when it creates the socket (Python `bind` failed
at 167 bytes and `gpg-agent` failed to start under a long path on this host; Node 22.22.0 accepted 157 bytes, behaviour
not investigated). Details and sources: [TDD §3.10](TDD.md#310-git-commits-attribution-and-publication).

**As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.4, [ADR-0026](../adr/0026-per-run-tool-channel-socket-and-token.md); owner text
above unchanged).** Code: `src/git/channel.ts` (`GitToolService`), `src/git/tool-protocol.ts` (wire constants shared with
the shim), `src/cli/tool-shim.ts` (the shim), lifecycle wiring in `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`, start-up wiring in
`src/cli/serve.ts`. Choices where this section was silent:

- **Shim.** `switchboard tool-shim --socket <path>`, internal (not in `--help`), dispatched before the operator argument
  parser. It serves only `commit` (§12.1 schema; `push` is T9.6), imports nothing from the controller but the protocol
  constants, reads the token from `SWITCHBOARD_TOOL_TOKEN` and deletes it from its own environment. The shim command is
  the daemon's own entry point: `process.execPath`, the daemon's Node options minus `--inspect*`/`--debug*`, the
  script path, `tool-shim`.
- **Wire format.** One request per connection: `{"v":1,"token","tool","arguments"}` and a newline (at most 2 MiB, within
  10 s), answered by one line `{"ok":true,"result":{"sha","files_changed"}}` or `{"ok":false,"error":{"code","message"}}`.
- **Token.** `sbt1_` plus 256 random bits (`crypto.randomBytes`, base64url). Only its SHA-256 digest is kept, in memory;
  digests are compared with `timingSafeEqual`. It reaches the provider only in the invocation's environment and is bound
  to the run id, meta-session and worktree path; the artifact redactor removes any `sbt1_` token.
- **Checks per call**, each refusal answered with a code and recorded as `git_tool_denied` (code, tool, refused-path
  count; never content or token; at most 32 events per channel): framing and size (`MALFORMED_REQUEST`,
  `REQUEST_TOO_LARGE`, `REQUEST_TIMEOUT`); 60 requests per rolling minute (`RATE_LIMITED`); exact shape; channel open
  (`CHANNEL_CLOSED`); token (`TOKEN_INVALID`); tool offered (`UNKNOWN_TOOL`); arguments (`INVALID_ARGUMENTS`,
  `MESSAGE_TOO_LONG`); run exists, is RUNNING and matches the binding (`RUN_NOT_FOUND`, `NOT_RUNNING`,
  `BINDING_MISMATCH`); then the §12.3 library codes. Eight connections at most. Tool errors never fail the run.
- **Socket path.** `<data_dir>/runs/<run_id>/tools.sock` if at most 100 bytes, else a `mkdtemp` directory
  `sb-git-XXXXXX` directly inside `$XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` or the OS temporary directory; every directory is checked with
  `lstat` (not a link, owned by this user, mode without group or other bits). Node 22.22.0 accepted a 251-byte path in
  `listen()` on this host and left no file at it (consistent with silent truncation; where it bound was not
  determined), which is why the length is checked first. If nothing fits the run fails `TOOL_FAILURE` before spawn.
- **Peer credentials.** Not checked: Node.js exposes no `SO_PEERCRED`. Controls: directory, socket mode, token.
- **Lifetime.** Opened after the compile, immediately before spawn; the adapter's `channelArgs` result is appended to
  the compiled invocation (not inside `compile`, which stays pure and knows no token), so the argv audit sees it.
  Closed in a `finally` once the provider process has exited (calls already past the checks finish first), before
  FINALIZING. Events `git_channel_opened` (tools, socket directory, fallback flag) and `git_channel_closed` (counts).
  At `serve` start, after reconciliation: `GitPublisher.recover()`, then a sweep that closes channels a crash left open
  (unlinks only a socket; removes only an empty, private `runs/<run_id>` or fallback `sb-git-*` directory).
- **When offered.** Write-capable run, adapter `commitChannel: "injected"`, and a frozen project snapshot with a `host`
  environment, a writable primary context and an `identity`; a project run missing one of these gets
  `git_channel_not_offered` with a reason. The snapshot's project block gained `writable`, `identity` and `push.policy`
  (Q-41; the push remote and prefix are not frozen because `remote` is not validated as a name).
- **Adapters.** Only the fake adapter declares `injected`. Claude Code and Codex declare `none` with the reason (Q-40,
  unverified live behaviour, Codex not installed); their argv is unchanged.

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.6, [ADR-0028](../adr/0028-controller-push-and-credential-reach.md)).* The shim
command is `switchboard tool-shim --socket <path> --tools commit[,push]`; the shim lists exactly those tools (the §12.1
`push` schema has no properties) and the controller still refuses a tool it did not offer (`UNKNOWN_TOOL`). `push` is
offered when the frozen snapshot's push policy is `branch`, the BR-31 rule holds (§12.6) and a push service is
configured; otherwise, for a `branch` policy, a `git_push_withheld` event records the reason. A `push` call passes the
same checks as `commit` up to the run binding, must have empty `arguments` (`INVALID_ARGUMENTS`), counts against 10 push
attempts per channel (`PUSH_LIMIT_REACHED`, a `git_tool_denied` event), and then runs §12.6. Its answer is
`{"pushed":true,"ref","head_sha"}` or an error with a §12.1 code and the reason word (`push refused: <REASON>`).
`git_channel_closed` counts `pushes`. *Note 2026-10-13 (decision 37):* for an approved bypass run `push` is offered
whatever the shell (§12.7 BR-31 note) and `git_channel_opened` carries `credential_reach: "bypass_exempt"`; the
provider's Git-write denial and every other check are unchanged.

*As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.4b, [ADR-0029](../adr/0029-claude-code-receives-tool-shim-via-mcp-config.md);
supported, bypass-only since owner decision 56 (2026-10-16)).* Claude Code declares `injected` only with the operator opt-in
`providers.claude-code.git_channel: true` (§11.1 note); without it, `none` and an unchanged argv as above. Code:
`claudeChannelArgs` and `CLAUDE_GIT_CHANNEL_CAPABILITIES` in `src/adapters/claude/adapter.ts`, `auditMcpArgs` and
`assertMcpAudit` in `src/adapters/audit.ts`, `OpenChannel.writeFile` in `src/git/channel.ts`. Choices:

- **Contract.** `ShimSpec` gained `dir` (the run's private channel directory) and `channelArgs` may return `files`
  (`ChannelFile`: a name and contents). The lifecycle has the channel service write them before spawn; an adapter never
  writes a file. The service accepts only names in `CHANNEL_FILE_NAMES` (today `mcp-config.json`), creates the file with
  `O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW` and mode `0600` after re-checking the directory, refuses contents containing `sbt1_`, and
  removes it on close, on the startup sweep and when a crashed run's directory is reused (regular files only).
- **Fragment.** `--mcp-config <dir>/mcp-config.json --allowedTools mcp__switchboard-git__commit` (plus
  `mcp__switchboard-git__push` when offered), appended after the compiled argv; environment exactly `ShimSpec.env`.
  Server name `switchboard-git` (`TOOL_SHIM_MCP_SERVER`). The file (the `env` entry is a reference only, ADR-0029
  item 3):

  ```jsonc
  { "mcpServers": { "switchboard-git": {
      "type": "stdio",
      "command": "<ShimSpec.command[0]>",
      "args": ["<the rest of ShimSpec.command>"],
      "env": { "SWITCHBOARD_TOOL_TOKEN": "${SWITCHBOARD_TOOL_TOKEN}" } } } }
  ```

- **Strictness.** No flag is added for it: the compiled argv already has `--strict-mcp-config` on every invocation but
  an approved bypass run with `repository_config` `enabled` (decision 26), which is ADR-0029 item 5. `--safe-mode`,
  `--restricted`, `--tools` and the Git deny rules are unchanged; the channel tools are not named in `--tools` (built-in
  set only, per the help text).
- **Audit.** Before every spawn, after the bypass audit, `assertMcpAudit` admits at most one `--mcp-config`, in argv
  only, with exactly one value equal to the path the channel service wrote (absolute, normalised, named
  `mcp-config.json`), with `--strict-mcp-config` unless the run context is an approved bypass with `repository_config`
  `enabled`; and an `mcp__` token only as a value of `--allowedTools` equal to `mcp__switchboard-git__<tool>` for an
  offered tool. It refuses the `--mcp-config=` form, inline JSON, other paths, a second occurrence or value,
  server-wide, wildcard and foreign MCP rules, and any MCP rule without a channel; it then checks the file is a plain
  `0600` file owned by this user in a private directory. A refusal fails the run `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`
  (`reason: "MCP_CONFIG_REFUSED"`) and closes the channel (removing the file) before the run is terminal.
- **Assumptions (live checks, ADR-0029 item 7 and ROADMAP T9.4b):** the JSON shape; `${VAR}` expansion; that a second
  `--allowedTools` adds to the first (if it replaces it the run is denied more, not less); that `--tools` does not
  filter MCP tools; the `mcp__<server>__<tool>` rule form. Each failure mode found so far leaves the agent without the
  tool (fail closed), never with more permission.

*Note 2026-10-13 (owner decision 41, [ADR-0033](../adr/0033-claude-channel-bypass-only-without-safe-mode.md); mechanism
Delegated; UNSUPPORTED at that point — supported, bypass-only since owner decision 56 (2026-10-16)).* An operator-machine
live check (`docs/research/live-checks-2026-10-13.md`, PR #41; a
hand-made server, not the shim) confirmed the `mcpServers` shape, `${VAR}` expansion (an unset variable stays literal),
environment inheritance and exact-name rules under `dontAsk`, and found that `--safe-mode` stops the server from
starting. So, superseding the "unchanged `--safe-mode`" bullet above for Claude:

- **Bypass only.** `GitCapabilities` gained the optional, pure `offersChannel(request, profile)`. When it is false for
  a run the lifecycle treats the adapter as `none` for that run: no channel, no `channelArgs` call, no event, argv
  unchanged, and `finalize` receives `commitChannel: "none"` (so FR-GIT-09 applies). Claude's predicate
  (`claudeChannelOffered`) is true only for `privileged` with a scope that sets `bypass` and `bypassApproved`;
  `claudeChannelArgs` refuses every other run (`PROFILE_UNSUPPORTED`) as a second check.
- **`--safe-mode` removed.** `ChannelArgs` gained the optional `removeFlags`: standalone flags (`--name`, no `=`) the
  lifecycle drops from the compiled argv and `policyFlags` before appending the fragment (absent flags are a no-op; any
  other spelling fails the run `ELIGIBILITY_NONE`, `reason: "GIT_CHANNEL_INELIGIBLE"`). Claude returns
  `["--safe-mode"]`. `--strict-mcp-config`, the Git deny rules, `--tools` and worktree isolation are unchanged.
- **Audit additions.** `auditMcpArgs` also refuses `--mcp-config` unless the run context admits a bypass, and when the
  invocation's `env` lacks a non-empty `SWITCHBOARD_TOOL_TOKEN`. `auditSafeMode` (run before every Claude spawn, as
  `assertSafeModeAudit`) refuses an argv without `--safe-mode` unless the run is an approved bypass run and either
  `repository_config` is `approved` or `enabled` (decision 26) or the argv carries `--mcp-config`. A refusal fails the
  run `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` (`reason: "ISOLATION_REFUSED"`) and closes the channel.
- **Environment.** The stdio server inherits the claude process environment, which is the adapter's child environment:
  `PATH`, `HOME` (its allowlist) and the token only.

### 12.3 Commit algorithm and refs

Refs (Proposed): the **publish ref** for a meta-session is `refs/heads/<prefix><sess>`, where `<prefix>` is the context's
`push.branch_prefix` (default `switchboard/pub/`), kept apart from the internal run branches `switchboard/<sess>/<run>`.

1. Verify the run is RUNNING and holds the lease; the path set is inside the worktree after canonicalization.
2. Build a temporary index file seeded from the publish ref's tree (or the run's base commit for the first commit); add
   the requested paths, or all worktree changes, from the worktree; write the tree. If the tree equals the parent's,
   `NOTHING_TO_COMMIT`.
   *(M11)* If the `commit_message` task applies (no agent message, or `mode` `always`), the inner pipeline
   ([§13.4](#134-call-pipeline)) runs here on the diff between the parent tree and the new tree, limited to the paths
   being committed. The result is a draft message or "unavailable".
3. Sanitise the message (§12.4); drafted text goes through exactly the same sanitiser, with the same built-in and
   operator patterns, as the agent's own text. If it is empty afterwards, `MESSAGE_EMPTY_AFTER_SANITISING` for an agent
   message and `MESSAGE_REQUIRED` for a draft or when neither exists.
4. Create the commit object with author and committer set to the context identity, current time, no trailers, no
   signature unless the context sets `sign: true` (§12.3.1), hooks disabled and a scrubbed environment (as in §5).
5. Move the publish ref from the old head to the new commit with a compare-and-swap; on a lost race, retry from step 2
   once, then fail the call.
6. In one ledger transaction insert the `git_commit` row and the `git_commit` run event.

**Note 2026-10-13 (T9.0 spike; Delegated; verified on Git 2.43.0 in a throwaway repository with a neutral configuration;
owner text above unchanged).** The plumbing below built a commit from a path subset, with the worktree's own index file,
`HEAD` and run branch byte-for-byte unchanged (`sha256sum` of the worktree index before and after was identical; `git
status --porcelain` identical; `HEAD` and the run branch unchanged). The lab ran with `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL` set to an
empty file and `GIT_CONFIG_SYSTEM=/dev/null`, not through `src/workspace/git.ts`; the controller adds `scrubbedGitEnv()`
and `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null` (see Hooks below). `cwd` was the worktree and `GIT_INDEX_FILE` an absolute path
outside the worktree and its Git directory.

| Step | Command | Observed on 2.43.0 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Seed | `GIT_INDEX_FILE=$IDX git read-tree <publish head or base>` | temporary index holds the parent's tree |
| Add | `GIT_INDEX_FILE=$IDX git --literal-pathspecs add -- <path>...` (or `--pathspec-from-file=- --pathspec-file-nul` with NUL-separated paths on stdin) | adds modified and new files and **stages deletions** (a deleted `sub/s.txt` left the tree); other changed files stay out of the tree |
| Tree | `GIT_INDEX_FILE=$IDX git write-tree` | tree id; equal to the parent's `^{tree}` means `NOTHING_TO_COMMIT` (checked with a second identical add) |
| Commit | `printf '%s' "$msg" \| GIT_AUTHOR_NAME=.. GIT_AUTHOR_EMAIL=.. GIT_AUTHOR_DATE=.. GIT_COMMITTER_NAME=.. GIT_COMMITTER_EMAIL=.. GIT_COMMITTER_DATE=.. git commit-tree <tree> -p <parent> -F -` | commit object with exactly the author, committer and dates given (`2026-10-13T10:00:00Z` gave `1791885600 +0000`), the message and no trailer |
| Publish | `git update-ref refs/heads/<publish> <new> <old>`; `<old>` is 40 zeros for the first commit | succeeds with the right old value; with 40 zeros on an existing ref: `cannot lock ref ...: reference already exists`; with a wrong old value: `is at <sha> but expected <sha>` (rc 128 each), which is the compare-and-swap of step 5 |

Observations the algorithm should respect:

- **Pathspecs.** Without `--literal-pathspecs`, `git add -- '*.txt'` staged every matching file; with it, the same
  argument matched nothing (`fatal: pathspec '*.txt' did not match any files`). A path that is literally `we*ird[1].txt`
  staged only that file in both modes when named exactly (exact names win), so use the literal flag for safety, not
  because exact names break. `git add -- ../outside.txt` is refused (`fatal: ... is outside repository`, rc 128);
  a missing path is refused (`did not match any files`, rc 128); an ignored path is refused (`The following paths are
  ignored by one of your .gitignore files`, rc 1) and `git add -- .` skips it. The commit tool's own
  `PATH_OUTSIDE_WORKTREE` check still comes first.
- **Checkpoints stay out of the publish history.** After a checkpoint commit was made on the run branch with the same
  temporary-index method (author `Switchboard <switchboard@localhost>`), `git rev-list refs/heads/<publish>` listed only
  the publish commits and the base, `git merge-base --is-ancestor <checkpoint> <publish>` returned 1, and
  `git for-each-ref --contains <checkpoint> refs/heads/switchboard/pub/` printed nothing. This holds by construction:
  each publish commit's parent is the previous publish commit or the base, never a run-branch commit.
- **Second commit** seeded from `read-tree <publish ref>`, then `add -- c.txt`, gave a tree whose
  `git diff-tree -r <first> <second>` was exactly `A c.txt`.
- **Reflog.** `update-ref` without `-m` records an empty reflog message; with `-m 'x'` it records `x`. The reflog stays
  local, but use no `-m`, or a fixed neutral text, so no identifier lands there.
- **Hooks.** Only `update-ref` was seen to run a hook (`reference-transaction`): with the layer 3 guard of [TDD
  §3.10](TDD.md#310-git-commits-attribution-and-publication) in the environment the update was refused, and with `-c
  core.hooksPath=/dev/null` or an environment without the guard it succeeded. The controller therefore must not inherit
  the guard variables. Whether `read-tree`, `write-tree` and `commit-tree` run any hook was not tested (none was configured
  for them in the lab).
- **Identity.** `commit-tree` takes identity from the four `GIT_AUTHOR_*`/`GIT_COMMITTER_*` variables; set all of them
  (and the dates) rather than relying on configuration, because Git may derive an identity from the host name.
- **Minimum versions.** Every command above exists on 2.43.0. Introduction versions are unverified, except
  `--pathspec-from-file`, which the Git 2.25.0 release notes introduce for "a few commands" without naming `git add`,
  so its availability for `add` below 2.43 is unverified. The decision on `MIN_GIT_VERSION` (raised to 2.32, ADR-0023) is
  in TDD §3.10 and ROADMAP Q-39.
- **Not tested.** Nested repositories (ADR-0022), symlinks, very large path sets, concurrent publishers beyond the
  compare-and-swap above, and `-c core.hooksPath=/dev/null` under a Git older than 2.43. (Clean and smudge filters
  during `git add`, and submodules, are covered through the publisher path instead: `src/git/commit.test.ts`,
  "repository filter drivers and submodules".)

**As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.3, [ADR-0025](../adr/0025-git-commit-provenance-and-sanitiser.md); owner text above
unchanged).** Code: `src/git/commit.ts` (`GitPublisher.commit`, `GitPublisher.recover`), `src/git/publish-ref.ts`;
every Git
call goes through `src/workspace/git.ts`, whose `GitRunOptions` gained `env` (restricted to `GIT_INDEX_FILE` and the six
author and committer variables) and `input` (standard input). Choices where §12.3 was silent:

- **Publish ref.** `refs/heads/switchboard/pub/<sess>` by default, not `<push.branch_prefix><sess>`: the example prefix
  `switchboard/` would give `refs/heads/switchboard/<sess>`, which cannot coexist with the run branch
  `refs/heads/switchboard/<sess>/<run>`. `push.branch_prefix` stays the remote branch prefix (§12.6). A prefix in the
  `switchboard/sess_*` namespace is refused. Open: Q-42.
- **Order (replaces "move the ref, then insert" for crash safety).** (1) temporary index seeded from the publish head
  (or the
  session's `base_commit`), `add`, `write-tree`, `NOTHING_TO_COMMIT` if the tree equals the parent's; (2) sanitise;
  (3) `commit-tree` (identity and dates from the context, message on standard input, trailing newline only); (4) lineage
  check: `rev-list --count <new> --not <base> <old head>` must be 1; (5) append the `git_commit_intent` run event,
  which holds
  the whole provenance record (SHAs, ids, provider, harness, model, rule counts; no message text); (6) `update-ref
  <ref> <new>
  <old>` (old is all zeros for the first commit); on failure re-read the ref: moved means `git_commit_aborted` and a retry
  from step 1, once, then `PUBLISH_CONFLICT`; (7) in one transaction append the `git_commit` event and insert the
  `git_commit` row. Failure to write step 7 after a successful step 6 is reported as `PROVENANCE_PENDING` with the SHA.
- **Recovery.** `GitPublisher.recover()` (call at controller start, before serving; wiring is T9.4) finds `git_commit_intent`
  events with neither a `git_commit` row nor a `git_commit_aborted` event for the same SHA. If the commit is an
  ancestor of
  the publish ref it writes the row and event (the event payload has `recovered: true`); if not it appends
  `git_commit_aborted` (`not_published_at_recovery`); if the repository cannot be read it reports the SHA as `unresolved`
  and tries again next time.
- **Paths.** With `paths`, each is normalised and checked before anything is staged; any refusal refuses the whole
  call and
  reports every refused path with a reason (`invalid`, `outside_worktree`, `git_dir`, `symlink_in_path`, `missing`,
  `ignored`, `nested_repository`, `contains_nested_repository`); the call code is `PATH_OUTSIDE_WORKTREE` when any
  path is
  outside or reached through a symbolic link, else `PATH_REFUSED`. A path must be worktree-relative (an absolute path is
  `outside_worktree`); `.` is `invalid`. A missing path is a deletion only if the parent tree has it. An ignored path is
  refused unless the parent tree already tracks it. A directory path that contains a nested repository (ADR-0022) is
  refused: name the files. Staging uses `git --literal-pathspecs add -- <paths>`; names starting with `-` or `:` were
  tested. Without `paths`, `git add -A -- . <nested excludes>` stages every change; ignored files are skipped.
- **Not tested here.** Very large path sets (the limit is 256; T9.7 covers 300 paths with the loosened finalize-only
  flag), Git older than 2.43.0 and concurrent publishers in separate processes (only the compare-and-swap protects
  them). Clean/smudge/process filters, textconv/external diff drivers and submodules are covered by
  `src/git/commit.test.ts` ("repository filter drivers and submodules"): the drivers never run and the working-tree
  bytes are committed; a submodule gitlink is left at the parent's, and naming it (or a directory above it) is refused
  as a nested repository.

**As built (2026-10-13, Delegated, T9.7, finalize-only mode, FR-GIT-09, FRD BR-30; [decision
38](../ROADMAP.md#42-owner-decisions); owner text above unchanged).** Code: `src/git/finalize.ts`, called from
`RunLifecycle.finalize` (`src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`) with a second `GitPublisher` whose `allowedStates` is
`["FINALIZING"]` (`src/cli/serve.ts`); the tool channel's publisher still accepts only RUNNING. Choices where §12.3 and
BR-30 were silent:

- **When.** Only for an adapter whose `commitChannel` is not `injected`, a project run whose frozen snapshot has
  `finalize_commit: true`, and a worktree run. Order inside FINALIZING: ref check (§12.5), assessment and evidence
  checkpoint, then the commit, then the terminal transition. The steps of §12.3 apply unchanged except step 1, which
  checks FINALIZING instead of RUNNING (the run still holds the lease). The FRD §4.5 placement "ahead of the
  checkpoint" is read as "before the terminal transition": the commit takes its path list from the checkpoint's
  changed-file list, and neither touches the other's refs.
- **Input.** Paths: the changed-file list of the post-run checkpoint (pre to post, nested repositories excluded),
  deletions included; at most 256 (§12.1), else no commit (an approved bypass run has no count limit, decision 44).
  Message: the adapter's structured final summary
  (`RunResult.summary`, FR-ADP-04), passed whole to the §12.4 sanitiser with the run's native session ids; empty,
  absent or empty after sanitising means no commit; over 8192 characters means no commit (no truncation, so an
  identifier cannot be split past the sanitiser). *Approved bypass runs (ROADMAP decision 44, Q-45):* neither limit
  applies. The publisher receives `looseLimits: true` (honoured only with `origin: "finalize_only"`; the `commit` tool
  never sets it), skips its 256-path check, sanitises the whole message, and then cuts a message longer than 8192
  characters at a line boundary (or at the limit when no boundary keeps half of it) and appends a one-line note; path
  classification, the temporary index and the compare-and-swap are unchanged, with pathspecs passed on stdin. Identity,
  project and context: the frozen snapshot.
- **Never committed** (each a `git_finalize_commit` event `{outcome: "skipped", reason}`): the run is not about to end
  SUCCEEDED (`run_not_succeeded`); `environment_not_host` (M10); `context_not_writable`; `no_identity`; any
  `git_policy_violation` event in any run of the meta-session (`git_policy_violation`, BR-27); no publisher configured
  (`service_unavailable`); `no_changes`; `too_many_paths`; `no_message`; `message_too_long`; `nothing_to_commit`.
  A §12.3 error is `{outcome: "failed", reason: <code>}` with refused paths as counts per reason (`refused`), never
  names; `PROVENANCE_PENDING` adds the SHA. A commit is `{outcome: "committed", commit_sha, provenance_id,
  files_changed}`. The run's outcome never changes; the terminal transition payload gains `finalize_commit: <sha>` or
  `finalize_commit_skipped: <reason>`.
- **Provenance.** The T9.3 sequence unchanged (intent, compare-and-swap, event and row in one transaction;
