  "resume_directory_unavailable"` and `details.problem` (one of `no_origin_run`, `directory_mismatch`,
  `outside_session_directory`, `not_a_directory`, `not_registered`, `locked`, `unclean`, `unreadable`). `TOOL_FAILURE`
  rather than `INVALID_REQUEST` for the reason ADR-0038 item 7 gives: the request is valid and the fault is the
  controller's state. T5.6 replaces this refusal with the handoff of step 3. If the LEASED re-check fails (a race after
  admission), the run fails `LEASED → FAILED` before spawn with `TOOL_FAILURE` and the same `reason` and `problem` in
  the transition event; nothing ran in any directory. *Superseded for `continue_task` by T5.6 (2026-10-14): the
  refusal is now the handoff of step 3 (§15.7 "As built"); the LEASED re-check is unchanged.*
- No migration and no new path scheme: run paths stay `<data_dir>/worktrees/<sess>/<run>`, and a resume's
  `run.worktree_path` is its origin's path, so GC (which de-duplicates recorded paths) and the orphan sweep are
  unchanged.

Tests: `src/lifecycle/resume-directory.test.ts`, the T5.7 case in `src/mcp/mcp.test.ts`, the `resumeCwd` cases in
`src/adapters/providers.test.ts` and the T5.12 declaration and second-worktree cases in
`src/adapters/claude/claude.test.ts`.

### 15.7 `continue_task` with handoff

The input schema of §3 is unchanged. After C1 to C4 and C7 ([FRD §7.2](../requirements/FRD.md#72-continue_task)):

1. Session provider `P0`: the provider of the latest `provider_binding`, else of the latest run that had one.
2. Target and mode (BR-18): `routing.provider_preference` naming a provider other than `P0` gives a handoff to that
   provider (`require_provider` as V8 and BR-3). With no other provider named: `P0` eligible, a binding present and
   the §15.6 directory usable gives a resume; `P0` eligible but no binding, or the directory unusable, gives a handoff
   preferring `P0`; `P0` ineligible gives a handoff over the project's allowed providers (FR-PRJ-06) without `P0`,
   unless `require_provider` is set, which fails with the BR-3 code.
3. `privileged` across providers (Q-61): if the target differs from `P0` and the resolved profile is `privileged`, and
   `PRIVILEGED_CROSS_PROVIDER` (one constant, Proposed `src/continuity/policy.ts`) is `'refuse'`, fail
   `INVALID_REQUEST` with `details.reason = "PRIVILEGED_CROSS_PROVIDER"`. Default `'refuse'` until zemdregon answers
   Q-61; `'allow_under_approval'` applies C7 and §3.1 **Bypass** unchanged to the target provider.
4. A target not allowed by the project fails `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` before any side effect.
5. Handoff only: take `as_of`, build the bundle. Any build failure fails the call with `TOOL_FAILURE`,
   `retryable: false`, `details.reason = "HANDOFF_BUNDLE_UNAVAILABLE"` and a fixed message; nothing is written
   (an artifact file already stored is an orphan for GC). No new code is added to §4.
6. One admission transaction: the run row (`request_json.handoff` for a handoff), the `handoff_view` artifact row,
   the `route_selected` and `handoff_built` decisions and the first `run_event`. The new run starts from the head
   checkpoint in a fresh worktree; the provider gets a fresh native session and a second binding is written
   (FRD WF-3). Provider A's binding is untouched.
7. The envelope's `route.reasons` names the mode and reason code; `artifacts.handoff_view` holds the view's id
   (T5.10). No bundle text is in any envelope (FR-MCP-04).

*As built (T5.6, 2026-10-14, Delegated, not owner-approved; no migration).* Code: `continueTask` in
`src/mcp/server.ts`; the Q-61 constant `PRIVILEGED_CROSS_PROVIDER = 'refuse'` and `refusesPrivilegedCrossProvider` in
`src/continuity/policy.ts` (read by `continueTask` and by the lifecycle's handoff guard, so the hook cannot route around
it); the `HandoffBundleError` mapping in `src/mcp/errors.ts`; the `continuity.*` keys in `src/config/schema.ts` and
`src/config/load.ts`, passed to the server by `src/cli/serve.ts`. Choices the steps above left open:

- **Order.** Only `P0` is probed before step 3, so the Q-61 check runs before any other provider is probed or anything is
  written. Consequences (fail closed): a `privileged` continuation that names another provider is refused even if
  routing would have fallen back to `P0`; when `P0` is ineligible a `privileged` continuation is refused with
  `PRIVILEGED_CROSS_PROVIDER` (no `to_provider` in `details`) rather than with `P0`'s BR-3 code.
- **Project (step 4).** A named provider other than `P0` that the project does not allow is `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`, with or
  without `require_provider` (in `delegate_task` a preference outside the project only narrows). `P0` outside the project
  with no provider named is ineligible (BR-23) and not probed, so the continuation is a `bound_provider_ineligible`
  handoff over the project's other providers; with `require_provider` naming it, `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`.
- **Named provider ineligible without `require_provider`** (**Assumption**): routed normally over the project's
  providers, so the target may be another provider or `P0`; it is still a handoff with reason `provider_named` (BR-18:
  "route normally and build a handoff bundle"). A name equal to `P0` is treated as no name.
- **No other provider allowed.** `P0` ineligible and no other provider in the candidate set: `P0`'s BR-3 code, or
  `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` when `P0` is outside the project.
- **No head checkpoint.** A session whose runs left no head (its only run was cancelled before it started) cannot give a
  bundle, so a continuation that must hand off (`no_binding`) fails `HANDOFF_BUNDLE_UNAVAILABLE` (ADR-0038 item 7); it was
  `INVALID_REQUEST` ("no native session to resume") before T5.6.
- **`as_of` still current.** The bundle is built and stored before the admission transaction; inside it the lifecycle
  checks that the session still has exactly the runs, base and head of `as_of`, else it refuses with the bundle failure
  code and writes nothing (a stored file is a GC orphan, as step 5 allows).
- **Record.** The route decision's `reasons_json` gains `continuation: { mode, reason, from_provider, problem? }`
  (`problem` only for `resume_directory_unavailable`); the selection basis, and so the envelope's first route reason, reads
  `selected <provider> (<mode> <reason> from <P0>; <basis>)`.
- **Configuration.** `continuity.max_bundle_bytes` (16,384 to 1,048,576), `continuity.max_diff_excerpt_bytes` (0 to
  1,048,576) and `continuity.max_text_chars` (1 to 65,536), integers, unknown keys refused; defaults as §15.4. The upper
  limits are **Assumptions** (§15.4 names none) that keep a typo from producing a prompt of many megabytes.
- **WF-3 postcondition** (**Assumption** on its reading): after a handoff the latest binding is B's, so `P0` is B and a
  continuation naming A again is a handoff (step 2), not a resume of A's session. "Still resumable" is read as: A's
  binding row, native session and transcript are untouched and a resume of A's native session still runs; the test shows
  it through the lifecycle (`resume` on A in A's origin worktree).

Tests: `src/mcp/continue-handoff.test.ts` (both fakes: each BR-18 branch, the refusals with nothing written, an end to
end handoff with commits whose provenance names each run's provider and the transcript canaries absent from B's prompt
and the bundle), the T5.6 case in `src/mcp/mcp.test.ts` (an unusable resume directory becomes a same-provider handoff),
`src/continuity/policy.test.ts` and the `continuity` cases in `src/config/config.test.ts`.

*As built (T5.10, 2026-10-14, Delegated, not owner-approved; no migration, no new operator command).* `get_artifacts`
already lists the `handoff_view` row of a handoff run and `get_run` carries its id (T5.6); neither carries bundle text
outside `get_artifacts`, whose purpose is the view. `runs show <run>` (`src/cli/runs.ts`) adds `run.handoff` for a run
whose `request_json` names a bundle, in `--json` and as a "Handoff (FR-SES-05)" block in the text view:
`bundle_artifact_id`, `bundle_sha256`, `as_of_sha256`, `renderer_version`, `from_provider` and `reason` from
`request_json.handoff`; `source_run_ids` (the runs of the stored view's `as_of`, oldest first, read through the content
address and checked against the recorded hash) and `source_run_id` (the last); `binding` (id, provider, harness, backing
provider, model, creation time). Choices:

- **Source runs** come from the stored view, not from a guess at the run history. A collected, altered or unreadable view
  gives `null`, shown as `-`.
- **Binding** is **Assumption**-grade: `provider_binding` has no run column, so it is the first binding of the run's
  provider created at or after the run was created; `null` until the run reports a native session.
- **No native session id** in this block (not needed to audit a handoff); the run's own events print what they print.
- **Regenerating a view on demand** remains not planned: it would be a new operator command and needs an FR-OPS entry
  first.

Tests: the "handoff view on the read surfaces" cases in `src/mcp/continue-handoff.test.ts` (MCP and CLI on the two fakes,
plain text and `--json`; a handoff section absent for a non-handoff run; a missing view gives unknown source runs).

## 16. Capacity and resilience (M6)

Implements FR-CAP-01 (use) to FR-CAP-08, FR-MCP-02 (`get_capacity`), FR-MCP-10, FR-OPS-05 and NFR-PERF-03 (per
provider) under [ADR-0043](../adr/0043-capacity-estimate-breaker-and-retry.md), which extends
[ADR-0007](../adr/0007-capacity-as-confidence-estimate.md). Everything here is **Proposed** (T6.1, 2026-10-14,
Delegated). Every default is an **Assumption** that no measurement supports; each is configurable and recorded in ROADMAP
§7.3 (Q-69 to Q-72). Provider facts come only from the [T6.0 inventory](../research/capacity-signals-2026-10-14.md).
No migration: every column used exists since migration 0001.

### 16.1 Configuration

```jsonc
{ "capacity": {
    "window_seconds": 3600,          // 60..604800; the estimator's look-back window (Q-69)
    "confidence_runs": 10,           // 1..1000; terminal runs in the window at which confidence reaches 1 (Q-69)
    "queue_horizon_seconds": 0,      // 0..86400; BR-9 wait for a known reset; 0 = never wait (Q-72)
    "breaker": { "failure_threshold": 3, "open_seconds": 300 },   // 1..100, 1..86400 (Q-70)
    "retry": { "max_attempts": 3, "base_ms": 2000, "cap_ms": 120000 } // 1..10, 1..600000, 1..3600000; §9 values (Q-71)
  },
  "providers": { "<id>": { "concurrency": 1 } }  // 1..64, T6.9; default limits.per_provider_concurrency
}
```

Unknown keys and type errors abort startup (NFR-SEC-06). `base_ms` must not exceed `cap_ms`. Absent section: the
defaults above. There is no rate-gate key (FR-CAP-07, §16.7).

### 16.2 Estimator

A pure function `estimateCapacity(inputs, config, now)` per provider (Proposed module `src/capacity/`), fed by a
collector that reads only:

| Input | Source | Label in `sources` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Terminal runs of the provider with `finished_at` in the window, with their state and `failure_code` | `run` | `controller:run_outcomes` |
| `rate_limit` provider events of those runs and of running ones, with `reset_at` | `run_event` (`compactEvent` payload) | `provider_reported:rate_limit` |
| Count and latest `observed_at` of `usage_observation` rows with `source = 'provider_reported'` in the window | `usage_observation` | `provider_reported:usage` |
| Breaker state, `breaker_until`, `consecutive_failures` | `provider_health` (§16.4) | `controller:breaker` |
| Slots in use (runs in `PREFLIGHT`, `LEASED`, `RUNNING`, `VERIFYING`, `FINALIZING`) and the provider's limit | `run`, config | `controller:concurrency` |
| `providers.<id>.weight` (ADR-0007's prior) | config | `operator:weight` |

It never reads `inner_agent` or `controller_estimated` usage rows (BR-41, FR-INN-15), retrieval or inner-agent output
(FR-INN-06, FR-RAG-12), or any artifact content. A label is listed in `sources` only if that input contributed (the
slot count always does).

Derivation, first match wins for `throttle_risk`:

1. **Evidence.** There is evidence if the window has a terminal run that reached `RUNNING`, a `rate_limit` event, or a
   breaker that is not `CLOSED`.
2. `reset_at`: the latest provider-reported `reset_at` in the window that is later than `now`, with `source:
   "provider_reported"`; else `null`.
3. `throttle_risk`: `high` if the breaker is `OPEN` or `HALF_OPEN`, `reset_at` is set, or a run ended
   `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` in the window; else `elevated` if a run ended `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT` or `PROVIDER_OVERLOADED` or a
   `rate_limit` event occurred in the window; else `low` if there is evidence; else `unknown`.
4. `capacity_score` in [0, 1]: `null` for `unknown`; 0 for `high`; otherwise `prior × factor` with `prior` the
   configured `weight` (1 when unset) and `factor` 1 for `low`, 0.5 for `elevated` (Q-69).
5. `confidence` in [0, 1]: 0 for `unknown`; 1 when `high` comes from the breaker or a future `reset_at` (a controller
   fact or a provider report); otherwise `min(1, terminal_runs / confidence_runs)`, rounded to two decimals.
6. `explicit_remaining`: `{ value, unit, source: "provider_reported", observed_at }` from the latest usage row that
   holds the key declared for that provider in `REMAINING_KEYS`, else `null`. `REMAINING_KEYS` is empty: no run output
   reports remaining quota (T6.0), so the value is `null` for every provider until a task adds a sourced key.
7. `concurrency_available = max(0, limit - in_use)`; `concurrency_limit = limit`.

Numbers are rounded to two decimals, keys are sorted and providers are listed in registry order, so the same inputs
give byte-identical canonical JSON.

*As built (T6.2, 2026-10-14, Delegated):* `src/capacity/estimate.ts` (the pure function and `REMAINING_KEYS`, empty and
frozen), `src/capacity/collect.ts` (the reads above through four read-only ledger helpers; a crash whose
`failure_detail` carries `cause controller_restart` is flagged and not counted), `src/capacity/projection.ts` (the
§16.3 object and its canonical JSON) and `src/config/capacity.ts`. A `CANCELLED` or pre-spawn run is in
`terminal_runs` and the confidence count but is not evidence on its own.

### 16.3 `get_capacity` and `switchboard capacity`

Input `{ provider?: string }`, a provider id known to the registry. An unknown id is `INVALID_REQUEST`. With no input,
the tool covers every registered provider. Output:

```jsonc
{ "as_of": "<ISO time>", "window_seconds": 3600,
  "note": "capacity_score, confidence and throttle_risk are controller estimates, not provider-authoritative quota",
  "providers": [ {
    "provider": "fake", "enabled": true,
    "capacity_score": null, "confidence": 0, "throttle_risk": "unknown",   // controller_estimated
    "estimate_source": "controller_estimated",
    "sources": ["controller:concurrency"],
    "explicit_remaining": null,         // or { value, unit, source: "provider_reported", observed_at }
    "reset_at": null,                   // or { at, source: "provider_reported" }
    "concurrency_available": 1, "concurrency_limit": 1,
    "breaker": { "state": "CLOSED", "until": null, "consecutive_failures": 0 },
    "window": { "terminal_runs": 0, "counted_failures": 0, "quota_exhausted": 0, "rate_limit_signals": 0,
                "usage_observations": 0 }
  } ] }
```

T6.7 adds an optional per-provider `cache` block and a top-level `affinity` block (§16.8). `switchboard capacity
[--provider <id>] [--json]` prints the same projection: `--json` is the same object, and the text view prints one block
per provider, headed by the `note`. **Wording rule (FR-CAP-03):** on every surface, any line or key containing
"remaining" also contains "provider_reported" or "provider-reported", and no surface prints "quota left", "available
quota" or a percentage of an estimate. `doctor` prints one `capacity` line per provider: breaker state, `throttle_risk`
and `confidence`, labelled "estimate".

T6.14 adds an optional top-level `pacing` array, one entry per subscription (§16.9). It is omitted when no subscription
is configured, so an unconfigured projection stays byte-identical. `doctor` prints one pacing line per subscription.
The gate verdict is shown; the projection holds nothing. The hold is T6.8 (§16.9), applied at admission and in
`pump()`.

*As built (T6.3, 2026-10-14, Delegated):* `get_capacity` is the eighth tool, after `list_projects`; its input reuses the
provider-id pattern of `delegate_task` and the handler refuses an id that is not registered. The listed providers are
every registered provider, in candidate order first (fixed 2026-10-17: the `provider` filter accepts any registered id,
including `fake`, even when `candidateOrder` omits it). `switchboard capacity` and `doctor` list the adapters `doctor`
probes. `capacity --json` adds `schema_version` as every CLI `--json` does. With no ledger file, the CLI computes over
an empty in-memory ledger and creates nothing. The `doctor` JSON gains a `capacity` key, and its text gains one line per
adapter. The section never changes `ok`, and an unreadable ledger leaves it out.

### 16.4 Per-provider circuit breaker

State in `provider_health` (one row per adapter provider id; created on first write, with `auth_state = 'UNKNOWN'` and
`version_state = 'UNVERIFIED'` as placeholders that nothing reads, because the preflight cache stays authoritative for
auth and version). The breaker writes only `breaker_state`, `breaker_until`, `consecutive_failures`, `last_failure_code`,
`updated_at` and `version`.

| Outcome of a run on provider P (at its terminal transition) | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `FAILED` with `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT`, `PROVIDER_OVERLOADED`, or `PROCESS_CRASH` whose cause is not `controller_restart` (counted) | `consecutive_failures + 1`; `CLOSED` at the threshold, or `HALF_OPEN`: to `OPEN`, `breaker_until = now + open_seconds` |
| `SUCCEEDED`, or `FAILED` with `TEST_FAILURE`, `TOOL_FAILURE`, `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` or `SANDBOX_VIOLATION` after the provider ran (healthy) | `consecutive_failures = 0`; `HALF_OPEN` to `CLOSED` |
| Anything else (`CANCELLED`, `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`, `PROCESS_TIMEOUT`, failures before spawn, a restart crash) | No change |

Admission and dispatch read the row with the clock. `OPEN` before `breaker_until` gives the eligibility reason
`CIRCUIT_OPEN`, which maps to `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` (FRD BR-3), and `pump()` leaves that provider's queued runs in `QUEUED`
(FRD T4 guard). `OPEN` at or after `breaker_until` is treated as `HALF_OPEN`, and the row is written `HALF_OPEN` when the
first run is dispatched. While a provider is `HALF_OPEN`, `pump()` dispatches one of its runs only when none is in a
slot state: that run is the probe. Each transition appends one run event to the run whose outcome caused it (or, for
`HALF_OPEN`, to the probe run): `breaker_opened` (`provider`, `consecutive_failures`, `until`), `breaker_half_open`
(`provider`) or `breaker_closed` (`provider`). After a restart the row is read as is. A `HALF_OPEN` provider with no run
in a slot state gets a new probe.

*As built (T6.4, 2026-10-14, Delegated):* `src/capacity/breaker.ts` (the pure table and admission) and the lifecycle hooks.
The outcome is applied in the transaction of the terminal transition. `CIRCUIT_OPEN` is a stage-1 reason at
admission and in `list_capabilities`, but not at PREFLIGHT: by then `pump()` has already held the run or picked it as
the probe. A run that was in PREFLIGHT when the breaker opened therefore still runs. One wake timer re-runs `pump()` at
the earliest cool-down end. A healthy outcome while the breaker is `OPEN` (a run that started before it opened) resets
the count but leaves the breaker open; only a probe closes it. A probe that ends neutral (for example cancelled) leaves
`HALF_OPEN`, and the next queued run becomes the probe.

### 16.5 Retry with successor runs

Applies at T16 to a run that ended `FAILED` with `RATE_LIMIT_TRANSIENT`, `PROVIDER_OVERLOADED` or `PROCESS_CRASH`, when
the `workspace_assessment` shows the worktree unchanged since the pre-run checkpoint (BR-7; `retry_eligible` true). The
steps run in one ledger transaction, after the breaker update of §16.4:

1. **Stop conditions**, first match recorded as `retry_not_scheduled` (`reason`): `attempts_exhausted` (`attempt >=
   max_attempts`); `breaker_open` (the provider's breaker is now `OPEN`); `reset_beyond_bound` (a provider-reported
   `reset_at` of this run lies more than `max(cap_ms, queue_horizon_seconds)` ahead).
2. **Delay** (LLD §9): `random_uniform(0, min(cap_ms, base_ms × 2^(attempt - 1)))`. If the run reported a `reset_at`,
   the delay is at least `reset_at - now`. Clock and random source are injectable.
3. **Successor row:** same `meta_session_id`, `trust_profile`, `policy_snapshot_json`, `request_json`, `request_hash`,
   `client_id`, `provider`, `priority` and project; `parent_run_id` = the failed run; `attempt + 1`; `idempotency_key`
   `NULL` (the first run keeps it, so a replayed key still returns the first run); `state = 'WAITING_CAPACITY'`;
   `next_attempt_at = now + delay`. Its first event is `retry_of` (`run_id`, `attempt`, `failure_code`). The failed run
   gets `retry_scheduled` (`successor_run_id`, `attempt`, `delay_ms`, `not_before`), and its `workspace_assessment`
   records `auto_retry: true`.
4. **Wake:** the lifecycle keeps one timer for the earliest `next_attempt_at` among `WAITING_CAPACITY` runs. It arms the
   timer at start, after scheduling and after a cancel. When it fires, due runs move to `QUEUED` (T18) and `pump()`
   runs. A successor dispatches like any queued run: it re-runs preflight and routing, so an ineligible provider or an
   open breaker fails or holds it as for a new run, in a fresh worktree from the meta-session's head (unchanged, because
   the failure rolled the head back to the same base).

A `privileged` successor runs under the meta-session's approval as is (FR-POL-09, FRD C7): it is the same provider and
scope. Reconciliation after a restart (WF-8) does not schedule retries: an orphaned run keeps `auto_retry: false` (ADR-0043
item 4). A `WAITING_CAPACITY` successor survives a restart unchanged and is woken by the timer.

**`get_run`** gains `retry_of` (the parent run id when `attempt > 1`) and `superseded_by` (the run whose `parent_run_id`
is this run), each present only when set. `runs show` prints both and gives `null` in `--json` when one is unset.

*As built (T6.5, 2026-10-14, Delegated):* `scheduleRetry` and `releaseDueWaiting` in `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`.
`workspace_assessment.auto_retry` now equals `retry_eligible`: it means the controller will try. Whether it did is
recorded by `retry_scheduled` or `retry_not_scheduled`. A run with no worktree assessment (a read-only run without a
worktree) is never retried, with `reason: workspace_not_assessed`. A retryable code from a pre-spawn failure has no
assessment either, so it follows the same rule. The wake timer is the one the breaker uses (§16.4); `pump()` first moves
due `WAITING_CAPACITY` runs to `QUEUED` (T18, event payload `reason: next_attempt_due`). `next_attempt_at` stays on the
row as a record.

### 16.6 Quota exhaustion ladder (T6.6)

At T16 with `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`, after the breaker step (no change, §16.4), in BR-6 order:

1. **Failover** when the request permits it (BR-8): `require_provider` is not set, the profile is not `privileged`
   (Q-61 default), and another provider is eligible. A successor run (`parent_run_id`, `attempt + 1`) continues on that
   provider through the M5 handoff ([LLD §15.7](#157-continue_task-with-handoff)). Its bundle lists the failed attempt.
   A changed workspace is already preserved as the evidence checkpoint and the head is rolled back (FRD §4.4) before the
   bundle is built. The route decision records `continuation.reason = "quota_failover"`. M7 adds the affinity re-base.
2. **Wait** (BR-9) when a provider-reported `reset_at` of the run lies within `queue_horizon_seconds`: a successor on the
   same provider in `WAITING_CAPACITY` with `next_attempt_at = reset_at`.
3. Otherwise the run stays `FAILED` with `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`, and nothing more is created.

While a provider has a provider-reported `reset_at` in the future from a `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` run, the eligibility filter
gives it the reason `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED_KNOWN`. BR-3 maps that reason to `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` for `require_provider`, and FRD
T7 then applies at preflight.

*As built (T6.6, 2026-10-14, Delegated):* `quotaLadder` in `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` runs after the terminal
transaction, because a failover builds a bundle. Failover is permitted only when the run has a route decision whose
`features_json.required_provider` is null (no route decision: fail closed, `failover:no_route_decision`), the profile is
not `privileged`, and the attempt limit of §16.5 is not reached. The candidates are the providers eligible at admission
minus the failed one and any project-excluded one, re-checked now (breaker and known quota included). The first eligible
one in candidate order is chosen (BR-4). The successor is admitted through `submit` with `handoff` (reason
`quota_failover`, a new route reason code) and the internal `successorOf`. The wait step reuses the §16.5 successor
with `next_attempt_at` set to the reported reset. One `quota_ladder` event (`outcome` `failover`, `wait` or
`returned`, and `not_taken` with the reason each earlier step did not apply) records the result. FRD T7
(PREFLIGHT to WAITING_CAPACITY) is not built: a known reset is a stage-1 reason at admission instead
(`QUOTA_EXHAUSTED_KNOWN`, BR-3 `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`). Only the five most recent `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` runs of a provider are
read for it (Assumption). A queued run is not failed by it.

### 16.7 Rate gate (FR-CAP-07)

No gate exists. The T6.0 inventory finds no published enforceable limit for the logins in use. A token-bucket gate at a
published rate is added only for a provider whose limit a source names, with its own configuration key and source
citation; T6.8 no longer builds that gate (see the 2026-10-17 note below).

*Note 2026-10-17 (owner decision 57, [ADR-0055](../adr/0055-model-groups-and-usage-pacing.md)).* T6.8 is reopened as
the pacing gate of [§16.9](#169-usage-windows-and-pacing-fr-cap-09-fr-cap-10), whose source is observed usage and
operator-declared budgets, not a published limit. No token bucket at a published rate is specified, because none is
published.

### 16.8 Cache observations and switch counts (FR-CAP-08, T6.7)

The capacity module maps the reported cache keys that T6.0 sources (Grok Build `cache_read_input_tokens` and
`cache_creation_input_tokens`; Cursor `cacheReadTokens` and `cacheWriteTokens`; Antigravity `cache_read_tokens`;
OpenCode `cache.read` and `cache.write`) from `usage_observation.metrics_json` into a per-provider `cache` block (`read`,
`creation`, `rows`, `source: "provider_reported"`, `key_names`) summed over the window. A provider with no sourced key,
or no row holding one, has no block, never a zero. The top-level `affinity` block counts, per project key (§3.2), the
runs whose provider and model equal the previous run's (`kept`) and those that differ (`switched`), grouped by
`affinity_switch_reason` from `route_decision.reasons_json`, or `unrecorded` when the decision carries none (a row
written before M7, a continuation, a change of reported model alone, or affinity disabled; T7.11). Wording: "reported",
never "cache hits".

*As built (T6.7, 2026-10-14, Delegated):* `src/capacity/cache.ts`. Only Grok Build is mapped. It is the only provider
with both a sourced key name and an adapter that stores it (flat `cache_read_input_tokens` and
`cache_creation_input_tokens`; that its `streaming-json` `end.usage` uses the names seen in `json` output is an
Assumption). Cursor, Antigravity and OpenCode join when their adapters store their keys; Codex's key is synthetic only
and Claude Code's is unknown. The `cache` block sums the window's rows that hold a key, with `key_names` and
`key_source`. The `affinity` block is always present in the projection (`source: "controller"`, a `note`, `projects`
sorted by key, at most 100 with `truncated`; the bound is an Assumption). It counts runs that started a provider
process and were created in the window. `get_capacity` counts only the calling MCP client's own runs, so another
client's project ids are never shown (Delegated, least privilege). The operator's `switchboard capacity` and `doctor` see
every project. `doctor` prints one summary line, and `switchboard capacity` one line per project.

### 16.9 Usage windows and pacing (FR-CAP-09, FR-CAP-10)

**Proposed** (2026-10-17, owner decision 57, Delegated mechanism; T6.13, T6.14 and the gate T6.8 built 2026-10-17).
Design for T6.13 (built; see "As built (T6.13)" below), T6.14 (built; see "As built (T6.14)" below) and T6.8 (built;
see "As built (T6.8)" below) under
[ADR-0055](../adr/0055-model-groups-and-usage-pacing.md). Every default is an **Assumption** (Q-98). No migration:
pacing is computed on read from configuration, `usage_observation`, `run`, `run_event` and the clock, like the estimate
(§16.2). Nothing here names a provider's window, budget or reset: each comes from the provider (a declared key) or from
the operator.

**Configuration** (strict keys, NFR-SEC-06):

```jsonc
{ "capacity": {
    "subscriptions": {
      "<name>": {                                   // ^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]{0,31}$; operator's own label
        "providers": ["<config provider id>"],      // 1..16; a provider is in at most one subscription
                                                     // (`fake` only when the test-only fake adapter is registered; §17.7)
        "windows": [ {
          "id": "<window id>",                      // ^[a-z0-9_-]{1,32}$, unique in the subscription
          "length_seconds": 0,                       // 60..2678400; operator-declared
          "reset": { "kind": "provider_reported" }   // or { "kind": "fixed", "anchor": "<ISO instant>" } or { "kind": "rolling" }
          , "budget": { "unit": "runs", "value": 0 } // optional; unit runs | reported_tokens | reported_cost_usd; value > 0
        } ]                                          // 1..4 windows
      } },
    "pacing": {
      "mode": "gate",            // gate | score | off
      "target_fraction": 0.95,   // 0.5..1; T in ADR-0055 item 7 (Q-98)
      "burst_fraction": 0.10,    // 0..0.5; B in ADR-0055 item 8 (Q-98)
      "min_confidence": 0.5      // 0..1; an estimate below it gives the score term only (Q-98)
    } } }
```

A `fixed` window resets at `anchor + k × length_seconds`. A `provider_reported` window takes its reset from the latest
declared reset key (below) that is still in the future, and without one its reset is `unknown`. A `rolling` window has
no reset: its used share is taken over the last `length_seconds`. Without a `budget`, a window has no estimate.

**Declared keys** (`USAGE_KEYS` in the capacity module, empty and frozen until a task wires a sourced key, as
`REMAINING_KEYS` in §16.2). An entry names a provider, the `usage_observation.metrics_json` key or `rate_limit` event
field, and what it carries (`used_fraction`, `remaining_fraction` or `reset_at`), with the source document. Candidates
named by T6.0, none wired: Antigravity's `/usage` `remaining_fraction` and `reset_time` per bucket (a separate call,
Q-101), and Claude Code's `rate_limit_event` `resetsAt` (a synthetic fixture shape, T6.11).

*As built (T6.15, 2026-10-17, Delegated):* still none wired. The T6.11 captures
([limit captures 2026-10-17](../research/limit-captures-2026-10-17.md)) hold one structured limit signal that was not
already mapped, Antigravity's stderr line `AGY_ERROR: {...}` at exit 3. Its `status` `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` maps to
`QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` in the adapter's `classifyFailure` from the stderr tail (`src/adapters/antigravity/protocol.ts`,
`AGY_ERROR_STATUS_CATEGORIES`; the structured fields only, FR-ADP-04), and its reset is free text inside `short_error`,
so no reset key is declared (Q-100). Every other captured limit was free text. A 2026-10-18 follow-up
([limit captures 2026-10-18](../research/limit-captures-2026-10-18.md)) adds one T6.15 candidate, Grok Build's
`http_status` 402 in its text-mode `Internal error:` object (usage balance exhausted, no reset key); not wired.
`USAGE_KEYS` stays empty and frozen until
a capture shows a structured used or remaining fraction or reset time. The adapter contract gives `extractResult` no
stderr (§2), so the mapping is reached only by a caller that passes the tail `startAdapterRun` captures; the lifecycle
does not yet (pinned in `src/adapters/antigravity/limit-signals.test.ts`).

*Update 2026-10-18 (owner decision 64, [ADR-0058](../adr/0058-captured-limit-signatures.md), ROADMAP T6.15):*
two newer captures map here too without declaring a key: Cursor's
out-of-usage stderr line (`ActionRequiredError` plus `out of usage` on one tail line at exit 1) and Kimi Code's 5-hour
usage-limit stderr line (`403` plus `usage limit` on one tail line at exit 1, checked before the auth mapping because
the CLI labels the stop `provider.auth_error`), both to `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED` in `classifyFailure`
(`cursorQuotaClassification` in `src/adapters/cursor/adapter.ts`, `kimiQuotaClassification` in
`src/adapters/kimi/adapter.ts`). Neither sources a reset and neither adds a `USAGE_KEYS` entry, so the keys stay empty
and frozen. Both signals are free text under T6.11's classification (no structured field); they map as
structured-enough captured distinctive signatures under owner decision 64
([ADR-0058](../adr/0058-captured-limit-signatures.md), which supersedes ADR-0055 item 11 in part and answers Q-100;
history in `docs/_doc-alignment-notes.md` §24). As with Antigravity, the mapping is reached only through
`FailureEvidence.stderrTail`, which `extractResult` cannot supply (pinned in `src/adapters/cursor/limit-signals.test.ts`
and `src/adapters/kimi/limit-signals.test.ts`).

**Per window, at time `now`** (pure function, injected clock):

| Figure | Rule | `source` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `reset_at` | `fixed`: next anchor step after `now`; `provider_reported`: latest declared reset in the future; `rolling`: `null` | `operator_declared`, `provider_reported` or `null` |
| `window_start` | `reset_at - length_seconds`; `rolling`: `now - length_seconds` | as `reset_at`, else `controller` |
| `used_fraction` | the latest declared `used_fraction` (or `1 - remaining_fraction`) observed after `window_start`; else, with a `budget`, `observed / budget.value` where `observed` counts runs that started a provider process (`runs`), or sums the provider-reported token keys the capacity module maps (`reported_tokens`) or the reported cost (`reported_cost_usd`) over the subscription's providers since `window_start`, capped at 1; else `null` | `provider_reported`, `controller_estimated` or `unknown` |
| `confidence` | 1 for `provider_reported` with a reported or `fixed` reset; for `controller_estimated`, 0.8 with a `fixed` or reported reset and 0.5 for `rolling` (Assumptions, Q-98); 0 for `unknown` | — |
| `elapsed_fraction` | `1 - (reset_at - now) / length_seconds`, clamped to [0, 1]; `rolling`: 1 | — |
| `allowance` | `(T - used) / max(1 - elapsed, 0.01)`; `rolling`: `T - used`; `null` when `used` is `null` | — |
| `pace` | `unknown` when `allowance` is `null`; `ahead` when `used > T × elapsed + B`; `behind` when `used < T × elapsed - B`; else `on_pace` | — |

An estimate never reads `inner_agent` or `controller_estimated` usage rows (BR-41). It is labelled `"lower_bound":
true` with the note "Switchboard-observed usage only; use of this subscription outside Switchboard is not seen".

**Per subscription:** the binding window is the one with the smallest non-null `allowance`. The `pacing` score term of
§17.7 is `clamp(allowance / (2T), 0, 1)` for the binding window, and 0 when every window is `unknown`.

**Gate** (`mode: gate`). A window *defers* when `reset_at` is not `null`, `used_fraction` is not `null`, `confidence ≥
min_confidence` and `used ≥ min(T, T × elapsed + B)`. Its gate-open time is the earlier of `reset_at` and the instant at
which `T × elapsed + B = used`, that is `window_start + length_seconds × (used - B) / T`. A subscription is deferred
while any window defers, until the latest gate-open time among those windows. The gate holds that value fixed until a
new observation arrives; usage that Switchboard dispatches in the meantime only moves it later.

- **Admission** (after stage 1, before selection, §17.3): a candidate whose subscription is deferred gets the
  per-candidate verdict `PACING_DEFERRED` (with `until`) and is left out of selection when an undeferred eligible
  candidate remains. It is not a stage-1 reason, so it never produces `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` or a BR-3 code.
- **All deferred:** affinity holds if the affine candidate is among them (§17.7); otherwise the candidate whose gate
  opens first is chosen, ties broken by the §17.3 selection rule. The run is admitted to it, and the decision records
  `pacing.hold_until`, that candidate's gate-open time. `pump()` leaves the run in `QUEUED` until that time, as for an
  open breaker (FRD T4 guard), and appends one `pacing_hold` event (`subscription`, `window`, `until`, `source`). The
  lifecycle's single wake timer (§16.4) also arms for the earliest `pacing_hold` time. At dispatch, a run whose
  subscription is no longer deferred goes ahead.
- **Never:** a refusal, a failure, a new run, a probe or any call to a provider made in order to use a subscription.
  `require_provider` keeps its meaning: the run waits for its own subscription's gate. A `privileged` run waits the same
  way, under its approval as is.

**Surfaces.** The §16.3 projection gains a top-level `pacing` array, one entry per subscription:

```jsonc
{ "subscription": "<name>", "providers": ["<id>"], "mode": "gate", "deferred_until": null,
  "windows": [ { "id": "<window id>", "length_seconds": 0,
                 "reset_at": { "at": "<ISO>", "source": "operator_declared" } | null,
                 "used_fraction": { "value": 0.42, "source": "controller_estimated", "confidence": 0.8,
                                    "lower_bound": true, "basis": "runs" } | null,
                 "elapsed_fraction": 0.4, "allowance": 1.32, "pace": "on_pace", "defers": false } ] }
```

The FR-CAP-03 wording rule applies: "remaining" only next to `provider_reported`; an estimate is never shown as a
percentage of a plan, and the text view prints "used (estimate, Switchboard-observed only)" for `controller_estimated`.
`doctor` prints one pacing line per subscription: `pace`, `deferred_until` and the source.

*As built (T6.13, 2026-10-17, Delegated):* the configuration keys and the per-window figures. The projection block that
reads them is T6.14 (below); the gate that holds runs is T6.8 and the score term is T7.15 (built; see §17.7). `capacity.subscriptions`
and `capacity.pacing` are
strict in `src/config/schema.ts`; `src/config/capacity.ts` refuses a provider id that is not configured, except the
test-only `fake` adapter id when that adapter is registered (`checkCapacity` over `memberProviderIds(true)` via
`loadConfig({ testFakeAdapter })`; T7.13 fix, 2026-10-18, `src/config/fake-model-groups.test.ts`), a provider in
two subscriptions, a window id repeated in a subscription, a `fixed` reset without an ISO instant with an offset as
`anchor`, and an `anchor` on any other reset. Subscriptions resolve in name order with the adapter id of each provider,
and only when the file sets `subscriptions` or `pacing`, so a configuration without them resolves exactly as before.
`usageWindows` in `src/capacity/usage-window.ts` computes the table above and returns nothing when no subscription is
configured. Points this section left open, each an Assumption: an unknown figure is `{ "value": null, "source":
"unknown", "confidence": 0 }` rather than `null`; with an unknown reset, `window_start` and `elapsed_fraction` are
`null`, so a `provider_reported` window without a declared reset key has no estimate either; a reported share in a
`rolling` window has confidence 0.5; a declared reset is read only as an ISO string; `reported_tokens` uses the
`usage_per_window` token rule of `src/control/budgets.ts` (`total_tokens`, else every `*_tokens` key);
`reported_cost_usd` sums the first of `total_cost_usd` (Claude Code) and `cost_usd` (OpenCode) in each row; figures are
rounded to six decimals. `USAGE_KEYS` entries name `origin` (`usage_observation` or `rate_limit`), `key`, `carries`
and `source`.

*As built (T6.14, 2026-10-17, Delegated):* the optional top-level `pacing` array on the one projection behind
`get_capacity`, `switchboard capacity` (text and `--json`) and `doctor`. It is omitted — not sent as `[]` — when
`usageWindows` returns nothing, so an unconfigured projection stays byte-identical. Each entry has `subscription`,
`providers` (config ids), `mode`, `deferred_until`, `verdict` (`deferred`, `clear` or `inactive`) and `windows`. Each
window is the T6.13 figures, including `window_start`, plus `defers`. `used_fraction` stays the T6.13 object. There is
no `openAt`: the hold that acts on `deferred_until` is T6.8 (`Done` 2026-10-17; see "As built (T6.8)" below). A window
defers only in mode `gate`,
with a known reset, a non-null used value and elapsed fraction, confidence at or above `min_confidence`, and used at or
above `min(T, T × elapsed + B)`, using those published figures. Its gate-open time is the earlier of `reset_at` and
`window_start + length_seconds × (used − B) / T`, rounded to the millisecond — except at `used ≥ T`, where the capped
defer line never catches up, so the gate opens at the reset (T6.16, `gateOpenMs` in `src/capacity/projection.ts`).
`deferred_until` is the latest of those
times, else null. Mode `score` or `off` is `inactive` and never defers; a rolling window, an unknown source and an
estimate below `min_confidence` never defer. `doctor` prints one line per subscription from the binding window (the
smallest non-null allowance, else the first): pace, `deferred_until` and the source, with
"estimate, Switchboard-observed only" when that source is `controller_estimated`. The text view prints the same
phrase on every `controller_estimated` pacing line, and does not print "remaining" there or a plan percentage. The
`get_capacity` output schema publishes the
block as an optional property; other tool schemas are unchanged. `src/mcp/server.ts` passes `subscriptions` and `pacing`
through to the projection so the tool and the CLI read one object. Q-98 defaults stay the Delegated ones already in
the configuration (`gate`, T 0.95, B 0.10, `min_confidence` 0.5).

*As built (T6.8, 2026-10-17, Delegated):* `src/capacity/pacing-gate.ts` turns the T6.14 figures into deferrals:
`pacingDeferrals` reads the same `pacingViews` and `gateOpenMs` the projection publishes, so `get_capacity` and the gate
never disagree, and returns per adapter provider id the subscription, the window whose gate opens last, that time
(`until`) and the window's used-share source. A gate-open time not after `now` is not a deferral: at that instant the
pace line has caught up, so the run goes ahead. `serve` passes `capacity.subscriptions` and `capacity.pacing` to the
lifecycle only when the file sets them; without them no pacing figure is read. `RunLifecycle.checkEligibility` sets
`pacingDeferred` on each candidate's facts, which `filterEligible` ignores (not a stage-1 reason). `pacingAdmission` in
`src/routing/eligibility.ts` applies the admission rule inside `buildRouteDecision`: undefined when no admissible
candidate is deferred, so such a decision is built exactly as before; with `require_provider` nothing is narrowed and
the run waits for its own gate; otherwise the deferred candidates are left out while an undeferred one remains, and
when all are deferred a held affine provider stays the choice, else the candidates whose gate opens first remain and
the §17.3 rule breaks the tie. The decision stores the narrowed set as `features_json.admissible`, so version 2
replay recomputes the same selection from the row alone (version 3 decisions since T7.15, §17.7). Each deferred candidate's
`candidates_json` entry gains `pacing` (`verdict: "PACING_DEFERRED"`, `until`, `subscription`, `window`, `source`), and
`reasons_json.pacing` lists them with `hold_until` (null when an undeferred candidate was chosen). `pump()` reads the
deferrals once per dispatch pass while runs are queued, before the breaker check (a held run never becomes the
breaker's probe): a queued run whose provider is deferred stays in `QUEUED`, one `pacing_hold` event (`subscription`,
`window`, `until`, `source`) is appended per run and gate-open time, also across a restart (the run's latest
`pacing_hold` is read on first sight), and the single wake timer arms for `until`. This holds every routed run,
including `privileged` and `require_provider` runs and runs admitted to an undeferred provider whose subscription
became deferred before dispatch. Nothing creates a run, a probe or a provider call. Tests:
`src/lifecycle/pacing-gate.test.ts`, `src/routing/pacing-admission.test.ts`.

## 17. Routing (M7)

Implements FR-RTE-01 (full) to FR-RTE-04, FR-RTE-06 to FR-RTE-08 and FR-MCP-02 (`explain_route`) under
[ADR-0044](../adr/0044-deterministic-route-scoring-and-replayable-decisions.md), which extends
[ADR-0008](../adr/0008-two-stage-routing.md) and [ADR-0032](../adr/0032-provider-model-affinity.md). It details
[§3.2](#32-provider-and-model-affinity-m7), which stays the owner of the affinity table and algorithm; where this
section fills a point §3.2 leaves open it says so. Everything here is **Proposed** (T7.0, 2026-10-14, Delegated). Every
default is an **Assumption**, configurable, and recorded in ROADMAP §7.3 (Q-80 to Q-82). No provider price, model
ranking, quality score or sandbox-strength order is used: none is sourced.

### 17.1 Configuration

```jsonc
{ "routing": {
    "weights": {                 // each 0..100; default 1 (Q-80); all 0 = every candidate scores 0 (configured order)
      "success": 1, "latency": 1, "capacity": 1, "security": 1, "handoff": 1, "cost": 1
    },
    "affinity": {
      "enabled": true,           // §3.2
      "low_water_mark": null,    // 0..1 or null; FR-CAP-02 capacity_score at or below which the affine provider is maxed out
      "min_confidence": null,    // 0..1 or null; lowest estimate confidence at which the low-water mark counts
      "idle_ttl": null           // only null is accepted in M7 (Q-52: no expiry)
    }
} }
```

Unknown keys and type errors abort startup (NFR-SEC-06). The estimate trigger for `capacity_exhausted` is on only when
both `low_water_mark` and `min_confidence` are set (§3.2); the default leaves it off (Q-81), so only positive evidence of
exhaustion switches. A value for `idle_ttl` is refused rather than ignored until expiry is built. Absent section: the
defaults above. *As built (T7.13, Done 2026-10-17):* the schema also accepts a seventh weight
`routing.weights.pacing` (0 to 100, default 1, Q-80; `src/config/schema.ts`, resolved by `resolveRouting` in
`src/config/routing.ts`). Since T7.15 the scorer adds the `pacing` term after the six when a candidate's provider is
in a configured subscription (§17.7); otherwise it sums the six terms above.

### 17.2 Heuristic features (FR-RTE-04)

A pure function of the validated request: objective text, number of constraints, number of verification commands and
whether the profile is writable. No repository metadata is read in M7 (no I/O in routing; Q-82). Output uses the
[§13.6](#136-task-contracts) `route_features` enumeration, each feature stored as `{ "value": ..., "source":
"heuristic" }` under `features_json.route_features` with `"classifier": "heuristic.v1"`:

| Feature | Rule (`heuristic.v1`, Assumptions) |
| --- | --- |
| `context_volume` | objective plus constraints under 400 characters `small`, under 2000 `medium`, else `large` |
| `autonomy` | read-only profile `low`; writable without verification commands `medium`; writable with them `high` |
| `task_kind` | first match, case-insensitive whole words in the objective: `investigation` (investigate, why, explain, analyse, analyze, diagnose), `test` (test, tests), `docs` (doc, docs, readme, documentation), `refactor` (refactor, rename, restructure), `bug_fix` (fix, bug, crash, error, regression), `feature` (add, implement, create, support); else `other` |
| `reasoning_depth` | `high` for `investigation` or `refactor` or `large`; `low` for `docs` or `small` (when not `high`); else `medium` |

The inner agent's `route_features` (FR-INN-12) may replace the values later with `source: "inner"`; the heuristic stays
the fallback. **No feature changes a route in M7**: a feature can only rank providers through a per-provider mapping
(for example "provider X is better at refactors"), and no source gives one (Q-82). Features are recorded for
`explain_route` and for later learning (FR-RTE-05, Deferred).

### 17.3 Score (FR-RTE-02)

Stage 2 runs over the eligible candidates only (BR-1). Each term is a number in [0, 1] where higher is better, computed
per candidate from inputs stored in the decision (§17.4), so replay needs nothing else:

| Term | Value | Source | When unknown |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `success` | `(healthy + 1) / (healthy + counted + 2)` over the provider's runs that started a process within `capacity.window_seconds`, classified as the breaker classifies them (§16.4: counted, healthy, neutral) | Controller ledger | No runs: 0.5 (the formula gives it) |
| `latency` | `min_median / median`, where `median` is the provider's median wall time of `SUCCEEDED` runs in the window and `min_median` the smallest median among the candidates | Controller ledger | No succeeded run: 0.5 |
| `capacity` | the FR-CAP-02 `capacity_score` (§16.2) | M6 estimate | `null`: 0, the worst value, never headroom (Q-69) |
| `security` | 0.5 for every candidate; every candidate already passed the profile's hard filter, and no sandbox-strength order is sourced | none | always 0.5 |
| `handoff` | 1 when the candidate is the provider bound to the meta-session (`continue_task`), else 0; for `delegate_task` every candidate gets 1 | Controller ledger | — |
| `cost` | 0.5 for every candidate; no price is sourced | none | always 0.5 |

`score = Σ(weight_t × term_t) / Σ weight_t`, or 0 when every weight is 0. Numbers are IEEE doubles computed in a fixed
term order and stored unrounded; JSON keeps them exact, so a replay compares with `===`.

**Selection, first match wins:** (1) `require_provider`: the only candidate (BR-2); (2) affinity holds (§3.2 step 4);
(3) `provider_preference` names an eligible candidate (BR-4; a hint, not an affinity reason, §3.2); (4) the highest
score; (5) ties broken by configured candidate order. The stored `selection.basis` is `required_provider`, `affinity`,
`provider_preference`, `score` or `configured_order` (a tie at the top), with the existing suffixes (privileged compile
deferred, continuation mode). With the default weights and no history every candidate ties, so the M2 behaviour
(configured order) is unchanged.

### 17.4 Decision record (FR-RTE-03)

`route_decision` keeps its columns (no migration for it). A decision written by M7 has `schema_version: 2` in
`features_json`; a row without it is an M2 eligibility-only row, which replay and `explain_route` report as such.

```jsonc
// features_json
{ "schema_version": 2, "trust_profile": "...", "client_max_profile": "...", "platform": "linux",
  "required_provider": null, "provider_preference": null, "requirements": { },
  "candidate_order": ["claude", "codex"],                       // configured order after project narrowing
  "route_features": { "classifier": "heuristic.v1", "context_volume": { "value": "small", "source": "heuristic" } },
  "score_inputs": { "claude": { "healthy": 3, "counted": 1, "median_ms": 41000, "capacity_score": null,
                                "capacity_confidence": 0, "session_bound": false } } }
// weights_json: the six weights in force
// candidates_json: [{ "provider", "eligible", "reasons", "score": number|null, "terms": {six terms}|null }]
// reasons_json: { "outcome", "request_reason", "selection": { "provider", "basis" }, "affinity": {§3.2},
//                 "project"?, "continuation"? }
```

**Replay** (`replayRouteDecision`): parse the row, recompute every term and score from `score_inputs` and
`weights_json`, apply the selection rule with the stored `required_provider`, `provider_preference`, `candidate_order`
and `affinity` object, and compare provider, basis prefix and every score with the stored values. It reads no other row,
calls no model and no provider, and reports any difference.

*As built (T7.2, 2026-10-14, Delegated):* `src/routing/decision.ts`. Three points the text above leaves open, each an
**Assumption**: the window counts runs that *finished* in it (the same rows the M6 estimator reads), not runs that
started in it; `reasons_json.selection` gains `rule` (the selection rule, one of the five basis values) beside the
human `basis`, which keeps its M2 to M6 wording for continuations, the quota failover and a required provider
(`provider_preference`), and replay compares `rule`;
`features_json.admissible` lists the providers selection could pick (for `privileged`, the admissible set of FRD V11),
and `score_inputs` holds one entry per admissible provider. A same-provider continuation asks eligibility with P0 as its
required provider, so its rule is `required_provider`. The quota failover (§16.6) now picks among the other eligible
providers by stage 2 instead of the first in configured order.

### 17.5 `explain_route`

Input `{ "run_id": string }` (closed schema). The caller must own the run, exactly as `get_run` checks it; an unknown run
or another client's run is `INVALID_REQUEST` (no existence oracle). Output, from the run's latest `route_decision` only:

```jsonc
{ "run_id": "run_...", "schema_version": 1 | 2, "decided_at": "...", "selected_provider": "claude" | null,
  "selection": { "provider": "...", "basis": "..." } | null,
  "candidates": [{ "provider": "...", "eligible": true, "reasons": [{ "code": "...", "detail": "..." }],
                   "score": 0.5 | null, "terms": { } | null }],
  "features": { } | null, "weights": { } | null, "affinity": { } | null,
  "continuation": { } | null, "project": { "id": "...", "excluded_providers": [] } | null,
  "deciding_factors": ["..."] }                 // short controller strings, e.g. "affinity held (claude)"
```

`deciding_factors` is derived from the stored record by a fixed template (the basis, the affinity outcome and reason,
the top two scores when scored, each excluded provider's primary code); it never contains model text. Reason details are
bounded to 300 characters, as in eligibility errors. A run with no decision (none exists on `main`) returns every field
`null` and `deciding_factors: ["no route decision recorded"]`.

*As built (T7.3, 2026-10-14, Delegated):* `src/routing/explain.ts` and the `explain_route` handler in
`src/mcp/server.ts`. `selection` also carries `rule` (T7.2; `null` on an M2 row); reason details and the basis pass
through the MCP scrubber (secrets, paths outside the allowed roots); `features` is `null` on an M2 row, whose request
facts stay in the ledger; `deciding_factors` lists the rule, the affinity outcome when recorded, the top two scores when
the rule is `score` or `configured_order`, and each excluded provider's first code. A run admitted without a decision
(possible only through the lifecycle API, never through the MCP tools) is explained as none recorded.

### 17.6 Affinity details (fills §3.2)

- **Project key** as in §16.8: the project id, else `ws:` and the SHA-256 of the canonical workspace path.
- **When the row is written.** Only when a run reaches `SUCCEEDED` and its route decision (or, for a retry successor
  without one, its nearest ancestor's through `parent_run_id`) has `affinity.applies: true`. `provider` and `model` come
  from the run (the model the adapter reported, §3.2 step 7), `since` changes only when the provider changes, and
  `last_switch_reason` is the decision's reason. A first run that fails creates no row; the next decision is again a
  first run (scored, reason `null`).
- **Continuations.** A `continue_task` decision is governed by FR-SES-03 and BR-18, not by affinity: its `affinity`
  object has `applies: false` and the row is not moved by it (Q-81, fail closed: only a delegate decision with a reason
  moves a project). The quota failover successor (§16.6) is the exception: it is a `capacity_exhausted` switch with
  `applies: true`.
- **Detecting the reasons** from ledger facts at the routing step, after stage 1, in §3.2's order:
  `user_request` when `require_provider` names a provider other than the affine one, or the row has a pending reset;
  `ineligible` when the affine provider is not an eligible candidate for any reason other than
  `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED_KNOWN` (including leaving the project's `allowed_providers`); `capacity_exhausted` when it is excluded
  with `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED_KNOWN`, or the project's latest terminal run on it failed with `QUOTA_EXHAUSTED`, or the estimate
  trigger fires (§17.1); `failed_after_retry` when the project's latest terminal run on it carries a
  `retry_not_scheduled` event with reason `attempts_exhausted`. Only runs after the row's `last_run_id` count.
- **Where scoring starts after a reason.** For `capacity_exhausted` and `failed_after_retry` the affine provider is left
  out of the score when another candidate remains (otherwise it is kept: fail closed, no refusal for this alone); for
  `user_request` and `ineligible` the candidates are as stage 1 left them.
- **Model (FR-RTE-08).** When affinity holds and the request names no model, the run is admitted with the row's model
  (`request_json.model`, passed to the adapter's `compile`); a `null` model means the adapter default. After a switch
  the new provider runs with its adapter default: the row keeps only the current provider's model.
  `delegate_task` has no model argument, so "an explicit model in the request" cannot occur until one is specified.
  Not built as written: see the T7.5 "As built" note below (the model is remembered, not passed to `compile`; T7.12).
- **Recorded object** (`reasons_json.affinity`): §3.2's fields plus `applies` (boolean) and `excluded_from_score`
  (the affine provider when it was left out). Invariants (TDD §3.13.3): `held` implies a null reason and `scored`
  false; a provider or model different from the row's, when `applies`, implies a non-null reason.
- **Reset.** `switchboard affinity reset <project_key>` sets `reset_requested_at` and `reset_by` (OS user) on the
  existing row in one transaction; no row means nothing to reset (exit non-zero, nothing written). The next routing step
  for the project treats it as `user_request`, records a `routing_affinity_reset` event on that run and clears the two
  columns when the row is next written. `switchboard affinity list [--json]` prints each row.
- **Table columns added to §3.2's DDL** by the M7 migration: `reset_requested_at TEXT`, `reset_by TEXT`, `updated_at
  TEXT NOT NULL`.
- **Privileged.** Affinity never moves a `privileged` meta-session to another provider; a new `privileged` meta-session
  routes like any other, under its own approval (the Q-61 rule for continuations is unchanged).

*As built (T7.4, 2026-10-14, Delegated):* migration 0014 (`src/ledger/migrations/0014_project_affinity.ts`) creates the
table with §3.2's columns and the three above. One deviation, an **Assumption**: `last_run_id` has **no** foreign key,
because retention GC deletes old runs and must not be blocked by a row naming one (the id is informational). Ledger
helpers in `src/ledger/repo.ts`: `getProjectAffinity`, `listProjectAffinities`, `writeProjectAffinity` (insert only when
no row was read; otherwise an update guarded by the version read, which keeps `since` and the last reason when the
provider is unchanged and the reason is null, and clears a pending reset) and `requestAffinityReset` (an existing row
only). `src/config/routing.ts` refuses `low_water_mark` without `min_confidence` or the reverse, so a half-configured
trigger is an error rather than silently off.

*As built (T7.5, 2026-10-14, Delegated):* `src/routing/affinity.ts` (the pure step: `affinityReason`, `planAffinity`,
`affinityRecord`), `collectAffinityFacts` in `src/routing/decision.ts`, the `affinity` argument of the MCP `route`
helper, the quota failover's forced `capacity_exhausted`, and `recordAffinity` at the `SUCCEEDED` transition in
`src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`. Points decided here, each an **Assumption**:

- **The model is remembered, not pinned** (Q-90). At that time
  `run.model` was never set, so the row's `model` was the one the run's
  native session reported (`provider_binding.model`, else `null`). It was **not** passed to `compile`: the Codex adapter
  refused any model (`CAPABILITY_MISSING`), and no adapter declared that a reported model id is accepted by its model
  flag, so pinning could make a held provider ineligible. The provider still held; the model was the adapter default.
  Pinning is T7.12, after the adapters declare it. (Superseded in one respect: the T7.14 fix in §17.7 now records
  `run.model` at admission from `request_json.model`, so the envelope reports the requested/resolved model.)
- "Runs after the row" means runs that finished after the row's `updated_at` (the success write, or a reset), so the
  run that wrote the row never counts and a GC'd `last_run_id` does not matter.
- The `routing_affinity_*` events are appended when the row is written, on the succeeded run: `routing_affinity_held`
  when provider and model are unchanged, otherwise `routing_affinity_switched` (with `from_provider: null` for a first
  run), and `routing_affinity_reset` before either when the write consumed a reset. A reset is therefore consumed only
  by a run that succeeds; until then every decision for the project reads it as `user_request`.
- With affinity disabled the `affinity` object is still recorded (`enabled: false`, `applies: false`), so `explain_route`
  and replay see the same shape.

### 17.7 Model groups (FR-RTE-10, FR-RTE-11)

**Proposed** (2026-10-17, owner decision 57, Delegated mechanism) under
[ADR-0055](../adr/0055-model-groups-and-usage-pacing.md). Defaults are **Assumptions** (Q-98, Q-99).
*As built (T7.13 and T7.14, Done 2026-10-17, `src/config/model-groups.test.ts`,
`src/mcp/routing-request-fields.test.ts`, `src/cli/serve-model-groups.test.ts`):* the **Configuration** subsection
below (`model_groups` schema, startup
refusals, routing-time resolution, the seventh weight `routing.weights.pacing`), the `doctor` listing of each group
with each member's resolution (resolved id, alias, or the stage-1 code it would get; `src/cli/doctor.ts`, informational,
present only when groups are configured), and the **Request** subsection below (`delegate_task` and `enqueue_work`
closed schemas, refusals, mutual exclusion, request carry to admission, and `serve` passing the resolved group
table to both validators through the one `modelGroupsOf` path with adapter-id narrowing via `groupMembersFor`;
`src/cli/serve.ts`, `src/config/load.ts`, `src/config/routing.ts`). The **Candidates**, **Score** (`pacing`
term), **Affinity**, **Decision record, version 3** and **Run fields** subsections below are built by T7.15 (Done
2026-10-17, Delegated; see "As built (T7.15)" at the end of this section). Design for T7.13 to T7.15.

**Configuration** (strict keys, NFR-SEC-06). Placeholders only: neither this document nor code names a model.

```jsonc
{ "model_groups": {
    "<name>": {                                     // ^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]{0,31}$, at most 32 groups
      "members": [                                  // 1..32, ordered; order is the last tie-break
        { "provider": "<config provider id>", "model": "<model id or alias>" }   // `fake` only with the test-only switch
      ] } },
  "routing": { "weights": { "pacing": 1 } } }      // seventh weight, 0..100, default 1 (Q-80)
```

Startup refuses an unknown provider id, a duplicate `{ provider, model }` pair, an empty `members` list and a malformed
name or model. The test-only fake adapter id `fake` is accepted as a member `provider` (and in
`capacity.subscriptions.<name>.providers`, §16.9) only when that adapter is registered (`serve`'s test-only switch,
`loadConfig({ testFakeAdapter })`); otherwise the messages are unchanged. Whether the model resolves is checked at
routing time, not at startup, because the registry's listing changes with each probe (§2.1). `loadConfig` resolves
`model_groups` once into `ResolvedConfig.modelGroups` (`src/config/load.ts`); `serve` passes that table to the MCP
server and to queue admission (`src/cli/serve.ts`), and both validate through `modelGroupsOf` (`src/config/routing.ts`):
the resolved table when present, else the raw `model_groups`, else the file at `configPath`, else none.

**Request** (`routing.model_group`, `routing.model`; §3.0). Validation, before any side effect (FRD V14):

1. Both present: `INVALID_REQUEST`. An unknown group: `INVALID_REQUEST` (the message names the field, not the
   configured groups).
2. `model` without `provider_preference` or `require_provider`: it must resolve in exactly one enabled provider's
   registry (an alias or a configured or listed id; §2.1). None, several, or an unrestricted registry (which would
   accept anything) give `INVALID_REQUEST` with "name a provider for this model".
3. `model_group` with `provider_preference`: the members are narrowed to that provider. Members name config provider
   ids (`claude-code`) while `provider_preference` names adapter ids (`claude`), so each member is mapped to its
   adapter id first (`groupMembersFor` in `src/config/routing.ts`; an id with no mapping compares as is). If none
   remains, the result is `INVALID_REQUEST`. `require_provider` alone names no provider, so it narrows nothing.
4. `continue_task` takes neither field: either one is `INVALID_REQUEST` with the message
   "continue_task accepts neither routing.model_group nor routing.model (Q-99)" and
   `details.field` `"routing"` (returned before `checkRouting` runs, so the closed schema never
   masks it with the generic message; any other unknown routing key keeps the generic
   additional-properties refusal). Continuations stay governed by FR-SES-03 and BR-18 (Q-99).

**Candidates.** Without either field, candidates are providers, as in §17.3. With a group, a candidate is a member,
keyed `<provider>/<model>`. Stage 1 per member: (a) the provider's stage-1 reasons, unchanged; (b) registry resolution
of the model (`CAPABILITY_MISSING` when restricted and unknown); (c) the `minimum_capability` floor against the resolved
model (T7.8); (d) the adapter's compile check with that model (an adapter that accepts no model, such as Codex today,
gives `CAPABILITY_MISSING`). An explicit `model` makes one candidate per named provider, checked the same way.

**Score.** Each eligible member gets its provider's six terms (§17.3), because the ledger facts are per provider, plus
`pacing` from its provider's subscription (§16.9; 0 when it has none or every window is unknown). With
`capacity.pacing.mode: off` the term is 0.5 for every candidate (neutral, as `security` and `cost`), so it cannot decide
a route. Selection, first match wins: `require_provider` (its members only); affinity (below); `provider_preference`
(its members, then score among them); the highest score; member order in the group. A candidate whose subscription is
deferred is handled by the §16.9 admission rule before selection.

The `pacing` term also applies to requests without a group (candidates are then providers). It is included only when
at least one candidate's provider is in a configured subscription; otherwise it is left out of both sums, so the
version 2 score, the M7 tests and every existing decision are unchanged until the operator declares a subscription.

**Affinity** (§3.2 and §17.6 unchanged otherwise). Affinity applies to group requests:

- The affine `<provider>/<model>` is an eligible, undeferred member: affinity holds and the member is chosen, unscored.
- The affine provider is a member but only with another model, or is not a member: `user_request`. The request named a
  group that excludes the affine pair, so the scored selection decides and the row moves on success.
- The affine member is deferred by the pacing gate and an undeferred eligible member remains: `capacity_exhausted`,
  with `detail: "pacing_gate"`, and the affine member is left out of the score. When every eligible member is
  deferred, affinity holds and the run waits for the affine member's gate (§16.9).
- The affine member is ineligible: `ineligible`, as today.
- An explicit `model` different from the row's model is `user_request` (FR-RTE-07 already lists it).

**Decision record, version 3** (`features_json.schema_version: 3`, §17.4 otherwise unchanged). It adds:

```jsonc
{ "model_group": "<name>" | null, "requested_model": "<id>" | null,
  "members": [ { "key": "<provider>/<model>", "provider": "...", "model": "<resolved id>", "order": 0 } ],
  "pacing_inputs": { "<subscription>": { "mode": "gate", "T": 0.95, "B": 0.1, "binding_window": "<id>",
                     "used": 0.42, "used_source": "controller_estimated", "confidence": 0.8,
                     "elapsed": 0.4, "allowance": 1.32, "defers": false, "until": null } },
  "candidate_subscription": { "<provider>": "<subscription>" | null } }
```

`candidates_json` entries gain `model` and `pacing_verdict` (`null` or `PACING_DEFERRED` with `until`). `score_inputs`
stays keyed by provider. Replay recomputes the `pacing` term from `pacing_inputs`, never from the ledger or the clock,
and compares as in §17.4; version 1 and 2 rows replay as before. `explain_route` adds `model_group`, the chosen member,
each excluded member's first code, and the pacing factor (for example "pacing: codex ahead of pace until 14:00Z") from a
fixed template.

**Run fields.** The chosen member's resolved model goes into `request_json.model` and `CompileRequest.model`, as for
T7.12 pinning. The envelope's `provider.model` reports the run's recorded model (`run.model`), `null` when none was
requested (see the As built fix below; it no longer reports whatever the native session reported).

*As built (T7.14 fix, 2026-10-18, Delegated):* `run.model` is recorded at admission from `request_json.model`
(`null` when no model was requested, so the adapter default applies); a `WAITING_CAPACITY` successor keeps the
failed run's model. The envelope's `provider.model` therefore reports the requested/resolved model the run used.
Evidence: `src/mcp/envelope-model.test.ts` (through the SDK client on the fake adapter: `get_run`, `list_runs` and
`explain_route` report the requested model; `null` when none was requested).

*As built (T7.15, 2026-10-17, Delegated):* `src/routing/groups.ts` (member candidates), `src/routing/score.ts` (the
seventh term and member selection), `src/routing/affinity.ts`, `src/routing/decision.ts` (version 3 record, replay,
`collectPacingInputs`), `src/routing/explain.ts`, and the `delegate_task` and `enqueue_work` admission paths
(`src/mcp/server.ts`, `src/queue/admission.ts`). Tests: `src/routing/group-selection.test.ts`, part 1b of
`src/routing/affinity-property.test.ts`, and the byte-identity fixtures `src/routing/fixtures/v2-golden-input.ts` and
`v2-golden.json` (records produced by `main`'s builder before T7.15). Points this section left open, each an
**Assumption**:

- **When version 3 is written.** Only for a request with `model_group` or `model`, or when at least one admissible
  candidate's provider is in a configured subscription (the `pacing` term is then present). Anything else is written as
  version 2, byte for byte as before (the fixtures above).
- **Stage 1 per member** is one eligibility pass with the member's provider required and its model requested, which
  checks (a) to (d) in that order (`collectFacts`). A member's configured provider id maps to its adapter id; members
  whose provider the project does not allow are dropped like providers, and none left is `ELIGIBILITY_NONE` (reason
  `GROUP_MEMBERS`). An explicit `model` is one member: the named provider, or the one provider whose registry resolves it
  (FRD V14). For `privileged`, a member refused only by compile is admissible, as a provider is (FRD V11).
- **Selection with members.** `require_provider` and `provider_preference` select among that provider's members (highest
  score, then member order; the rule stays `required_provider` or `provider_preference`), and a held affine member among
  them stays the choice, so the model never moves without a reason.
- **Affinity.** The affine member is the one with the row's provider and the row's model as its resolved or configured
  id; a row with a `null` model matches no member, so a group request is then `user_request`. The `pacing_gate` rule
  also applies to requests without a group (candidates are providers): otherwise a deferred affine provider would be
  passed over with no reason. A `capacity_exhausted` or `failed_after_retry` reason leaves out only the affine member;
  other members of its provider stay in the score.
- **Pacing inputs.** Taken at routing time from the T6.14 figures (`pacingViews`, binding window per subscription);
  `defers` and `until` there are informational. Admission uses the T6.8 gate verdict on each candidate's facts (T6.8 was
  merged, so no stand-in function was needed). `features_json.pacing_mode` stores the mode at top level so replay knows
  `off` (0.5 for every candidate) even for a candidate without a subscription; `weights_json` gains `pacing` (1 when the
  file leaves it unset) only when the term is present.
- **Record fields.** `features_json` adds `model_group`, `requested_model`, `members` (`key`, `provider`, `model`,
  `configured`, `order`), `pacing_mode`, `pacing_inputs` and `candidate_subscription` (`null` without pacing, `[]` without
  members). A member's `candidates_json` entry has `provider` and `member`; every version 3 entry has `model` and
  `pacing_verdict` (`null` or `{ verdict, until }`). `reasons_json.selection` adds `member` and `model`;
  `reasons_json.affinity` adds `member` (group requests) and `detail: "pacing_gate"`; `reasons_json.pacing.deferred`
  entries add `member`.
- **Run fields.** A member decision does not run the T7.12 remembered-model pin: the member's model is the model.
- **`explain_route`.** Its published output schema lists `schema_version` as 1, 2 or `null`, and T7.15 changes no tool
  schema, so a version 3 row reports 2 (a scored decision) there and its exact version in `features.schema_version`. A
  member candidate is labelled `<provider>/<model>`; `deciding_factors` adds "model group `<name>`: member `<key>`
  chosen" (or "requested model ..."), one "pacing: `<provider>` ahead of pace | on pace | behind pace | pace unknown"
  line per candidate provider in a subscription with " until `<time>`" when deferred, "pacing: off (neutral term)" in
  mode `off`, and ", pacing_gate" on the affinity switch line. Widening the enum to 3 is left to a later schema change.
- **Not changed.** Continuations route over providers (Q-99) and receive the `pacing` term like any provider request; the
  quota failover successor (§16.6) is built by the lifecycle without pacing inputs, so it stays version 2.

## 18. Project queue, schedules and CI triggers (M15, Proposed, T15.0)

Design gate for FR-QUE-01 to 05, [ADR-0050](../adr/0050-ci-status-via-operator-gh-cli.md) (Proposed). Owner decisions 51
and 54. Everything here is **Proposed** and **Delegated** unless an SRS ID or a Proposed ADR fixes it. Implementation
tasks: T15.1 (queue table and admission), T15.2 (schedules), T15.3 (CI trigger with a fake `CiStatusPort` in tests),
T15.4 (queue low-water event, Done), T15.5 (CI-fix cap, Done).

This section is **not** the FR-CAP-04 bounded **run-admission** queue (COMP-RTE ingress while a run is `QUEUED`). M15
adds a durable **project work queue**: pending objectives that become `delegate_task` admissions under the **creating**
client's identity and ceiling.

### 18.1 Configuration (operator defaults)

```jsonc
{ "queue": {                              // Proposed top-level key; exact path in TDD §3.1
    "max_items_per_project": 500,           // proposed target; enqueue over limit -> INVALID_REQUEST
    "max_objective_bytes": 32768,           // matches delegate_task objective max
    "dequeue_batch": 1 },                  // at most one admission per project per scheduler tick
  "ci_trigger": {
    "enabled": false,                      // master default off (FR-QUE-03 scope is per project below)
    "poll_interval_seconds": 120,          // proposed target; 0 means disabled at operator level
    "gh_binary": "gh",                     // resolved on PATH at serve startup; missing -> doctor warning
    "max_consecutive_fixes": 2 } }         // T15.5 (FR-QUE-05) cap per publish ref; overridable per project
```

Per-project CI trigger settings live in `ci_trigger.projects[]` in the operator configuration (`src/config/ci-trigger.ts`;
T15.3 as-built, superseding the ledger-row design in §18.6 below), not in the static project config file, so dynamic projects
can enable them without widening filesystem paths.

### 18.2 Queue work item record (FR-QUE-01)

Ledger migration **0017** (next after 0016; built in T15.1) adds `project_work_item` and `project_work_dependency`.

**IDs.** `que_` plus 26 Crockford base32 characters (same alphabet as `run_`, `evt_`).

```sql
CREATE TABLE project_work_item (           -- migration 0017 (T15.1); mutable only in state, admitted_run_id, terminal_at
  id              TEXT PRIMARY KEY,        -- que_*
  project_id      TEXT NOT NULL,
  creator_client_id TEXT NOT NULL,         -- mcp_clients[].id that created the row; never changes
  state           TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (state IN (
                    'PENDING','READY','ADMITTING','ADMITTED','DONE','CANCELLED','FAILED_ADMISSION')),
  priority        INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,  -- larger runs first among READY items; ties: created_at ASC, then id
  request_json    TEXT NOT NULL,           -- JSON: delegate_task input shape (§3), without wait_seconds
  source_kind     TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (source_kind IN ('mcp','schedule','ci_trigger')),
  source_id       TEXT,                    -- schedule id or ci_trigger_event id when not mcp
  idempotency_key TEXT,                    -- optional; unique per (creator_client_id, idempotency_key) when set
  admitted_run_id TEXT,                    -- set when state becomes ADMITTED; FK to run optional (retention GC)
  last_error_code TEXT,                    -- failure taxonomy code on FAILED_ADMISSION
  created_at      TEXT NOT NULL,
  updated_at      TEXT NOT NULL,
  terminal_at     TEXT
);
CREATE INDEX project_work_item_project_state_prio
  ON project_work_item (project_id, state, priority DESC, created_at, id);
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX project_work_item_creator_idem
  ON project_work_item (creator_client_id, idempotency_key)
  WHERE idempotency_key IS NOT NULL;

CREATE TABLE project_work_dependency (     -- migration 0017; insert-only
  successor_id    TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES project_work_item(id),
  predecessor_id  TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES project_work_item(id),
  PRIMARY KEY (successor_id, predecessor_id)
);
```

**`request_json`.** A JSON object validating as `delegate_task` input (§3) for the item's `project_id`: `objective`,
`trust_profile`, optional `constraints`, `routing`, `verification_commands`, `privileged_scope` when profile is
