`privileged`, and `project` set to the queue item's `project_id` (workspace_path omitted). Values are frozen at enqueue
time. At admission the controller may inject a fresh `idempotency_key` derived from `que_*` so dequeue retries do not
collide with the client's direct `delegate_task` keys.

**States.**

| State | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `PENDING` | Waiting on dependencies (§18.3). |
| `READY` | Dependencies satisfied; eligible for dequeue. |
| `ADMITTING` | Dequeue started; ledger transaction in progress. |
| `ADMITTED` | A `run` row exists; `admitted_run_id` set. |
| `DONE` | Admitted run reached a terminal success state (`SUCCEEDED`). |
| `CANCELLED` | Operator or creator cancelled before or instead of admission. |
| `FAILED_ADMISSION` | Admission refused (ceiling, pause, `QUEUE_FULL`, and so on); not retried automatically unless the operator requeues. |

When the admitted run ends `FAILED` or `CANCELLED`, the item stays `ADMITTED` with `terminal_at` set in the same
transaction (**Delegated**, `closeWorkItemForRun` in `src/ledger/work-queue.ts`, called from `casRunState` in
`src/ledger/repo.ts` on every terminal transition), so it never satisfies a dependency.
A crash after `ADMITTING` leaves the item `ADMITTING`; startup reconciliation (same pass as FR-EXE-06) resets it to
`READY` when no `admitted_run_id` is set.

**Events.** Append `project_work_event` rows (type `enqueued`, `ready`, `admitted`, `done`, `cancelled`,
`admission_failed`) in the same transaction as state changes. Shapes are **Proposed**; counts only in MCP listings.

### 18.3 Priority and dependencies (FR-QUE-01)

**Priority.** Among items in `READY` for one `project_id`, order by `priority` descending, then `created_at` ascending,
then `id` ascending. Priority is an integer in `[-1000, 1000]` (default `0`). It is independent of `delegate_task`'s
`priority` enum (`interactive` / `normal` / `batch`), which is copied into `request_json` when present.

**Dependencies.** Edges are directed: `predecessor_id` must reach `DONE` before `successor_id` leaves `PENDING`. Cycles
are rejected at enqueue with `INVALID_REQUEST`. A predecessor in `CANCELLED` or `FAILED_ADMISSION` blocks the successor
until the operator removes the edge or cancels the successor (fail closed; **Delegated**, `isBlockingPredecessor` in
`src/ledger/work-queue.ts`). When the last blocking predecessor becomes `DONE`, the successor moves `PENDING` →
`READY` in the same transaction as the predecessor's terminal event. Enqueue applies the same blocking rule up front:
an item whose predecessors are all already `DONE` starts `READY`; otherwise it starts `PENDING`
(`src/ledger/work-queue.ts`; enqueue up-front rule from T15.1 fix [#660](https://github.com/zemdregon/switchboard/pull/660),
fail-closed `CANCELLED`-blocks rule from T15.1 fix [#665](https://github.com/zemdregon/switchboard/pull/665)).

**Fairness with FR-CAP-04.** Dequeue still passes through the existing run lifecycle admission path; a full global or
per-provider admission queue returns `QUEUE_FULL` and leaves the work item `READY` for a later tick.

### 18.4 Admission through the creating client's ceiling (FR-QUE-01)

Dequeue is implemented inside COMP-RTE (scheduler tick) calling the same admission pipeline as MCP `delegate_task`, but:

1. **Identity.** `creator_client_id` supplies the MCP client record for authorization, `projects` list,
   `manage_projects` rules, and `effectiveMaxProfile` ([§11.2](#112-resolution-and-ceilings)). The tick does not use the
   operator OS user or a console client's identity. A schedule or CI trigger does not widen the ceiling: only the
   client that created the schedule row or CI template may have created the enqueued item, under that client's entry at
   enqueue time.
2. **Ceiling at admission time.** Steps (1)–(6) of §11.2 run with the **current** configuration. If the creator's
   `max_trust_profile` or the project's ceiling is lower than `request_json.trust_profile`, admission returns
   `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` and the item becomes `FAILED_ADMISSION`. A queued item is **never** admitted under another
   client's ceiling, even if a different client could legally run the same objective today.
3. **Dynamic projects.** Creator-only resolution and approval gates from [ADR-0030](../adr/0030-dynamic-project-revisions-and-approval.md)
   apply unchanged. A widening revision still waits for `projects approve` before admission.
4. **Pause and budgets.** Project pause (FR-CTL-05) and budget caps (FR-CTL-06) are checked like any `delegate_task`; the
   work item stays `READY` when the refusal is retryable (`QUEUE_FULL`, pause), and becomes `FAILED_ADMISSION` when not
   (`PERMISSION_BLOCKED`, `INVALID_REQUEST`).
5. **Privileged.** `privileged` items follow WF-7: `AWAITING_APPROVAL` on the admitted run is allowed; the work item is
   `ADMITTED` when the run row exists, and becomes `DONE` only when that run succeeds.

**MCP tools (T15.1).** `enqueue_work`, `list_work` and `cancel_work`, scoped as `list_runs` (§3.4.2): a `project_id`
must name a project the client may use (unknown → `INVALID_REQUEST`, outside its `projects` list → `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`,
its own dynamic project allowed), and an item is visible only to its `creator_client_id` (another client's `que_*` id
is unknown, `INVALID_REQUEST`). Only the creator may cancel over MCP; the operator uses CLI `switchboard queue cancel`
(FR-OPS-01). Closed schemas live in `src/mcp/schemas.ts`. **Delegated** details:

- `enqueue_work { project_id, request, priority?, depends_on?, idempotency_key? }`. `request` is the closed
  `delegate_task` subset frozen in `request_json` (§18.2): `objective`, `trust_profile`, `constraints`,
  `verification_commands`, `routing`, `priority`, `privileged_scope`. At enqueue the server applies the `delegate_task`
  checks that need no workspace: the ceiling against the lower of the client's and the project's maximum (V5,
  `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`), V13, V7 and V8. Admission re-checks under the configuration of that time (§18.4 step 2).
  `depends_on` (at most 32) names the caller's own items. A project already holding `queue.max_items_per_project` rows
  is `INVALID_REQUEST`. The key is stored as `mcp:<key>` so it cannot collide with schedule or CI keys, and is replayed
  (`replayed: true`) only for the same project, request, priority and `depends_on`.
- `list_work { project_id?, state_in?, limit? }` returns the caller's items ordered as §18.3, at most `limit` (default
  50, at most 200). An item shows `work_item_id`, `project_id`, `state`, `priority`, `trust_profile`, `source_kind`,
  `depends_on`, `admitted_run_id`, `last_error_code` and timestamps, never the objective or the rest of `request_json`.
- `cancel_work { work_item_id }` moves a `PENDING` or `READY` item to `CANCELLED` and returns any other item unchanged.
  `ADMITTING` is not cancelled, so a run never sits behind a `CANCELLED` item; an admitted item is stopped with
  `cancel_run`.
- Fail closed: the three tools are listed right after the base tools. They are offered over stdio, and since
  T15.9 (FR-QUE-08, §20.8) also over the network transport, but only to an `orchestrator` client under the same scope
  check as `delegate_task` over HTTP; a `console` client neither lists nor calls them over either transport (answered
  exactly like an unknown tool, FR-CTL-11). Ownership is by the authenticated client id, and admission stays under the
  creating client's ceiling.

### 18.5 Schedules (FR-QUE-02)

```sql
CREATE TABLE project_schedule (            -- next migration after 0018 (T15.2; 0018 is T14.6, §3.4.7); enabled default false
  id              TEXT PRIMARY KEY,        -- sch_*
  project_id      TEXT NOT NULL,
  creator_client_id TEXT NOT NULL,
  cron_expr       TEXT NOT NULL,           -- five fields, UTC; see below
  enabled         INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (enabled IN (0, 1)),
  template_json   TEXT NOT NULL,           -- delegate_task-shaped template; objective may contain {{schedule_fire_at}}
  last_fire_at    TEXT,                    -- ISO-8601; dedup anchor
  created_at      TEXT NOT NULL,
  disabled_at     TEXT
);
```

**Cron syntax.** Five fields: `minute hour day-of-month month day-of-week`, UTC only, subset of Vixie cron:

- Minute `0-59`, hour `0-23`, day-of-month `1-31`, month `1-12`, weekday `0-7` (`0` and `7` = Sunday).
- `*`, `,`, `-`, `/` with the usual meanings; no named months or `@yearly` aliases.
- Invalid expressions are rejected at create time (`INVALID_REQUEST`).

**Behaviour.** Schedules are **off by default** (`enabled = 0`). On each scheduler tick (injected clock in tests), for
each enabled schedule whose expression matches the current minute and whose `last_fire_at` is not already this minute,
the controller inserts one `project_work_item` with `source_kind = 'schedule'`, `source_id = sch_*`, and
`template_json` copied into `request_json` after substituting `{{schedule_fire_at}}` with the tick timestamp. No second
item for the same schedule in the same minute after restart (dedup uses `last_fire_at` updated in the same transaction as
enqueue). **Operator CLI (T15.2):** `switchboard schedule list|disable <sch_id>`; disable sets `enabled = 0` and
`disabled_at` and stops future fires.

**Widening schedules.** A schedule created by a chat client that would enqueue a `privileged` or wider profile than the
creator's current ceiling waits for operator approval (**Proposed**, discovery §8): row state `PENDING_APPROVAL` on the
schedule until `switchboard schedule approve` (T15.2).

### 18.6 CI failure trigger (FR-QUE-03, ADR-0050)

**Published branch.** A branch "the project published" is a `refs/heads/*` publish ref ([§12.3](#123-commit-algorithm-and-refs))
that COMP-GIT moved on a `git_push` event for a run whose `policy_snapshot_json` names the same `project_id`, and whose
push succeeded. Other branches are out of scope for FR-QUE-03.

```sql
CREATE TABLE project_ci_trigger (          -- next migration after 0018 (T15.3; 0018 is T14.6, §3.4.7); per project, off by default
  project_id      TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
  enabled         INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (enabled IN (0, 1)),
  creator_client_id TEXT NOT NULL,         -- client that enabled the trigger
  template_json   TEXT NOT NULL,           -- delegate_task-shaped template for fix-up work
  created_at      TEXT NOT NULL
);

CREATE TABLE ci_trigger_event (            -- dedup and audit; text fields untrusted
  id              TEXT PRIMARY KEY,        -- cie_*
  project_id      TEXT NOT NULL,
  publish_ref     TEXT NOT NULL,           -- refs/heads/...
  external_id     TEXT NOT NULL,           -- stable id from gh JSON (check run or workflow run id)
  conclusion      TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (conclusion IN ('failure','cancelled','timed_out')),
  untrusted_summary TEXT,                  -- optional; max 500 chars; label untrusted_external_text in storage metadata
  observed_at     TEXT NOT NULL,
  work_item_id    TEXT,                    -- que_* when enqueue succeeded
  UNIQUE (project_id, external_id)
);
```

**`CiStatusPort` (Proposed).** Production implementation runs allowlisted `gh` argv arrays with `--json` and a closed field
list, for example:

- `gh api repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/{sha}/check-runs --jq '...'` only after resolving `owner/repo` from the project's
  remote via a read-only `git` call (no network credential in Switchboard).
- Or `gh run list` / `gh run view` with `--json databaseId,conclusion,headBranch,status` for workflow conclusions on the
  published branch.

Exact argv templates are fixed in implementation (T15.3) and audited like provider argv ([ADR-0037](../adr/0037-argv-audit-short-flags-and-bypass-values.md));
no `gh auth login`, no arbitrary `gh api` paths, no shell interpolation of branch names (pass argv array). Switchboard never
sets `GH_TOKEN` or `GITHUB_TOKEN` for these calls ([ADR-0050](../adr/0050-ci-status-via-operator-gh-cli.md)).

**Trigger logic.**

1. On each poll interval (§18.1, per-project `enabled`), for each published ref of the project, call `CiStatusPort` for
   the tip commit on that ref.
2. When the latest relevant check or run has conclusion `failure`, and no `ci_trigger_event`
   row exists for `(project_id, external_id)`, insert the event row and enqueue one work item from `template_json` with
   `source_kind = 'ci_trigger'`. *Amended by T15.6 (FR-QUE-06; before it this step also enqueued for
   `cancelled` and `timed_out`):* `cancelled` and `timed_out` are `CI_INFRA` and
   enqueue nothing ([§20.1](#201-ci-transient-handling-fr-que-06-t156)).
3. Optional `untrusted_summary` (from an operator-forwarded notification) is stored length-capped; it is **not**
   passed to `delegate_task` objective unless the operator template explicitly references a controller substitution token
   `{{ci_untrusted_summary}}`, which is wrapped in the same `untrusted_text` label pattern as §3.4.1 and never parsed for
   instructions.

**Tests (T15.3).** Inject `CiStatusPort` returning synthetic conclusions; never call real `gh` in CI.

**As built (T15.3, 2026-10-16, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved).** `src/ci/`: `port.ts` (`CiStatusPort`,
`CiStatusError` reasons `AUTH_REQUIRED`, `GH_FAILED`, `GH_MISSING`, `PARSE_FAILED`, `TIMEOUT`), `gh-argv.ts` (the closed
argv templates G1 `run list`, G2 `run view`, G3 `api …/check-runs` with a fixed `--jq`, an exact-match audit every builder
runs, the closed JSON field allowlists and the `gh` environment allowlist, which never carries `GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`,
`GH_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_ENTERPRISE_TOKEN`, `GH_HOST` or `GH_REPO`), `gh-port.ts` (`GhCiStatusPort`: `execFile`
without a shell, stderr dropped, `gh` exit code 4 read as `AUTH_REQUIRED` per `gh help exit-codes`), `published-refs.ts`
(the published-ref allowlist from `ACCEPTED` `git_push` rows of the project, tip = latest accepted pushed SHA; `owner/repo`
from a read-only `git config --get remote.<name>.url`, github.com only), `untrusted.ts` and `trigger.ts`
(`pollCiTriggers`, `CiTriggerPoller`), wired as scheduler step 2 in `src/cli/serve.ts` before the T15.1 dequeue. Three
Delegated deviations from the Proposed design above, each to avoid a migration while T15.2 owns the next number:

1. **No `project_ci_trigger` table.** The per-project trigger is operator configuration, `ci_trigger.projects[]`
   (`project`, `client`, `enabled`, `max_consecutive_fixes`, `rerun_failed`, `cancel_stale_queued`,
   `template` with `objective`, `trust_profile` other than
   `privileged`, `constraints`; `src/config/ci-trigger.ts`); `load.ts` refuses an unknown client, an unknown project
   or a client that does not list the project. The `client` is the `creator_client_id`. A chat client cannot enable
   a trigger in v1. T15.5 adds an optional top-level and per-project `max_consecutive_fixes` (Assumption default 2;
   `DEFAULT_MAX_CONSECUTIVE_FIXES`). T15.6 adds the per-project `rerun_failed` and `cancel_stale_queued` switches
   (FR-QUE-06; both default `false`; §20.1).
2. **No `ci_trigger_event` table (T15.3 as-built; superseded by T15.5 below).** At T15.3, dedup was the work item
   itself: `source_kind = 'ci_trigger'`, `source_id = ci:<external_id>`, idempotency key
   `ci_trigger:<project_id>:<external_id>`, checked per `(project_id, source_id)` and written in the one enqueue
   transaction, so a crash leaves no half-written event to reconcile. T15.5 (migration **0023**) adds the
   `ci_trigger_event` table for observations and audit alongside the work-item dedup, which is kept.
3. **`untrusted_summary` is not stored on its own.** An operator-forwarded summary (injected source; no operator ingress
   CLI in v1) is NFC-normalised, stripped of control characters, redacted with the artifact redactor, capped at 500
   characters, and reaches storage only inside the objective, as a block labelled `untrusted_external_text`, when the
   template holds `{{ci_untrusted_summary}}`. Templates may also use the controller facts `{{ci_publish_ref}}`,
   `{{ci_head_sha}}`, `{{ci_conclusion}}` and `{{ci_external_id}}`; substitution is one pass.

G2 confirms the G1 run's terminal conclusion; G1's run must be on the asked branch (else `PARSE_FAILED`) and on the
published tip SHA (else nothing applies). The G3 jq filter emits one object per completed check run (REST field `id`)
because `--paginate` applies `--jq` per page; the latest by `completed_at` is chosen in code. Poll errors are reason codes
on `CiTriggerPoller.lastErrors` and in the `serve` log; a `doctor` check is not built. Tests: `src/ci/ci-trigger.test.ts`
(matrix C1–C18 on a fake port, a fake `gh` runner and a fake `gh` script).

**CI-fix cap (T15.5, FR-QUE-05; Delegated, not owner-approved).** Consecutive fix-up work items for a published ref are
capped by `ci_trigger.max_consecutive_fixes` (Assumption default 2). Forward ledger migration **0023**
(`src/ledger/migrations/0023_ci_fix_cap.ts`) adds `ci_trigger_event` (ids `cte_*`; `id`, `project_id`, `publish_ref`, `external_id`,
`conclusion`, `untrusted_summary`, `observed_at`, `work_item_id`) and `ci_trigger_ref_state` (`project_id`, `publish_ref`,
`consecutive_fixes`, `stuck`, `last_external_id`, `last_conclusion`, `reset_at`, `updated_at`). When `consecutive_fixes`
reaches the cap, further failures on that ref are recorded in `ci_trigger_event` with `work_item_id = NULL` without enqueuing
work, and `ci_trigger_ref_state.stuck` is set to 1, raising the `ci_fix_stuck` needs-attention flag (FR-CTL-03; §3.4.5).
A passing conclusion or an operator reset (`resetCiFixCounter`) resets `consecutive_fixes` and `stuck` to 0.

### 18.7 Scheduler integration (COMP-RTE)

One **Proposed** scheduler loop in `serve` (after retrieval build, before or after admission dequeue):

1. Fire due schedules (§18.5).
2. Poll CI triggers when operator and project enabled (§18.6).
3. For each project with `READY` items, dequeue up to `queue.dequeue_batch` items through §18.4.

The loop respects project pause and holds when FR-CAP-04 admission queue is full. It does not run inside an MCP tool
handler.

### 18.8 Queue low-water event (FR-QUE-04, T15.4)

A project may configure a low-water mark for its work queue (`projects.<id>.queue.low_water`, integer ≥ 1; off by
default). The admissible count is the number of work items of that project in state `READY`, or in `PENDING` whose
dependencies can still be met (none of their transitive predecessors in `project_work_dependency` is in `CANCELLED`
or `FAILED_ADMISSION`, or is an `ADMITTED` item whose run already ended `FAILED` or `CANCELLED` and so never becomes `DONE`).

**Hysteresis and re-arming.**

- When the admissible count drops below the configured mark, the controller emits exactly one `queue_low` sink event
  (FR-CTL-10, [ADR-0048](../adr/0048-notification-sinks.md)).
- The detector is re-armed only when the admissible count rises to or above the mark again.
- An unset mark emits nothing.

**Closed payload.** The event payload keeps the closed shape of ADR-0048:
`{ project_id, event_kind: "queue_low", reason_code: "queue_low", run_id: null, link }`.

**As built (T15.4, Delegated, not owner-approved).** The detector attaches to the ledger through `attachQueueLowWater`
(`setQueueChangeListener` in `src/ledger/work-queue.ts`); emission is demonstrated in tests directly with a capturing
emit (`src/queue/low-water.test.ts`) and through the sink dispatcher with a capturing sink
(`src/mcp/work-queue.test.ts`). `serve` attaches one tracker per daemon process on the real ledger and the sink
dispatcher when notification sinks exist, seeding the armed state from the level at startup (`src/cli/serve.ts`;
end-to-end serve test `src/cli/serve-queue-low-water.test.ts`, [PR #678](https://github.com/zemdregon/switchboard/pull/678)).
The MCP server attaches no tracker, so only a `serve` run emits `queue_low` in production.

## 19. Switchboard-controlled skills (M16, Proposed)

*Proposed (2026-10-17, owner decision 58, [ADR-0056](../adr/0056-switchboard-controlled-skills.md); built: the
store, migration `0021` and state derivation (T16.1), the configuration keys and loader (T16.3), the operator CLI
(T16.2), the MCP tools with the §19.5 steps 2 to 4 resolver (T16.9), admission (T16.4), prompt-prefix delivery
(T16.5), the native-delivery framework with its exclusions and cleanup (T16.6), adapter declarations
(T16.7 Done 2026-10-17, Delegated, not owner-approved), continuity across handoff and resume (T16.10: the bundle
`skills` facts and re-delivery in the receiving provider's way), the malicious-fixture leg (T16.11) and the M16
acceptance test on fakes (T16.12), all Delegated, not owner-approved;
per-provider native (T16.8) Done 2026-10-19, decision 70: Claude Code `NOT_LOADED`, Grok Build, Cursor and Muse Code
`NOT_CHECKED`, no native cells).
Delegated mechanism, not owner-approved beyond the direction. The four limit defaults are Assumptions and their ranges
are Delegated ([§19.3](#193-configuration-fr-skl-03-fr-skl-04-fr-skl-08)).* Requirements: FR-SKL-01 to
FR-SKL-10. Business rules: [FRD](../requirements/FRD.md#3-business-rules) BR-52 to BR-56, V15, C8, WF-16. Tasks:
ROADMAP T16.0 to T16.12. Per-provider delivery: [TDD §3.16](TDD.md#316-switchboard-controlled-skills-proposed).

### 19.1 Skill source format (FR-SKL-01, FR-SKL-02)

A skill source is one UTF-8 file. It starts with a front matter block between two `---` lines, then a Markdown body.

```markdown
---
name: security-review
description: Review the change for injection, authz and secret handling before it is published.
applies_to_providers: [claude-code, codex]   # optional; absent means every provider
---
# Security review
...
```

| Field | Rule |
| --- | --- |
| `name` | Required; `^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,47}$`; must equal the CLI `<name>` argument |
| `description` | Required; 1 to 280 characters, one line |
| `applies_to_providers` | Optional list of config provider ids; a skill that does not apply to the run's provider is skipped for that run with `skill_resolved.skipped = "provider"` (not an error) |
| any other key | Refused (`SKILL_FRONT_MATTER_KEY`); this includes provider keys such as `allowed-tools`, `hooks`, `mcp`, `model` |
| body | Required; 1 byte to `skills.max_body_bytes` (default 32768) after canonicalisation |

Canonicalisation before hashing: strip a UTF-8 BOM, convert CRLF and CR to LF, strip trailing whitespace on each line,
end with exactly one LF. The version's `sha256` is over the canonical front matter (re-serialised in the order above)
plus the canonical body, so the same text always gives the same hash.

Refusals at `add` (nothing is stored; exit code 65 and one reason):

| Reason | Check |
| --- | --- |
| `SKILL_ENCODING` | Not valid UTF-8, a NUL byte, or a control character other than tab and LF |
| `SKILL_SIZE` | Body or description over its limit |
| `SKILL_FRONT_MATTER_KEY` | A key outside the closed set, or a missing required key |
| `SKILL_MARKER` | The body contains `[operator skills:`, `[end of operator skills]`, `[skill <name>@<n>`, `[untrusted data:`, `[end of untrusted data]` or `[instruction from the orchestrator]` (case-insensitive) |
| `SKILL_SECRET` | A match of the ADR-0042 secret patterns or `git.sanitize_patterns` secret classes; the match is not echoed |
| `SKILL_REPOSITORY_SOURCE` | The source path resolves (after symlinks) inside a configured project context root or an `allowed_roots` entry, without `--from-repository` |
| `SKILL_UNCHANGED` | The canonical hash equals the newest version's; no new version is added (exit 0, message only) |
| `SKILL_LIMIT` | Adding the name would exceed `skills.max_names` (default 64) |

### 19.2 Store and ledger tables (FR-SKL-01, FR-SKL-07, FR-SKL-10)

Content lives in `<data_dir>/skills/<sha256>.md` (0600, directory 0700), written to a temporary file, `fsync`ed and
renamed; it is never edited or deleted while a `skill_version` row names it. One forward-only migration (next free
number at implementation; 0021 on 2026-10-17) adds:

```sql
CREATE TABLE skill_version (               -- append-only
  name        TEXT NOT NULL,
  version     INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (version >= 1),
  sha256      TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (length(sha256) = 64),
  size_bytes  INTEGER NOT NULL,
  description TEXT NOT NULL,
  applies_to  TEXT,                        -- JSON array of provider ids, or NULL
  origin      TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (origin IN ('operator_file','repository_path')),
  created_at  TEXT NOT NULL,
  PRIMARY KEY (name, version)
);
CREATE TABLE skill_event (                 -- append-only operator audit
  id          INTEGER PRIMARY KEY,
  name        TEXT NOT NULL,
  version     INTEGER,                     -- NULL for name-level events (retire)
  kind        TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (kind IN ('added','approved','revoked','retired','unretired')),
  actor       TEXT NOT NULL,               -- 'operator_cli'
  note        TEXT,                        -- optional, at most 280 characters, operator text
  at          TEXT NOT NULL
);
CREATE TABLE run_skill (
  run_id        TEXT NOT NULL REFERENCES run(id),
  position      INTEGER NOT NULL,          -- order in the resolved set
  name          TEXT NOT NULL,
  version       INTEGER NOT NULL,
  sha256        TEXT NOT NULL,
  source        TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (source IN ('global_default','global_role','project_default','project_role',
                                                'request','continued')),
  delivery      TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (delivery IN ('prompt_prefix','native','skipped')),
  native_path   TEXT,                      -- worktree-relative, only for 'native'
  written_sha256 TEXT,                     -- hash of the bytes written, only for 'native'
  delivered_at  TEXT,
  removed_at    TEXT,
  removal       TEXT CHECK (removal IN ('verified','failed','not_needed')),
  PRIMARY KEY (run_id, position)
);
```

A version's state is derived from its newest `approved`/`revoked` event (`draft` when there is none) and the name's
newest `retired`/`unretired` event. A retired name resolves to nothing for new runs; runs already admitted keep their
pinned version.

*Implementation (T16.1, 2026-10-17, Delegated, not owner-approved):* `src/skills/` and migration 0021. Append-only is
enforced by `BEFORE UPDATE` and `BEFORE DELETE` triggers on `skill_version` and `skill_event`, as for `run_event`;
`run_skill` is not append-only because T16.6 fills in its delivery and removal columns. `size_bytes` is the byte length
of the stored canonical file (front matter plus body), the bytes `sha256` covers. `skills.max_names` counts names that
are not retired. LLD §19.1 cites `git.sanitize_patterns` "secret classes", but that key has no classes; every
configured pattern is treated as a secret pattern at `add` (fail closed), and the description is also checked for
markers.

### 19.3 Configuration (FR-SKL-03, FR-SKL-04, FR-SKL-08)

| Key | Meaning |
| --- | --- |
| `skills.enabled` | Boolean, default `false`. When false, the `skills` and `role` request fields are refused as `INVALID_REQUEST` with `details.reason = "SKILLS_DISABLED"`, the tools are not offered, and nothing else changes |
| `skills.defaults[]` | Names (`name` or `name@n`) applied to every run |
| `skills.roles.<role>[]` | Names applied when the request's `role` is `<role>`; `<role>` matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]{0,31}$` |
| `skills.delivery` | `native_preferred` (default) or `prefix_only` |
| `skills.allow_draft_in_trusted` | Boolean, default `false` (Q-105) |
| `skills.max_body_bytes`, `skills.max_per_run`, `skills.max_run_bytes`, `skills.max_names` | Defaults 32768, 8, 65536, 64 (Assumptions). Ranges (Delegated, T16.3, 2026-10-17, not owner-approved): integers 1 to 1048576, 1 to 64, 1 to 16777216 and 1 to 4096. Resolved `max_run_bytes` must be at least resolved `max_body_bytes`. A role map has at most 128 roles; each name list has at most 4096 entries |
| `projects[].skills.defaults[]`, `projects[].skills.roles.<role>[]` | Project-level defaults, applied after the global ones. Same name and role patterns, and the same list and role-map caps, as the global keys |
| `mcp_clients[].skills` | Optional array of bare names (no `@n`; Delegated reading of "array of names", T16.3) the client may see and inject; absent means every approved name; an empty array is an allow-list of nothing |

Names in configuration are schema-checked at `serve` start (unknown keys and name patterns abort startup) and
store-checked by `doctor` (T16.2): an unknown or unapproved name is a warning, not a
startup failure (the CLI adds skills after the config exists). At admission an unresolvable configured default is
skipped with a `skill_resolved` event (`skipped = "unresolved_default"`); an unresolvable *requested* name is an error
(§19.5).

*Ranges (T16.3, 2026-10-17, Delegated, not owner-approved).* The four limits are integers in the ranges in the table
above, and resolved `max_run_bytes` must be at least resolved `max_body_bytes` (one body cannot exceed the run total).
T16.3 does not warn at startup. `unresolvedConfiguredSkills` (`src/skills/configured.ts`) lists configured names that
do not resolve, for the T16.2 `doctor` line, and is not called while loading config. `skills.allow_draft_in_trusted`
stays the Q-105 Delegated default `false`.

### 19.4 Request fields (FR-SKL-03)

Added to `delegate_task`, `continue_task` and `enqueue_work` (all `additionalProperties: false`, so this is a schema
change):

```jsonc
"skills": { "type": "array", "maxItems": 8, "uniqueItems": true,
            "items": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^[a-z][a-z0-9-]{1,47}(@[1-9][0-9]{0,5})?$" } },
"role": { "type": "string", "pattern": "^[a-z][a-z0-9_-]{0,31}$" },
"skill_defaults": { "enum": ["apply", "omit"] }
```

The fields are part of `request_hash` (idempotency, FR-MCP-08). `skill_defaults` carries no schema `default`:
an absent field stays absent (so step 1 tells "no fields" from "apply", C8) and means `apply` at resolution.
A queued item stores them and resolves at its own
admission, not at enqueue time.

### 19.5 Resolution at admission (FR-SKL-03, FR-SKL-04)

Inside the admission transaction, after profile and ceiling checks and before the `run` row is inserted:

1. If `continue_task` has no `skills`, `role` or `skill_defaults`, copy the previous run's `run_skill` rows (same name,
   version, hash) with `source = 'continued'`, re-check step 4 for the new run's profile, and go to step 6.
2. Build the candidate list in this order: `skills.defaults`, `skills.roles.<role>`, `projects[].skills.defaults`,
   `projects[].skills.roles.<role>` (all four only when `skill_defaults` is `apply`), then the request's `skills`.
   An unknown `role` with no configured entry is not an error; it selects nothing.
3. De-duplicate by name: the first occurrence keeps its position; an explicit `@n` anywhere wins over an unpinned
   entry. Resolve each unpinned name to its newest `approved` version (or newest version at all when step 4 allows a
   draft).
4. Gate each entry:
   - requested name unknown, retired or with no usable version: `INVALID_REQUEST`, `details.reason = "SKILL_UNKNOWN"`;
   - name outside `mcp_clients[].skills`: `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`, `details.reason = "SKILL_NOT_PERMITTED"`;
   - version not `approved` and (profile is `inspect-untrusted`, `edit-untrusted` or `test-sandboxed`, or
     `skills.allow_draft_in_trusted` is false): `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`, `details.reason = "SKILL_NOT_APPROVED"`;
   - `origin = 'repository_path'` under one of the three untrusted profiles: `PERMISSION_BLOCKED`,
     `details.reason = "SKILL_REPOSITORY_ORIGIN"`.
   A configured default that fails a gate is skipped with an event instead; only requested names fail the request.
5. Apply the caps: more than `skills.max_per_run` entries, or more than `skills.max_run_bytes` of bodies, is
   `INVALID_REQUEST`, `details.reason = "SKILL_RUN_LIMIT"`.
6. Insert one `run_skill` row per entry (`delivery` is filled at dispatch, §19.6) and one `skill_resolved` run event
   listing names, versions, hashes, sources and any skipped defaults.

Skills are resolved once per run. They are not read by eligibility, scoring, affinity, the profile compiler, approval
or any other policy component (import-boundary test, as for `src/retrieval/**`).

*Implementation (T16.4, 2026-10-17, Delegated, not owner-approved):* `src/skills/admission.ts`. Steps 2 to 4 are the
T16.9 `resolveSkills`; `resolveRunSkills` adds steps 1 and 5 and is pure. The lifecycle calls it through a structurally
typed `SkillAdmissionPort` (it does not import `src/skills/`, because `src/capacity/` reaches the lifecycle), after the
meta-session and privileged checks and before the `run` row; a refusal rolls the transaction back. Delegated readings:
`skill_defaults` has no schema `default`, so an absent field stays absent for step 1 and means `apply`; the fields are
stored in `request_json` when given and are part of `request_hash`. A step 1 entry that fails the re-check (or whose
stored hash changed) is skipped with an event, not refused, as it was not requested by this request. The byte cap sums
`skill_version.size_bytes` (front matter and body, so at least the body). `run_skill.delivery` is NOT NULL, so
admission writes `prompt_prefix` with `delivered_at` NULL; `get_run` returns `skills` entries
`{name, version, sha256, source, delivery}` with `delivery` null until dispatch sets `delivered_at`. A run with no
entry and no skipped default gets no `skill_resolved` event. The import-boundary test exempts `src/skills/limits.ts`,
the constants-only leaf `src/config/` imports (T16.3).

### 19.6 Delivery at dispatch (FR-SKL-05)

The adapter contract (§2) gains one declaration per compiled cell, `skillDelivery(cell): "prompt_prefix" |
{ native: NativeSkillLocation }`, where a `NativeSkillLocation` gives a worktree-relative directory template
(`<dir>/switchboard-<name>/<file>` or `<dir>/switchboard-<name>.<ext>`) and a pure renderer from the canonical skill to
the bytes the provider expects. The default for every adapter and cell is `prompt_prefix`. A declaration may name
`native` only together with the evidence id of the T16.8 live check that confirmed it; the conformance suite fails a
native declaration without one.

Per run, after the worktree and lease exist and before the provider is spawned:

1. If `skills.delivery` is `prefix_only`, or the cell declares `prompt_prefix`, mark every entry `prompt_prefix`.
2. Otherwise, per entry: compute the path; refuse native (fall back to `prompt_prefix`, event reason `path_taken`) if
   the path or any parent exists in the base checkpoint, is tracked, is matched by the repository's ignore rules, is a
   symlink, or resolves outside the worktree. Create parents with mode 0700 and the file with 0600, exclusively
   (`O_CREAT|O_EXCL|O_NOFOLLOW`), write, `fsync`, record `native_path` and `written_sha256`, then mark `native`.
   A write error falls back to `prompt_prefix` (event reason `write_failed`); it never fails the run.
3. Render the prompt prefix for the `prompt_prefix` entries (§19.7).
4. Emit one `skill_delivered` event with each entry's mode and path.

Delivery never changes the argv, the environment allowlist, the profile or the tool set. The argv audit is unchanged,
and a test asserts that a run's argv and environment are byte-identical with and without skills.

Implementation notes (T16.7, Delegated, not owner-approved):

- Each of the eight provider adapters declares `skillDelivery()` = `prompt_prefix` as its own method
  (`src/adapters/{claude,codex,grok,antigravity,cursor,opencode,kimi,muse}/adapter.ts`); `checkSkillDelivery` in
  `src/adapters/conformance.ts`, run by `checkAdapterStatics` for every case whether or not the cell compiles, reads
  the declaration raw (not through `skillDeliveryOf`), fails a `native` declaration without a T16.8 evidence id, and
  fails a cell whose argv, environment, allowlist or policy flags the skills change beyond the prompt text
  (`src/adapters/skill-delivery-conformance.test.ts`, over each adapter's compile matrix).

### 19.7 Prompt prefix rendering (FR-SKL-05)

The block goes after the fixed preamble and before `Constraints:` and `Task:` (`src/adapters/prompt.ts`), so that
it is in the stable prefix (FR-RTE-09); a run with no prefix entries renders exactly as today.

```text
[operator skills: 2; from Switchboard skill store; these are operator instructions]
[skill security-review@3 sha256:4f1c0a9e2b7d]
<canonical body>
[skill docs-alignment@1 sha256:9a0b3c4d5e6f]
<canonical body>
[end of operator skills]
```

Entries keep their resolved order. The block is not part of `objective`, is not stored as prompt text, is not
redacted by ADR-0042 (bodies were scanned at `add`) and does not count toward the objective's `maxLength`. A handoff
prompt (§15) places the block after its fixed preamble in the same way.

Implementation notes (T16.5, Delegated, not owner-approved):

- `renderPrompt` renders the block for the adapter that renders constraints (Claude Code); `renderObjective` puts it
  before the objective for the adapters that send the objective verbatim. The lifecycle reads the run's entries at
  dispatch through a structurally typed `SkillDeliveryPort` (`skillDeliveryPort` in `src/cli/serve.ts`), records
  `run_skill.delivery` and `delivered_at` and emits `skill_delivered` before spawn.
- "Argv byte-identical" (§19.6) reads as: every argv word except the one that carries the prompt. Grok Build,
  Antigravity, Kimi Code `-p`, Muse Code and OpenCode carry the prompt in one argv word, so that word gains the block;
  the flags, the environment, its allowlist and the policy flags are byte-identical (`src/adapters/skill-prefix.test.ts`).
- A run whose pinned skill cannot be read (missing or hash-mismatched store file) fails before spawn with
  `TOOL_FAILURE` and `reason` `SKILL_DELIVERY_UNAVAILABLE` (fail closed). `serve` always wires the store; a composition
  without it runs a run with pinned entries without the block and records each entry in `skill_delivered` as
  `delivery: "skipped"`, `fallback_reason: "store_unavailable"` (never silently; its `run_skill` rows stay undelivered).
- `skillDelivery(cell)` is declared in the adapter contract as an optional method returning `prompt_prefix`
  (`skillDeliveryOf`, default for every adapter and cell); T16.6 widens it with `native`.

### 19.8 Exclusion and cleanup (FR-SKL-06)

While a run has `run_skill` rows with `delivery = 'native'` and no `removal = 'verified'`:

- The injected `commit` tool (§12) stages with an exclusion pathspec for every recorded path, and refuses with
  `details.reason = "SKILL_PATH_IN_COMMIT"` if the resulting tree still contains one.
- Checkpoint commits (§5), the `diff` and `patch` artifacts, `changed_files` and the handoff bundle's diff summary
  exclude the recorded paths.
- `push` (§12) and `switchboard git push` refuse with `details.reason = "SKILL_PATH_PRESENT"` while a recorded path
  exists in the worktree.

Removal points, each idempotent: (1) when the provider process group has exited, before `VERIFYING`; (2) on entry to
any terminal state; (3) in startup reconciliation for a run found non-terminal (FR-EXE-06); (4) in the retention GC
pass before the worktree is removed. Each removal hashes the file first (`skill_tampered` event if it differs from
`written_sha256`), unlinks it, removes now-empty `switchboard-` directories it created (never another directory), and
verifies with `lstat`. A failed unlink or verify sets `removal = 'failed'`, emits `skill_cleanup_failed` and derives
`needs_attention` with the reason `skill_cleanup_failed` (FR-CTL-03 amended by T16.6); the next removal point retries.

Implementation notes (T16.6, Delegated, not owner-approved):

- **Files.** TDD §3.16 puts the writer and cleaner in the workspace manager; they live in
  `src/workspace/native-skills.ts`, called through `WorkspaceManager` (`writeNativeSkills`, `markNativeDelivered`,
  `removeNativeSkills`), and directly by `src/git/commit.ts`, `src/git/push.ts` and `src/workspace/gc.ts` over the same
  ledger handle. The lifecycle (`src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts`) calls them at dispatch, at point 1 and at point 2 (in its
  terminal `settle`, before waiters resolve); reconciliation (`src/reconcile/reconcile.ts`) at point 3 after orphan
  termination and before the worktree is assessed; GC at point 4 before the worktrees go, and once more after (a path
  whose removal failed goes with its worktree and is then recorded `verified`). Nothing imports `src/skills/`.
- **Declaration.** `skillDelivery(cell)` returns `"prompt_prefix" | { native: NativeSkillLocation }`; the location
  carries `dir`, `layout` (`{ file }` or `{ ext }`), `render` and `evidence` (the T16.8 id). `skillDeliveryOf` reads a
  native declaration without a non-empty evidence id, a renderer or a safe relative path as `prompt_prefix`. Only the
  fake adapter can declare `native` (`nativeSkills: true`, `.fake/skills/switchboard-<name>.md`, evidence
  `fake-test-only`). `skills.delivery: prefix_only` reaches the lifecycle as `skillDeliveryMode` (`src/cli/serve.ts`).
  A handoff run delivers like any other run since T16.10: entries a native cell takes are written as files and left
  out of the block of the rendered handoff prompt, which is rendered again from the same verified view (§15.1
  "Version 2"); a native resume writes the files again for the new run.
- **Recorded path.** `native_path` is written as an intent before the file is created and cleared again only when a
  failed write left nothing; `delivery = 'native'` and `delivered_at` are set in the dispatch transaction. A recorded
  path is one whose `removal` is not `verified`.
- **Taken paths.** Besides the four Git checks, the path, the `switchboard-` directory and any symlinked or non-directory
  parent already on disk count as `path_taken`, so the cleaner only ever removes what the writer made. Ignore rules are
  checked for every prefix and, for parents, with a trailing `/` too (`git check-ignore --no-index`).
- **Exclusions.** The commit tool adds `:(exclude,top,literal)` pathspecs to `add -A`, resets explicitly named recorded
  paths to the parent's entries in its temporary index, and refuses `PATH_REFUSED` with `detailReason`
  `SKILL_PATH_IN_COMMIT` (named in the message; the tool protocol has no `details`) when the written tree still holds
  one, which happens when the publish ref already has a file there. Checkpoints use the same exclusions and reset the
  worktree index entries to `HEAD` (an agent's `git add`); `assessMutation`, `rollback` and the `diff` and
  `changed_files` artifacts exclude them too. No separate `patch` artifact exists today. The handoff diff summary is
  computed from checkpoint commits, which never contain a recorded path, so `src/continuity/` is unchanged.
- **Push.** `GitPusher` refuses `SKILL_PATH_PRESENT` (after the policy and violation checks, before any Git call)
  while a recorded path of any run of the meta-session exists in its worktree; `switchboard git push` explains it.
- **Removal.** A recorded path whose parent is no longer a real directory, or that is gone, is `verified` (the bytes
  may live on under another name, R-69). A link or other non-regular file there is `skill_tampered` with
  `found_sha256: null`; a link is unlinked without being followed, a directory is never removed (`failed`, `EISDIR`).
  `skill_removed` also carries `point` (`before_verifying`, `terminal`, `reconciliation`, `gc`). `removal = 'not_needed'`
  is not written for `prompt_prefix` rows. `needs_attention` reads the newest `skill_removed` event, written in the
  same transaction as the `run_skill` update.
- Tests: `src/workspace/native-skills.test.ts`, `src/lifecycle/native-skills.test.ts`,
  `src/reconcile/native-skills.test.ts`, `src/control/needs-attention.test.ts`.

### 19.9 MCP tools (FR-SKL-08)

Offered when `skills.enabled` is true, to clients whose `tool_profile` is `orchestrator`. Both are read-only.

```jsonc
// list_skills
{ "input": { "type": "object", "properties": {
    "project": { "type": "string" }, "role": { "type": "string" }, "trust_profile": { "$ref": "#/$defs/trust_profile" } } },
  "output": { "skills": [ { "name": "", "description": "", "version": 3, "sha256": "", "size_bytes": 0,
                            "approved": true, "origin": "operator_file", "applies_to_providers": null } ],
              "defaults": [ { "name": "", "version": 3, "source": "project_role" } ] } }   // only when project given

// get_skill
{ "input": { "type": "object", "required": ["name"], "properties": {
    "name": { "type": "string" }, "version": { "type": "integer", "minimum": 1 } } },
  "output": { "name": "", "version": 3, "sha256": "", "description": "", "approved": true, "body": "" } }
```

`list_skills` returns only names visible to the client (`mcp_clients[].skills`) and, per name, the newest version the
client could inject under `trust_profile` (default: the client's maximum). `defaults` shows what §19.5 steps 2 to 4
would add for `project` and `role`. `get_skill` returns a visible version's text; an invisible or unknown name is
`INVALID_REQUEST` with no hint that it exists. Neither tool writes a ledger row other than the existing tool-call
record.

*Implementation (T16.9, 2026-10-17, Delegated, not owner-approved):* `src/mcp/schemas.ts` (`SKILL_TOOL_NAMES`, listed
after `search_project_context`), handlers in `src/mcp/server.ts` behind the `skills` server dependency (store handle,
data directory, resolved `skills` settings), and the pure resolver `resolveSkills` in `src/skills/resolve.ts` for
§19.5 steps 2 to 4, which `defaults` uses and admission (T16.4) is to reuse; it writes nothing. The tools are outside
the `console` set (FR-CTL-11), so `src/mcp/tool-profile.ts` is unchanged. The MCP server records no tool call in the
ledger today, so "the existing tool-call record" is none: a test compares every table before and after. Delegated
readings: a `revoked` version is never usable (also where a draft would be); "the newest version the client could
inject" may be older than the newest approved one when that one fails a gate (it is the newest that passes step 4
on its own, so the client can pin it); `trust_profile` defaults to the client's maximum, or its ceiling in `project`,
and a higher one is `PERMISSION_BLOCKED` as in `delegate_task`; a `project` the client may not use is reported like an
unknown one, as `search_project_context` does; every `get_skill` miss (unknown, retired, invisible, version not
injectable) is one `INVALID_REQUEST` that never names the skill; `applies_to_providers` is `null` for every provider;
a duplicated name takes its position from the first occurrence and its version and source from the first `@n`, and is
a requested name if any occurrence is; a stored file that fails its hash is `TOOL_FAILURE` with `details.reason =
"SKILL_CONTENT_UNAVAILABLE"`. `switchboard serve` passes the `skills` dependency only while `skills.enabled`
is true (`src/cli/serve.ts`; `src/cli/serve-skills.test.ts`), so a running installation offers the tools when skills
are enabled and none when disabled.

### 19.10 Operator CLI (FR-SKL-09)

| Command | Effect |
| --- | --- |
| `switchboard skills list [--all]` | Names with newest version, state, origin, size and hash prefix; `--all` adds retired names |
| `switchboard skills show <name>[@n] [--body]` | Metadata and event history; the body only with `--body` |
| `switchboard skills add <name> --file <path> [--from-repository] [--note <text>]` | Validates (§19.1), stores and records `added`; prints `<name>@<n>` |
| `switchboard skills approve <name>@<n> [--note <text>]` | Records `approved` |
| `switchboard skills revoke <name>@<n> [--note <text>]` | Records `revoked`; runs already admitted keep their delivery |
| `switchboard skills retire <name>` / `unretire <name>` | Name-level state |
| `switchboard skills diff <name>@<a> <name>@<b>` | Line diff of two versions' canonical text |

The commands are short-lived and write only operator records and the content file (ADR-0005). `doctor` gains one line:
skills enabled or not, counts of approved and draft versions, configured names that do not resolve, and any run with
`removal = 'failed'`. `runs show` lists a run's skills (name, version, hash prefix, mode, removal).

*Implementation (T16.2, 2026-10-17, Delegated, not owner-approved):* `src/cli/skills.ts`, read helpers in
`src/skills/read.ts` and `src/skills/diff.ts`. The commands take no data-directory lock: both tables are append-only
and a running daemon only reads them. `list`, `show` and `diff` open the ledger read-only and never create it; a ledger
without the 0021 tables reads as an empty store. An unknown name or version is `NOT_FOUND` (exit 1); a malformed
`<name>@<n>`, a note over 280 characters and a flag on the wrong subcommand are usage errors (exit 2). `show --body`
prints the body without the front matter (the metadata already shows it), of the named version or else the newest;
`diff` is a unified diff with three context lines, which falls back to all-removed then all-added when the changed
middle exceeds 4,000,000 comparison cells. The `doctor` line (`skills` check) counts versions by state over every name,
adds the retired-name count, and is a warning, never a failure, when a configured name does not resolve or a removal
failed; with no ledger every configured name is reported `unknown`.

### 19.11 Run events and telemetry (FR-SKL-10)

| Event | Payload |
| --- | --- |
| `skill_resolved` | entries `{name, version, sha256, source}`, skipped `{name, reason}` |
| `skill_delivered` | entries `{name, version, delivery, native_path?, fallback_reason?}` |
| `skill_removed` | entries `{name, native_path, removal}` |
| `skill_tampered` | `{name, native_path, written_sha256, found_sha256}` |
| `skill_cleanup_failed` | `{name, native_path, errno}` |

No event, span, log line, artifact or sink payload contains a skill body or description. Spans gain
`switchboard.skills.count` and `switchboard.skills.hashes` (12-hex prefixes). The ADR-0048 payload is unchanged.

## 20. Harness and discovery capabilities (Proposed, 2026-10-19)

*Proposed design notes (2026-10-19, Delegated agent work, documentation only; not owner-approved beyond owner decisions
76 and 77 in [ROADMAP §4.2](../ROADMAP.md#42-owner-decisions)).* The sources are the operator's harness comparison
(recommendations R1 to R7, kept on the operator host) and the
[enhancement discovery of 2026-10-19](../research/enhancement-discovery-2026-10-19.md) as answered in its §8. Each
subsection is the starting contract for one roadmap task; the task refines it before code and records what it built.
Configuration keys, names and defaults here are **Proposed**, and every default number is an **Assumption**, not a
provider fact. Nothing here adds a run state: holds keep a run `QUEUED`, and parking applies to work items (§18.2).

### 20.1 CI transient handling (FR-QUE-06, T15.6)

- **Classification.** Each conclusion the CI trigger reads (§18.6) maps to one class: `success`; `failure`; or
  `CI_INFRA` for `cancelled` and `timed_out` (settled in T15.6 against `gh` 2.102.0: no allowlisted field
  reliably marks a runner fault inside a `failure`, so those stay code failures; see the as-built note below).
  `CI_INFRA` never increments `ci_trigger_ref_state.consecutive_fixes`.
- **Rerun once.** With `ci_trigger.projects[].rerun_failed: true` (default `false`), a `failure` or `CI_INFRA` run on
  a published ref's current head gets one `gh run rerun <run-id> --failed`. A new ledger table (migration 0024)
  keyed by `(project_id, publish_ref, head_sha, job_name)` makes the rerun happen at most once per key. The fix-up item
  of FR-QUE-03 is enqueued only when the rerun concludes `failure`; a second red on the same key is never rerun again.
- **Stale cancel.** With `ci_trigger.projects[].cancel_stale_queued: true` (default `false`), the poller lists runs on
  each published ref, re-reads each candidate's `status` and `headSha` immediately before acting, and runs
  `gh run cancel <run-id>` only for `status: queued` whose `headSha` is not the ref's current tip. An `in_progress` run
  is never cancelled; the small race between the re-read and the cancel is an accepted residual risk (Assumption).
- **Allowlist.** [ADR-0050](../adr/0050-ci-status-via-operator-gh-cli.md) amendment 1 adds exactly these two write
  verbs, each behind its own project switch; every other `gh` verb stays refused (`src/ci/gh-argv.ts`).
- **Events.** `ci_rerun_requested {publish_ref, head_sha, job_name, run_id}` and `ci_run_cancelled {publish_ref,
  head_sha, run_id}`; no `gh` free text is copied.

**As built (T15.6, 2026-10-19, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved).** Settled against `gh` 2.102.0 on the
operator host (`gh run view --help` lists the `attempt` and `jobs` fields, `gh run rerun --help` the `--failed` flag,
`gh run list --help` the `--status queued` filter; `--help` only, no call to GitHub):

1. **Classes.** `classifyConclusion` in `src/ci/port.ts`. No allowlisted `gh` JSON field reliably marks a runner fault
   inside a `failure` (neither `--help` field list names a runner-fault field, and a run-level `startup_failure` also
   follows an invalid workflow file, a code fault), so only `cancelled` and `timed_out` are `CI_INFRA` (Delegated,
   fail closed: a doubtful fault stays a code failure). The classification applies whether or not a switch is on, as
   FR-QUE-06 requires: with both switches off a `CI_INFRA` conclusion is recorded in `ci_trigger_event` with no work
   item and leaves `ci_trigger_ref_state` untouched (it neither counts nor resets). This changes T15.3's behaviour,
   which enqueued a fix-up for `cancelled` and `timed_out`; `success`, `failure` and the `gh` argv set are unchanged.
2. **Switches.** Per project, `ci_trigger.projects[].rerun_failed` and `ci_trigger.projects[].cancel_stale_queued`
   (FR-QUE-06 says "when the project enables"), both default `false` (`src/config/ci-trigger.ts`, `schema.ts`).
3. **Argv.** Five templates join G1 to G3 in `src/ci/gh-argv.ts`, each audited exactly: G4
   `run view <id> --repo <o>/<r> --json attempt,jobs`, G5 `run rerun <id> --failed --repo <o>/<r>`, G6
   `run list --repo <o>/<r> --branch <b> --status queued --limit 20 --json databaseId,conclusion,headBranch,status,headSha`,
   G7 `run view <id> --repo <o>/<r> --json databaseId,status,headSha` and G8 `run cancel <id> --repo <o>/<r>` (never
   `--force`). G4 and G5 run only with `rerun_failed`, G6 to G8 only with `cancel_stale_queued`. The job members read
   from G4 are `name`, `status` and `conclusion` (**Assumption**: `--help` lists `jobs` but not its members); a job name
   is kept only when it has 1 to 200 characters and no control character, else the read is `PARSE_FAILED`.
4. **Migration 0024** (`src/ledger/migrations/0024_ci_transient.ts`). `ci_rerun` (primary key `(project_id,
   publish_ref, head_sha, job_name)`, with `run_id`, `attempt`, `outcome` `requested` or `gh_failed`, `requested_at`)
   is the `ci_rerun_requested` event; `ci_run_cancel` (`UNIQUE (project_id, run_id)`, with `publish_ref`, `head_sha`,
   `outcome` `cancelled` or `gh_failed`, `cancelled_at`) is the `ci_run_cancelled` event. Rows are written **before**
   the `gh` write, so a crash can never cause a second rerun or cancel; a failed `gh` write spends the key.
5. **Rerun rule** (`rerunOnce` in `src/ci/trigger.ts`). A rerun happens only when the run has at least one completed
   job concluding `failure`, `cancelled` or `timed_out` (the rerun allowlist) and **none** of those jobs was rerun on
   this head before, because `--failed` reruns every failed job. A rerun keeps the run id and raises its attempt, so the
   trigger waits (no rerun, no fix-up) while the run still shows the attempt the rerun was requested from, for at most
   `RERUN_VISIBLE_MS` (10 minutes, Assumption), then treats the key as spent. Otherwise the conclusion is handled as
   without the switch: a `failure` admits one fix-up under the FR-QUE-05 cap, a `CI_INFRA` is recorded only.
6. **Stale cancel rule** (`cancelStaleQueued`). Only a run listed as `queued` on the ref's branch whose head is a SHA
   Switchboard earlier pushed to the same ref (an `ACCEPTED` `git_push` row other than the tip) is a candidate; a run
   on the tip or on a commit Switchboard never pushed is never cancelled, which keeps the action inside "branches the
   project published". The G7 re-read must still say `queued` on the same head.

Tests: `src/ci/ci-transient.test.ts` (fake `CiStatusPort`, fake `CiActionsPort` and a fake `GhRunner`; no real `gh`).

### 20.2 Host load hold (FR-CAP-12, T3.24)

- **Keys.** `host.max_load1` and `host.resume_load1` (numbers; both unset by default; `resume_load1` must be lower than
  `max_load1` or the configuration is refused). A `LoadReader` port returns the 1-minute load average
  (`os.loadavg()[0]` on Linux); tests inject it.
- **Hold.** In the dispatch pass of `pump()`, the same place as the disk floor (§5, T3.23): above `max_load1` the
  controller enters a hold episode, every queued run stays `QUEUED`, and each gets one `load_hold {load1, max, resume}`
  event per episode. The episode ends only when the load falls below `resume_load1` (hysteresis). Running runs are
  never signalled. `get_run` and `get_digest` show the hold reason `held_load`.
- **Light items.** A request or work item with `light: true` is admitted during a hold. Because a client could mark
  everything light, the flag is honoured only for a client whose configuration sets `mcp_clients[].allow_light: true`
  (default `false`, Delegated least privilege); the operator CLI may always set it.
- **Unreadable load.** A reader error never holds; `doctor` warns.
- **As built (T3.24, 2026-10-19, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved).** `host` needs both keys together, each a
  number above 0 and at most 100 000 (a typo guard, Assumption); `resume_load1 >= max_load1` is refused at load. The
  port is `LoadReader` in `src/capacity/host-load.ts` (`osLoadReader` reads `os.loadavg()[0]`); a thrown error, a
  non-finite or a negative value counts as unreadable. The hold starts when the load is above `max_load1` and ends when
  it is below `resume_load1`; an unreadable read also ends it. While held, `pump()` re-reads every 15 s (Assumption).
  The `load_hold` payload is `{reason: "LOAD_ABOVE_MAX", load1, max, resume}`. When the episode ends, each held run
  that is still queued gets one `load_release {load1}`. The envelope (`get_run`, `list_runs`) carries
  `hold: {reason: "held_load", since}` while a `QUEUED` run's latest `load_hold` has no later `load_release`. Each
  `get_digest` project carries `held: [{run_id, reason, envelope}]`, present only when non-empty, so an unconfigured
  controller returns the same digest as before. After a restart, open holds are adopted on the first read, so
  hysteresis survives it. `light` is a `delegate_task` and `enqueue_work.request` field. It is honoured (a `light_run`
  admission event) only for a client with `allow_light: true` and is otherwise ignored, not refused. It is part of
  the request hash only when given. No operator CLI command submits runs today, so the CLI clause has nothing to
  attach to yet. `doctor` adds a `Host load` line only when `host` is set.

### 20.3 Decision requests (FR-CTL-13, T14.15)

- **Record.** A new table (next free migration) `decision_request`: `id` (`dec_*`), `project_id`, `work_item_id`
  (nullable), `requester_client_id`, `question` (untrusted, at most 2,000 characters), `options_json` (two to six
  options, each a label of at most 200 characters and an action `resume`, `reroute`, `cancel` or `none`),
  `recommended_option`, `enqueue_json` (work items in the `enqueue_work` shape), `state` (`open`, `decided`,
  `withdrawn`), `decided_option`, `decided_by` (`cli`, `mcp_proxy:<client_id>` or `consent_link`), `note`, timestamps.
- **Parking.** The named work item moves to a new work-item state `PARKED_DECISION` (§18.2), which the dequeue step of
  §18.7 skips; other items of the project keep running. A decision without a work item parks nothing.
- **Tools.** `request_decision` and `list_decisions` in the `orchestrator` tool profile; `decide` only for clients
  marked `approval_proxy` (FR-CTL-08), plus `switchboard decisions list|decide` and a single-use consent link
  (FR-CTL-09). Deciding twice returns `CONFLICT`.
- **Exactly once.** `decide` updates the decision, applies the option's action and inserts the `enqueue_json` items in
  one transaction; the items are admitted later under the requester's ceiling, never the decider's.
- **Sinks.** Event kind `decision_requested`, reason `awaiting_decision`, the closed ADR-0048 payload; the question,
  options and note never leave the ledger except through `list_decisions`, labelled untrusted.

### 20.4 Published-ref conflicts and supersede (FR-QUE-07, T15.7)

- **Read.** For each watched published ref the poller reads `gh pr view <ref> --json
  number,state,isDraft,mergeable,headRefOid` (read-only, added to the ADR-0050 allowlist by amendment 1).
- **Rebase item.** On `CONFLICTING` the controller enqueues one rebase item. Agents cannot run Git writes (FR-GIT-01),
  so the controller prepares the worktree: the ref's head with the current base merged in by controller Git, conflicts
  left as markers for the agent to resolve; the commit tool then records the result. Whether the commit tool can record
  a two-parent commit, or must squash the resolution onto the ref, is settled in T15.7 (Assumption: two-parent commit
  through the same sanitiser and provenance record, FR-GIT-04, FR-GIT-07).
- **Cap and supersede.** Rebase items count against the FR-QUE-05 per-ref counter. At the cap, one re-implementation
  item starts from the current base with the old ref's diff as a context file (untrusted), the old ref's state gets
  `superseded_by` (the new item) and the poller stops watching it.
- **`dedupe_key`.** A text field on work items; a partial unique index on `(project_id, dedupe_key)` over live or
  published, non-superseded items refuses a second one with `CONFLICT`.
- **`hot_paths`.** Up to 32 path globs per item. Two items overlap when the literal prefix of any glob of one is a
  prefix of a literal prefix of the other (conservative). The §18.7 dequeue step skips an item that overlaps a running
  item of the same project.

### 20.5 Scheduled digest and progress stall (FR-CTL-14, T14.16)

- **Digest schedule.** A schedule of kind `digest` (§18.5, off by default) fires a sink event `digest` whose payload is
  the ADR-0048 closed shape plus a `counts` object of integers only (runs by state, needs-attention by reason, queue
  depth, holds by reason, open decisions). The extra object needs an amendment to
  [ADR-0048](../adr/0048-notification-sinks.md) (Proposed, so it can be amended) before code.
- **Stall.** `projects.<id>.queue.stall_hours` (unset by default). `stalled_progress` is derived on read: the project has
  `READY` items and no item reached `DONE` within the window (the window starts at the later of the last `DONE` and the
  first `READY`). The next `DONE` clears it.

**As built (T14.16, 2026-10-19, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved).**

- **Digest schedule** is operator configuration, `control_plane.digest_schedule: { "cron": "<five fields, UTC>" }`, in
  the §18.5 cron syntax; absent means off. It is not a `project_schedule` row: a sink is an operator-chosen egress path
  (ADR-0048 item 1), so a chat client cannot create one, and no migration is added. An invalid expression or an unknown
  key aborts startup. `serve` builds the schedule only when the key is set and a notification sink exists (it logs a
  warning when the key is set without a sink), and checks it at the start of every scheduler tick (§18.7) on the
  injected clock: a matching UTC minute fires once per process (`src/control/digest-push.ts`).
- **Event.** One aggregate `digest` event per fire: `project_id` `*`, `reason_code` `digest`, `run_id` null, the usual
  link, and `counts` with exactly these integer leaves: `runs_by_state` (every run state, all time),
  `needs_attention_by_reason` (the run-level reasons over non-terminal runs, plus `stalled_progress` per configured
  project), `queue_by_state` (work items of every state), `pending_approvals` and `paused_projects`. Holds by reason
  and open decisions join when T3.24 and T14.15 build them. The payload builder rebuilds `counts` key by key from the
  closed lists and drops the event when a key is missing or extra or a value is not a non-negative integer; `counts`
  on any other kind is dropped too (`src/control/sinks.ts`). The ntfy message appends the non-zero grouped counts
  and always the two scalar counts (`pending_approvals`, `paused_projects`), each as `group.key=n`.
- **Stall** reads `READY` count, the oldest `READY` item's `created_at` and the newest `DONE` item's `terminal_at`
  (`readProgressStallFacts`, `src/ledger/work-queue.ts`). "First `READY`" is the oldest `READY` item's `created_at`,
  so an item cycling `READY` → `ADMITTING` → `READY` on admission retries does not restart the window. `stall_hours` is
  an integer from 1 to 720 (the upper bound is an **Assumption**, a sanity limit); any other value aborts startup.
  `get_digest` lists `project_needs_attention: { "active": true, "reason": "stalled_progress" }` on a stalled project
  and omits the key otherwise (`src/control/progress-stall.ts`, `src/mcp/digest.ts`). No separate `needs_attention`
  sink event is sent for it; the pushed digest counts it.
- Tests: `src/control/digest-push.test.ts`, `src/config/digest-stall-config.test.ts`.

### 20.6 Head-pinned verification evidence and the docs-alignment skill (FR-EXE-10, FR-SKL-11, T8.10)

- **SHA in the report.** Before the verification stage, controller Git records `HEAD` and computes the worktree's tree
  with a temporary index (`GIT_INDEX_FILE`), never touching the run's index. The `test_report` gains `head_sha` and
  `tree_sha`; `head_sha` is kept only when `tree_sha` equals `HEAD^{tree}`.
- **Read.** `get_run` adds `verification.verified_at_sha`, `verification.tree_sha` and `verification.stale`
  (`stale` is true when the run's
  published ref (§12.3) has moved to another commit).
- **Skill.** `examples/skills/docs-alignment.md` is an ordinary skill file (§19); its run writes
  `docs_alignment.json` `{head_sha, verdict: "aligned" | "conflict", notes}` as an artifact (notes untrusted). A fix is
  a normal commit through the commit tool on the item's own branch.
- **Lessons kept from the harness gate.** Results are keyed in the ledger by `(publish_ref, head_sha)`, never by a file
  name, and no gate holds a box-wide lock while it waits for a verification slot.

*As built (2026-10-19, T8.10, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved):*

1. **Pin** (`src/git/head-pin.ts`, `readHeadPin`). In a Switchboard worktree the commit tool moves the run's publish ref
   (§12.3), not the worktree's `HEAD`, so the candidate commit is the publish ref's commit when that ref exists and the
   worktree's `HEAD` otherwise (Delegated reading of "`HEAD`"). The tree is built in a fresh private directory with
   `GIT_INDEX_FILE`: `read-tree <candidate>`, `add -A` with nested repositories excluded (as a commit-tool `commit`
   without paths), `write-tree`. `head_sha` is the candidate only when that tree equals `<candidate>^{tree}`. Any Git
   error gives no SHA (fail closed) and never fails the run. The pin is read in VERIFYING before the first command,
   after native skill files are removed (§19.8).
2. **Report and ledger.** The `test_report` body gains `head_sha` (null when the trees differ) and `tree_sha`.
   Migration 0027 adds `verification_result` (`id` `vrs_*`, `run_id`, `meta_session_id`, `publish_ref`, `head_sha`,
   `tree_sha`, `outcome`, `artifact_id`, `created_at`; index on `(publish_ref, head_sha)`), one row per stage whose
   report was stored, and widens `artifact.kind` with `docs_alignment` (the table rebuild of migrations 0016 and 0022).
3. **Read.** The run envelope gains `verification: {verified_at_sha, tree_sha, stale}` from the run's newest
   `verification_result`. `stale` is computed on read from the ledger's newest `git_commit` for the run's publish ref
   (Linus decision v10 (a); no poller change): true when that head is another commit. Delegated (fail closed): a result
   without a SHA is always `stale: true`, so it never counts as verification at the current head.
4. **Docs-alignment report** (`src/lifecycle/docs-alignment.ts`). Only for a run whose `skill_resolved` event names
   `docs-alignment`: after the agent exits and before verification, the controller removes `docs_alignment.json` from
   the worktree root (so no commit or push can carry it and the pin is not dirtied by it), reads it only as a regular
   file of at most 16 KiB, and checks it strictly (exactly `head_sha`, `verdict`, `notes`; a 40 or 64 character
   lower-case SHA; notes at most 8000 characters). A valid report is stored as a redacted `docs_alignment` artifact and
   a `docs_alignment` run event records `{present, valid, verdict, head_sha, artifact_id}`; `notes` never enters an
   event. An invalid report records `valid: false` and stores nothing. The verdict is reported, never acted on: no
   commit, push or merge follows from it, and the push tool still refuses a protected target (`TARGET_PROTECTED`,
   §12.6). The example skill is not enabled by default (Linus decision v10 (b)); the operator adds and approves it like
   any skill (§19.1, §19.10).

### 20.7 Opt-in merge policy and ref events (FR-SUB-01, FR-SUB-02, T15.8, ADR-0060)

- **Configuration.** `projects[].merge_policy`: `enabled` (default `false`), `method` (`squash` default, `merge`,
  `rebase`), `required_reports` (skill reports that must say `aligned` at the head, default none). An orchestrator that
  turns it on waits for operator approval (FR-PRJ-10).
- **Gate, evaluated by the controller from `gh` JSON only.** The pull request is open, not a draft, on a ref this
  project published, against the context's base; every required check is completed and successful at the current head
  SHA (a project whose base branch has no required checks is refused, fail closed); `mergeable` is `MERGEABLE`; a
  verification result is recorded at that head SHA (§20.6); every `required_reports` entry says `aligned` at that SHA.
- **Merge.** `gh pr merge <number> --<method> --match-head-commit <head_sha>`, so a head that moved after the read
  refuses on GitHub's side too. The command used by the operator's harness is the evidence for the flag; T15.8 checks
  it against the installed `gh` before relying on it, and how required checks are read is settled there too.
- **Ledger.** `merge_event`: `project_id`, `publish_ref`, `pr_number`, `head_sha`, `outcome` (`merged` or `refused`),
  `reason` from a closed list (`head_moved`, `check_failed`, `check_pending`, `no_required_checks`, `not_mergeable`,
  `unverified_at_head`, `report_missing`, `draft`, `policy_off`, `gh_error`).
- **Ref events (FR-SUB-01 first delivery).** `ref_event` rows of kind `ci_passed`, `ci_failed`, `foreign_commit`,
  `conflicting`, `merged`, `closed`, read through the same poller, shown in `get_digest` and sent to sinks as closed
  payloads. No inbound webhook; event text never authorises anything.

### 20.8 Work queue over HTTP (FR-QUE-08, T15.9)

Built (T15.9 Done): the network transport lists `enqueue_work`, `list_work` and `cancel_work` for a client whose
`tool_profile` is `orchestrator`, under the same scope check as `delegate_task` over HTTP (FR-MCP-14: bearer token
with the `mcp` scope, an approved and mapped OAuth client). The stdio-only filter (`workQueueOffered` in
`src/mcp/server.ts`, beside `WORK_QUEUE_TOOL_NAMES` in `src/mcp/schemas.ts`) is now profile-gated instead of
transport-gated: an `orchestrator` client is offered the tools over either transport, while a `console` client sees
and calls none of them over either transport (answered exactly like an unknown tool, FR-CTL-11). Ownership is by the
authenticated client id (the mapped MCP client over HTTP): `list_work` returns only the caller's own items and
`cancel_work` of another client's item fails exactly like an unknown id, so ids are not an existence oracle; an item
with no recorded owner is operator-owned and therefore invisible and uncancellable over HTTP. Admission stays under
the creating client's ceiling (§18.4).

### 20.9 Pre-push secret scan (FR-GIT-11, T9.14, ADR-0061)

- **Scanner.** Deterministic and in-process: the rule set of the artifact secret redactor
  ([ADR-0042](../adr/0042-objective-redaction-at-admission.md)) plus high-signal token formats, each rule with a stable
  id; no entropy-only rule by default (false positives), Assumption. No external scanner binary.
- **Scope.** Added lines of the outgoing diff (`git diff -U0 --inter-hunk-context=0 --text --no-renames --no-color
  --no-relative --src-prefix=a/ --dst-prefix=b/ --end-of-options <from> <head>` through the hardened helper, which adds
  `--no-textconv --no-ext-diff` and neutralises every driver), where `<from>` is the remote-tracking tip when the
  ledger holds an ACCEPTED push of exactly that SHA and it is an ancestor of the head, else the base checkpoint (after
  a push by hand the next controller push scans everything since the base again). Every file is diffed as text, so
  a `.gitattributes` line cannot hide added lines; paths Git counts as binary are still scanned and are listed in
  the event.
- **Outcome.** A hit refuses the push with `SECRET_SCAN_BLOCKED`, appends `secret_scan_hit {stage: "push", rule_id,
  path, line}` (never the value) and flags `secret_scan_blocked` until a clean push of that ref (an operator
  acknowledgement through the CLI is Proposed, not built). The commit tool appends the same event with `stage:
  "commit"` and does not refuse (built by the T9.14 split, part 1; see below).
- **No override in-band.** The only allowlist is operator configuration (`git.secret_scan.allow`, rule id and path
  glob); no MCP field, privileged scope or bypass flag changes the result. The operator can always push by hand.

**Implemented (T9.14, 2026-10-19, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved beyond owner decision 77).** The push side
is built, and so is the commit-time warning (`stage: "commit"`, T9.14 split part 1, 2026-10-20); the operator
acknowledgement through the CLI is not (the row stays `Partial` for it).

- **Scanner.** `src/git/secret-scan.ts`. The rule set is exactly the artifact redactor's (`SECRET_RULES` and
  `PREFIXED_RULES` in `src/artifacts/redact.ts`, which gained stable ids; no second pattern set): `pem_private_key`,
  `aws_access_key_id`, `github_token`, `github_fine_grained_pat`, `sk_api_key`, `slack_token`, `jwt`,
  `switchboard_tool_token`, `switchboard_oauth_token`, `bearer_authorization`, `credential_assignment`. No entropy
  rule. Each added line is tested against every rule; a report lists at most 100 hits and 100 binary paths with exact
  counts.
- **Scope and range.** `git diff -U0 --text --no-renames --no-color --no-relative --src-prefix=a/ --dst-prefix=b/
  <from> <head>` through the hardened helper (which adds `--no-textconv --no-ext-diff` and neutralises every driver),
  after every other push check and before any remote write. `<from>` is the remote-tracking ref
  `refs/remotes/<remote>/<branch_prefix><sess>` when the ledger holds an ACCEPTED `git_push` of exactly that SHA to
  that ref (the ref guard's own rule for a tracking-ref change) and it is an ancestor of the head (Git updates it on
  every accepted push; no network read happens before the push itself, Delegated), else the base checkpoint (a new
  ref, a tracking ref written by anything but a controller push, or one that cannot explain the head: the whole
  verified history, a superset; so after a push by hand the next controller push scans everything since the base
  again, and a false positive published by hand needs an allowlist entry). `--text` means a `.gitattributes` line
  marking a path `binary` or `-diff` cannot hide added lines; the paths Git counts as binary are still listed in the
  event (`numstat`). `--no-renames` makes the scan a function of the two trees alone, so a file moved into the
  outgoing diff is scanned whole. A Git failure or an oversized diff is `GIT_FAILED` (fail closed), never a clean scan.
- **Outcome.** As above: `REFUSED_POLICY` / `SECRET_SCAN_BLOCKED` in every profile (an exempt approved bypass run is
  refused with its `credential_reach: "bypass_exempt"` still recorded), tool code `SECRET_SCAN_BLOCKED`, at most 50
  `secret_scan_hit` events `{stage: "push", rule_id, path, line, ref}` on the run the push is recorded against, then
  the `git_push` event with `secret_scan {from, hit_count, allowed_count, binary_count, binary_paths}`. The push tool
  takes no input and `PushRequest` has no field the scan reads. `needs_attention` / `secret_scan_blocked` is derived
  on read from the meta-session's `git_push` events (§3.4.5, `src/control/needs-attention.ts`).
- **Allowlist.** `git.secret_scan.allow[]` (at most 64 entries of `{rule_id, path}`): a hit of that rule at a
  repository-relative path the glob matches exactly (`fixtures/**` for a tree; a bare directory name is not a prefix)
  is counted in `allowed_count` and does not refuse. An unknown rule id or an empty glob stops startup naming the
  entry. Tests: `src/git/secret-scan.test.ts`, the "pre-push secret scan" block of `src/git/push.test.ts`, the
  `secret_scan_blocked` block of `src/control/needs-attention.test.ts`, `git.secret_scan.allow` in
  `src/config/config.test.ts`.
- **Commit-time warning.** `GitPublisher.warnSecrets` in `src/git/commit.ts`, after the commit is published and its
  outcome recorded: the same scanner and the same `git.secret_scan.allow` entries over the added lines of
  `parent..commit`, at most 50 `secret_scan_hit` events `{stage: "commit", rule_id, path, line, ref, commit_sha}` on
  the committing run. It never throws and never refuses; a scan or ledger failure records nothing and leaves the
  commit unchanged, and it raises no `needs_attention`. Tests: the commit-time cases in the "pre-push secret scan"
  block of `src/git/push.test.ts`.

### 20.10 Repository-configuration preflight (FR-POL-11, T3.25)

At admission, after the worktree exists, a bounded walk (depth and count capped, symlinks not followed) records
`policy_snapshot_json.repo_config_seen`: up to 200 `{path, kind}` entries for the paths FR-POL-11 names. In an approved
bypass run it appends one `repo_config_warning` event; under the untrusted profiles the existing FR-POL-04 handling
applies unchanged.

Implemented by T3.25 (`src/policy/repo-config-preflight.ts`, called from `execute` in `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` once
the working directory exists, before compile and the FR-POL-04 refusal):

- **Walk.** Breadth-first in name order over the working directory with `readdir` file types only: no file is opened,
  read or hashed. A symbolic link whose name matches a kind below is listed by name (`symlink: true`); any other
  name — including a non-matching symlink — is not listed; no symlink is followed or entered. `.git/` and
  `node_modules/` are not entered. A listed directory (`.claude/`, `.cursor/`) is recorded once and not entered. A
  directory that cannot be read is skipped.
- **Caps** (Linus decision, WORK QUEUE v10, Delegated, Assumption). Paths of at most 4 segments, 200 entries, 5,000
  directory entries visited. Hitting any cap sets `policy_snapshot_json.repo_config_truncated` to `true` (always
  written, `false` otherwise).
- **Kinds.** `claude_config` (`.claude`), `mcp_config` (`.mcp.json`), `cursor_config` (`.cursor`), `vscode_tasks`
  (`.vscode/tasks.json`), `envrc` (`.envrc`), `env_file` (`.env`, `.env.*`) and `provider_config` for the provider
