  project files that can set a base-URL override (**Assumption**, contents not read so "may set": `.codex/config.toml`,
  `.grok/config.toml`, `.gemini/settings.json`, `opencode.json`, `opencode.jsonc`). Every name matches at any depth
  within the cap.
- **Record.** The ledger sets both keys with `json_set` (other snapshot keys untouched) for every run, in the same
  transaction as the warning event when there is one. A retry successor copies the snapshot and overwrites both keys
  at its own dispatch.
- **Warning.** Only an approved bypass run (`bypassApproved` from the dispatch verdict and `bypassApprovedFor`, as the
  argv audit gate uses) with at least one entry appends one `repo_config_warning` event `{count, kinds, truncated}`
  (sorted unique kinds, no paths, no contents); the run proceeds (owner decision 77 answer 1). No other profile gets an
  event.

### 20.11 MCP SDK v2 and Client ID Metadata Documents (FR-MCP-18, FR-MCP-19, T13.10, T13.11)

An ADR superseding the affected part of [ADR-0041](../adr/0041-http-transport-with-oauth-2-1.md) comes first and
follows the T14.12 readiness spike. CIMD fetch limits (Proposed): `https` only; the resolved address and every redirect
target must not be private, loopback, link-local or unique-local; at most one redirect; 5 s and 64 KiB caps; the
document's `client_id` must equal its URL; redirect URIs `https` only; the consent page shows the URL's host. Stdio is
unchanged.

(Superseded in one respect by the T13.11 as-built below: no redirects are followed at all, per the draft's §5.)

**T13.10 as built (2026-10-19, Delegated; [ADR-0062](../adr/0062-mcp-sdk-v2-dual-era-http.md)).** The packages are
`@modelcontextprotocol/server`, `client` and `core`, exact-pinned 2.3.1 (`client` is a runtime dependency because the
fake provider CLI shipped in `dist` is an MCP client). `src/mcp/server.ts` and the Git tool shim keep the low-level
`Server`; its `tools/list` and `tools/call` handlers are registered by method name (the v2 signature) and still publish
the Ajv-validated JSON Schemas of [§3](#3-mcp-tool-contracts). Stdio: `startStdioServer` still
connects one `Server` to `StdioServerTransport`, and the connection follows the client's opening handshake as before
(`serveStdio` is not used). HTTP (`src/mcp/http.ts`): every check of [§3.3](#33-network-mcp-transport-m13) runs
unchanged (allowed `Origin`, bearer token, approved client, `mcp` scope, client mapping, POST only, `Mcp-Session-Id`
refused with 400, in-flight caps, `Authorization` and `Cookie` stripped); then a non-JSON media type is refused with
415, and the request is classified with the SDK's `isLegacyRequest` under the same 256 KiB body cap. A 2025-era
request (an `initialize`, or any request without the per-request `_meta` envelope) is served by a fresh
`WebStandardStreamableHTTPServerTransport` with `sessionIdGenerator: undefined` and `enableJsonResponse`; a 2026-07-28
request (envelope plus `MCP-Protocol-Version: 2026-07-28`) by one `createMcpHandler` per listener with
`legacy: "reject"` and `responseMode: "auto"` (one JSON body, since no Switchboard tool emits a related notification
before its result; `"json"` would print an SDK warning at construction). One factory builds the same fresh
`SwitchboardMcpServer` per request for both legs and finds the request's configured MCP client and OAuth client in a
`WeakMap` keyed by the web `Request` the SDK hands back as `ctx.requestInfo`; nothing is passed through the SDK's
`AuthInfo`, so no token or placeholder enters an SDK structure. The `node:http` request is bridged to the SDK's
web-standard `fetch(Request)` entry in this module (`@modelcontextprotocol/node` lags the server package and is not
used). Neither leg issues or accepts a session id. Evidence: `src/mcp/http-eras.test.ts` (a 2025-era client and a
2026-07-28 client both initialize and call a tool through the real token checks; no response carries a session id; a
presented session id is still refused; a non-JSON body is refused), the unchanged T13.9 acceptance in
`src/mcp/mcp.test.ts`, and every unchanged stdio and in-memory suite.

**T13.11 as built (2026-10-19, Delegated; [ADR-0065](../adr/0065-client-id-metadata-documents.md)).** A `client_id`
that is an `https` URL names a Client ID Metadata Document (`src/mcp/oauth-cimd.ts`,
`draft-ietf-oauth-client-id-metadata-document`), accepted while `transport.http.cimd` is `true` (the default;
`false` makes such a `client_id` an unknown client with no fetch; stdio has no authorization server and is
unaffected). The identifier must be its own WHATWG serialisation: `https`, a DNS-name host (an IP literal is refused),
a path other than `/`, no userinfo, query, fragment or dot segments, at most 2048 characters; the server also refuses
its own origin before fetching. The fetch happens at `GET /authorize` only: the server resolves the host itself and
refuses the client when
**any** resolved address is special-use (unspecified, loopback, private, shared address space, link-local,
unique-local, multicast, documentation, benchmarking or reserved; the IPv4-mapped, NAT64 well-known-prefix, 6to4 and
Teredo forms of a blocked IPv4 range are blocked too, and `64:ff9b:1::/48` whole); the TLS connection is pinned to
the checked address with the server name and certificate check set to the host (no second resolution); one `GET`,
with any redirect refused (the draft's §5 says MUST NOT follow; the Proposed "at most one redirect" above is
tightened to none); the response must be `200` with a JSON media type; the whole fetch, resolution included, ends
after 5 s; a body over 64 KiB, declared or received, is refused unparsed; at most 2 fetches run at once; the trust
store is Node's default, with a certificate authority injectable only through the fetcher's constructor (tests). The
document's `client_id` must equal the identifier by simple string comparison, then `checkClientMetadata` applies as
for a registration request. The client is stored in `oauth_client` under `sbc_` + 128 bits of the URL's SHA-256
(`cimdInternalClientId`, so `oauth_token`, `http_subject` and the `transport.http.clients` mapping are unchanged;
`oauth_client_id` there may be the URL, mapped to the derived identifier at load) with the URL in `oauth_cimd_client`
(migration 0028, DDL below). A new URL is `pending` under the registration cap, checked before the fetch; a denied or
revoked client gets no fetch on its behalf; every authorization request fetches afresh and refreshes the stored
metadata (nothing is cached across consents); `POST /token` takes the URL as `client_id` and never fetches. The
consent page shows the URL as the client id, its host on its own line and the name labelled as taken from the
document; a refused document is an error page with no redirect. The authorization-server metadata carries
`client_id_metadata_document_supported`. Audit events `cimd_fetched` and `cimd_refused` carry the derived id, the host
and a reason code, never the document. Evidence: `src/mcp/oauth-cimd.test.ts` ("passes consent with the URL's host
shown and gets an audience-bound token under the normal PKCE flow"; "refuses a URL resolving to a … address before
any connection" for loopback, private, link-local, IPv4-mapped, NAT64, unique-local and IPv6 loopback; "refuses a
redirect, whether it points to a private address or anywhere else"; "refuses an oversize document, declared or
streamed, without parsing it"; "refuses a slow document at the fetch deadline"; "refuses a document whose client_id
is not its URL, and one that is not a public-client document"; "applies the pending-registration cap before fetching,
and refuses a denied client without fetching"; "blocks special-use addresses in every transition form and allows
public ones"), `src/config/config.test.ts` ("maps a Client Identifier URL under its derived id and takes cimd:
false"), `src/ledger/ledger.test.ts` ("0028 adds oauth_cimd_client …"); the DCR suites `src/mcp/oauth-clients.test.ts`
and `src/mcp/oauth-server.test.ts` pass unchanged.

```sql
CREATE TABLE oauth_cimd_client (         -- migration 0028 (T13.11); the URL behind a derived sbc_ client id
  client_id  TEXT PRIMARY KEY CHECK (client_id GLOB 'sbc_*') REFERENCES oauth_client(client_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  url        TEXT NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK (url GLOB 'https://*' AND length(url) <= 2048),
  fetched_at TEXT NOT NULL
) STRICT;
```

### 20.12 Property tests and coverage (NFR-MAINT-06, T8.11)

Built (Delegated, 2026-10-19): a pinned `fast-check` dev dependency (`4.10.2`, exact) and generated-input tests for
each parser NFR-MAINT-06 lists (`src/adapters/bypass-audit.property.test.ts`,
`src/adapters/git-write-detector.property.test.ts`, `src/git/sanitize.property.test.ts`,
`src/artifacts/redact.property.test.ts`, `src/continuity/render.property.test.ts`,
`src/ci/untrusted-block.property.test.ts`, `src/git/secret-scan.property.test.ts`), run by the normal `npm test`
with a fixed seed and `numRuns: 100` per property; coverage through `@vitest/coverage-v8` `5.0.4` (matching `vitest
5.0.4`), reported in CI without a threshold (Delegated, Linus decision WORK QUEUE v10): the Linux job runs the suite
a second time with `--coverage` and uploads `coverage/` as the `coverage-report` artifact through the SHA-pinned
`actions/upload-artifact`.
[TDD §3.6](TDD.md#36-testing-strategy) records the strategy.

### 20.13 Tamper-evident ledger, provider telemetry, evaluation, brief and critic (T8.13, T8.15, T8.14, T5.14, T14.17)

- **Chain (FR-TEL-05).** Implemented (T8.13, 2026-10-19, Delegated; [ADR-0064](../adr/0064-hash-chained-ledger-rows.md)
  owns the format). Migration 26 adds `chain_seq` (gapless position, unique) and `chain_hash` to `run_event` and
  `approval`: two independent chains. `chain_hash` = hex SHA-256 of the previous row's hash followed by the row's
  canonical JSON (the ADR's fixed column list, sorted keys, no whitespace, values as stored); genesis is 64 zeros.
  Every `run_event` row is linked on insert through `insertChainedRunEvent` (`src/ledger/chain.ts`), the only
  production writer; an approval row is linked when it is decided, in the decision's transaction, and a `PENDING` row
  carries no link. The migration backfills existing rows in insertion (`rowid`) order, which is id order except
  within one millisecond, with the update guards dropped and recreated in the same transaction (the runner's
  `backfill` and `postSql` steps; `postSql` is in the checksum). `doctor`'s `ledger_chain` check opens the ledger
  read-only and reports each chain's length and head hash, or `fail` naming the table and the first broken row's id.
  It detects edits made without the chain code; it does not detect a recomputed chain or a truncated tail
  (ADR-0064).
- **Provider OTLP (FR-TEL-06).** Done (2026-10-19, T8.15, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved):
  [ADR-0063](../adr/0063-per-run-provider-otlp-metrics-receiver.md) (new local listener) landed in the same PR, committed
  first. One listener per run on `127.0.0.1:0` for the run's lifetime, metrics only over OTLP/HTTP JSON (no protobuf
  or gRPC, no new dependency), 1 MiB body cap (Assumption), a closed token/cost metric allowlist mapped to
  `provider_reported` usage observations for that run, forbidden attributes dropped, run/task ids injected by the
  controller and never trusted from the payload. `telemetry.provider_otlp` defaults off (off opens no socket); a
  provider gets a listener only with a cited `otlp_metrics` manifest declaration, and none declares one yet. Tests:
  `src/telemetry/provider-otlp.test.ts`, the T8.15 block in `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.test.ts`, and the config and
  manifest declaration tests.
- **Evaluation (FR-OPS-06).** `switchboard eval run <suite>`: a suite file in the data directory, each task replayed as
  a normal run, graded in a network-off container through the ADR-0052 driver with the tests outside the agent's
  worktree; results in a new `eval_result` table.

  *As built (T8.14, 2026-10-19, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved).* `src/eval/` and `src/cli/eval.ts`;
  operator guide [docs/operations/evaluation.md](../operations/evaluation.md).

  - **Command.** `switchboard eval run <suite> --provider <id> [--live-budget <file>] [--json]` and
    `switchboard eval results <suite>`. With `CI` set to anything but empty, `false` or `0`, `eval run` reports
    `EVAL_SKIPPED_IN_CI`, exits 0 and opens nothing. It takes the data-directory lock for the whole suite (refused
    while `serve` runs, as `gc --apply`).
  - **Live budget** (Linus decision, WORK QUEUE v10, Delegated). Every provider but the test fake needs
    `--live-budget <file>`, a JSON object `{"provider": "<id>", "max_runs": <integer > 0>}` naming the same provider;
    anything else is `EVAL_LIVE_BUDGET_REQUIRED`, and a suite with more tasks than `max_runs` is
    `EVAL_LIVE_BUDGET_EXCEEDED`. Both are checked before the lock is taken, so no run is admitted.
  - **Suite file.** `<data_dir>/eval/<suite>.json` (the name matches `^[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9_-]{0,63}$`):

    ```json
    {
      "schema_version": 1,
      "project": "web",
      "trust_profile": "edit-untrusted",
      "grader": { "image_digest": "sha256:<64 hex>", "timeout_seconds": 600, "test_command": ["npm", "test"] },
      "tasks": [
        { "id": "fix-123", "base_ref": "v1.4.0", "objective": "...", "hidden_tests": "bundles/fix-123",
          "test_command": ["node", "/eval/tests/run.mjs"] }
      ]
    }
    ```

    `project` names a configured project; `trust_profile` defaults to `edit-untrusted` and may not be `privileged`
    or exceed the project's ceiling; `hidden_tests` is a directory resolved (through `realpath`) inside
    `<data_dir>/eval/` and not overlapping any project context; a task's `test_command` overrides the grader's. At
    most 200 tasks; unknown keys are refused.
  - **Replay.** Each task is submitted as a new run through `RunLifecycle.submit` (client id `switchboard-eval`,
    priority `batch`) under the project's snapshot with `base_ref` replaced by the task's, without commit identity or
    push (`writable: false`, push `none`), one at a time; the runner waits for the terminal state. The lifecycle is
    unchanged.
  - **Grader.** Only after a `SUCCEEDED` run: the worktree (without `.git`) and the bundle are copied into a
    staging directory under `<data_dir>/eval/grading/`; the container runs through the ADR-0052 port and Docker
    driver: rootless runtime required, the digest resolved among local images (never pulled), two mounts only (the
    staged output writable at `/eval/work`, the staged bundle read-only at `/eval/tests`), no forwarded environment,
    no login volume, `--network none`. The runner refuses an argv without `--network none`
    (`EVAL_GRADER_NETWORK_NOT_OFF`). Before and after grading it asserts the hidden tests are absent from the agent's
    worktree: no bundle file at the same relative path with the same content (`EVAL_TESTS_IN_WORKTREE`; content alone
    would flag a correct answer equal to an expected-output file). The container and the staging copy are removed.
  - **`eval_result`** (migration 0025): `id` (`evl_*`), `suite`, `task_id`, `provider`, `run_id`, `run_state`,
    `pass` (1 exactly when the test command exited 0, Linus decision; a `CHECK` ties it to `test_exit_code = 0`),
    `test_exit_code`, `grade_error` (`EVAL_RUN_<state>`, `EVAL_RUN_NOT_ADMITTED` or a grader code), `wall_clock_ms`
    (submit to terminal), `usage_json` (the run's provider-reported usage metrics, `[]` when none), `created_at`.
    Test output is not stored.
- **Brief (FR-SES-07).** `projects.<id>.brief_file`, a path outside the repository, at most 4 KiB, delivered as a
  labelled block before retrieved context and counted in its budget; the handoff bundle lists its SHA-256.
  *As built (T5.14, 2026-10-19, Delegated, not owner-approved; no migration).* `projects[].brief_file` is a
  configuration-file key only: a dynamic project definition (FR-PRJ-10) cannot carry it, since it would let an MCP
  client make the controller read a host file into a prompt (Delegated, least privilege). `loadConfig` resolves it
  against the configuration file's directory and refuses, with a `$.projects[i].brief_file` error, a file that is
  missing, not a regular file, empty, over 4,096 bytes, not UTF-8, whose canonical path is inside an allowed root or a
  context of the project (so no agent can edit its own brief), or whose block alone needs more than
  `rag.retrieval.max_context_tokens`. Admission (`admitBrief` in `src/mcp/project-tools.ts`, both the MCP and the queue
  path) reads the file again under the same rules, keeps its text in the controller process by hash and freezes
  `brief_sha256` into the run's project snapshot (Linus decision (a), WORK QUEUE v10); a brief that cannot be read
  refuses the run with `TOOL_FAILURE`, `details.reason: "PROJECT_BRIEF_UNAVAILABLE"`, never a silent skip (Linus
  decision (b)). At dispatch the context-pack port `serve` wires (`src/cli/brief-pack.ts`) takes the text by that hash
  (after a restart, from the file only if its hash still matches; else the run fails closed with the same reason
  before spawn), renders `<switchboard-project-brief <nonce>>` with a fixed operator-brief header, the hash, the text and
  a closing line carrying the nonce, and places it after the request text and before the retrieval pack. Its tokens
  (UTF-8 bytes / 4, block plus separator) are reserved from `max_context_tokens` before retrieval runs, so retrieval is
  trimmed first; retrieval is raced against `admission_timeout_ms`, so a slow index drops snippets, never the brief. A
  block that has grown past the budget since load refuses the run the same way (the `rag_retrieval` event reason is
  `PROJECT_BRIEF_OVER_BUDGET`; the run still fails closed with `TOOL_FAILURE`, reason `PROJECT_BRIEF_UNAVAILABLE`). The
  `rag_retrieval` event gains `brief_sha256` and `brief_tokens_est` (outcome `skipped`, reason `INERT`, when retrieval
  is off). A run without a brief compiles byte for byte as before. The brief goes with every run admitted on a
  briefed project, like the pack (§14.6): a handoff successor re-resolves the meta-session's project under the same
  checks as a new request, so it freezes its own hash at its admission and gets the block too, while its bundle lists
  the hash only; a native resume continues a session that already had it (Delegated: the handoff prompt's layout,
  instruction last, is unchanged). The handoff bundle becomes version 3
  (§15.1 "Version 3"). The brief's text never reaches the ledger, an artifact, a log or the bundle. Code:
  `src/projects/brief.ts`, `src/config/load.ts`, `src/cli/brief-pack.ts`, `src/cli/rag-index.ts` (`reservedTokens`),
  one refusal hook in `src/lifecycle/lifecycle.ts` (`ContextPackOutcome.refuse`). Tests: `src/projects/brief.test.ts`,
  `src/cli/brief-pack.test.ts`, `src/continuity/bundle.test.ts` ("project brief as a hash").
- **Critic (FR-CTL-15).** `projects.<id>.critic`: `enabled` (default `false`), `min_diff_lines` (Assumption 400), always
  another provider, `inspect-untrusted`; the critic's objective is controller-generated and its findings artifact is
  untrusted. It counts against the project's budgets (FR-CTL-06).
  *Implemented (T14.17, `src/control/critic.ts`, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved):* `projects[].critic` is a
  configuration-file key only (not a dynamic project definition), `min_diff_lines` an integer 1–1 000 000. Serve
  passes every committed transition to the critic (`RunLifecycleOptions.onTransition`, no lifecycle change). For a
  `SUCCEEDED` run that is write-capable (not `inspect-untrusted`) in an enabled project:
  1. **Diff size** (Linus decision v10 (a)): added plus deleted lines of `git diff --numstat` between the run's `pre`
     and `post` checkpoints; binary files (`-` counts) are excluded. Below `min_diff_lines` nothing is recorded.
  2. **Exactly once** (Linus decision v10 (b)): `critic_requested {diff_lines, min_diff_lines}` is appended to the
     original run in one transaction only when the run has no critic event yet, and the critic is admitted with the
     idempotency key `critic:<original run id>` under the original run's client, so a second notification or a restart
     cannot admit a second critic. A refusal is never retried.
  3. **Admission** goes through the work-queue admission path (`admitWorkItemRequest`, §18.4) with the candidates
     narrowed to every registered provider except the original's; routing picks among the eligible ones. Trust profile
     `inspect-untrusted`, priority `batch`, the project snapshot frozen read-only (`writable: false`, push policy
     `none`, no identity, finalize commit or signing; Linus decision v10 (c)): with no worktree the critic gets no Git
     channel and no finalize commit and never publishes. Budgets (FR-CTL-06), pause and ceilings apply as to any run.
  4. **Objective**: a fixed controller template naming the run, the project and the line count, then the text diff in
     one `[untrusted_diff]` block it cannot close (control characters removed, secrets redacted, block markers
     neutralised, capped at 60 000 characters, Assumption).
  5. **Outcome events** on the original run: `critic_admitted {critic_run_id, provider, diff_lines}`, or
     `critic_skipped {reason}` with `reason` one of `diff_unavailable`, `no_other_provider`, `no_eligible_provider`,
     `admission_refused` (budget, pause or queue full) and `admission_failed` (with `code`). When the critic ends,
     `critic_findings {critic_run_id, provider, state, trust: "untrusted", label, summary?}` with `artifact_id` set to
     the critic's `provider_stdout` artifact, once; the label is fixed by the controller. Nothing else on the original
     run changes: its row, commits, checkpoints, artifacts and publication are untouched.
  6. **Never a critic of a critic**: the critic is `inspect-untrusted`, so it is not write-capable.

### 20.14 Notes carried from the harness report

- **FR-EXE-09 lock order.** No box-wide lock (data-directory lock, GC slot, any test lock) is held while a run waits for
  a verification slot. The run's own write lease stays held; it protects only its worktree.
- **Slot wait does not fail open.** A wait that outlasts the stage's wall clock ends the stage with
  `outcome: "timeout"` (§8). The operator's shim runs the command after 30 minutes instead; Switchboard keeps the
  fail-closed behaviour on purpose.
- *As built (2026-10-19, T8.10, Delegated agent work, not owner-approved):* the lock module lists every lock it hands
  out in this process (`heldLocks()`, `src/lock/held.ts`: the data-directory lock and each verification slot) from
  acquisition to release. It is instrumentation, not a control. The verification stage takes no lock before or while it
  waits for a slot, and holds exactly one slot while its commands run (test "no box-wide lock is held during a slot
  wait" in `src/lifecycle/verification.test.ts`, no sleeps). The `serve` process holds its own data-directory lock
  for its lifetime; that lock is never waited on (a second acquirer fails at once, `acquireDataDirLock`), so it
  cannot form a wait chain with a slot. The fail-closed timeout is the existing test "a wait longer than the
  wall-clock limit ends the run TIMEOUT".
- **Network-off login probe.** When login volumes are mounted in containers
  ([ADR-0013](../adr/0013-projects-environments-and-contexts.md)), FR-AUTH-02's probe runs inside the container with
  networking off. A probe that cannot decide offline reports `Unverified`, never `AUTH_REQUIRED`: Antigravity stayed
  undecided offline with its token file present.
